## Current Stage

**STAGE 37 — Campaign-wide AGC difficulty curve: per-shift eligible types + response speed**

## Current Status

Session 79 pulled the previously-queued F9 (direct reactive-power control, replacing the AVR
voltage setpoint) forward ahead of Shift 10 per developer decision, then completed Movement 2 of

Session 77 merged Session 76's 3-shift tutorial arc back into a single shift (Shift 1 only; Shifts 2-3 are stubs again) after playtesting found the 3-shift split unnecessary and, more importantly, that starting Shift 1 with AGC off was confusing and that governor droop made manual RIVE-1 control feel broken. Headline changes:

- **`DROOP_ENABLED` defaults to `False`** (was `True`) — `constants.py` plus every hardcoded fallback default across `loader.py`, `main.py`, and `shift_io.py` (5 call sites total) flipped to match. Root cause: droop applies to every synchronous unit including slow-ramping COAL, non-ramp-limited, re-anchoring `target_mw` every tick — with AGC already handling fast correction on HYDRO/CCGT, droop's realism wasn't earning its confusion cost. Per-shift `DROOP_ENABLED = True` opt-in plumbing is untouched; any shift can still turn it on.
- **`UnitModel.set_voltage_setpoint()` bug fix**: previously gated on `state == 'ONLINE'`, silently no-op'ing (returning `False`) when called on an OFFLINE unit — broke `INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS` for any unit that starts a shift offline and is brought online later by the player. Now stores the setpoint unconditionally; it has no electrical effect until the unit is actually ONLINE and included in `pv_bus_constraints()` (unchanged). Found while building Shift 1's new "start Holt Hydro mid-shift" lesson — Holt's pre-configured low AVR setpoint was silently being dropped.
- **Shift 1 "First Watch" rewritten** as the entire Phase 2 tutorial arc in one ~8-real-minute (`DURATION_HOURS = 3.2`, `TIME_COMPRESSION = 24`) continuous shift: AGC on and explained from T+0 (not introduced mid-shift); only ASHC-1 is AGC-eligible and online at handover (Holt Hydro starts OFFLINE, deliberately, so ASHC-1 alone cannot cover the forecast peak); an explicit TUTOR beat states the real forecast peak (~515 MW) and that RIVE-1 needs to reach roughly 165 MW to cover it; two independent fixes work (raise RIVE-1 by hand, or start Holt Hydro — a 5-minute cold start) and both were verified via headless trace; margin `WARNING` beats fire on ASHC-1's output and on total spinning reserve as the ramp climbs. Then the voltage/AVR (Fenwick sags once Holt starts, fixed by raising its setpoint) and topology/N-1 (L09 spare circuit / L01 trip) content from Session 76's Shifts 2-3 is folded in as later acts in the same continuous `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` list, retimed against the new dispatch baseline.
- **Shifts 2-3 revert to stubs**, matching the exact pattern Session 71 already established once for this same kind of merge.
- Session 76's shared grid (`tutorial.json`), Ferranti-effect line charging, and `AGC_SET` action are all reused as-is — no changes this session beyond the developer's own FENW/L11 voltage-tier correction (150kV, already applied before this session started).

See Session 77 details below for the full tuning trail. Session 76 (superseded content, mechanics/grid/physics work still current) follows.

- **`TIME_COMPRESSION` 48→24** (global constant, `constants.py`) — every shift in the campaign now runs at half speed (more real-time reaction room), not just the 3 tutorial shifts. `TRIP_DELAY_S`/`BLACKOUT_TRIP_S`'s real-second doc comments updated to match (30s/15s at 1x, was 15s/7.5s).
- **New Ferranti-effect line-charging physics** — `voltage.py`'s decoupled model previously had no per-line reactive injection at all (only a flat per-bus `VSHUNT_REG` numerical regulariser). Added a small per-km, per-voltage-tier MVAr injection at both ends of every in-service line (`simulation.py::_line_charging_mvar_per_km()`, fed into `_build_q_injections()` — a Q-injection like the existing shunt-bank/SVC devices, deliberately **not** a change to `voltage.py`'s B' matrix, since Q injections here are solver inputs and B' diagonal terms are a different, numerical-stability thing). Applies to every shift, ungated (no per-shift flag) — a deliberate scope decision (see below). Gives closing a spare/parallel circuit a real, small, ongoing voltage cost instead of being a costless toggle, motivated by the developer's concern that players would otherwise just close every line permanently. **Constants are calibrated for the new shared tutorial grid's line lengths (2-150 km) and confirmed to produce severe overvoltage (~1.35 pu) on Shift 4's existing 126 km RIVE-CLOV line** — accepted explicitly by the developer since Shift 4/5 are placeholder/test content due for a full rebuild regardless.
- **New `AGC_SET` scripted-event action** (`simulation.py::_execute_action()`, documented in `shift_io.py`) — mutates the runtime `AGC_ENABLED` global mid-shift, mirroring `main.py`'s existing Ctrl+A debug toggle. Lets Shift 1 turn AGC on partway through a single continuous shift.
- **New shared grid** `assets/designer_grids/tutorial.json` (8 buses, 11 lines, 4 units — RIVE-1/RIVE-2/ASHC-1/HOLT-1) used by all 3 new shifts; each shift activates a different subset via `INITIAL_SCHEDULE`/`SUBSTATION_TYPES`/`MAINTENANCE_LINES` rather than separate grid files.
- **Shift 1 "First Watch"** rewritten: Part A (T+0-90, manual-only single-unit dispatch, AGC/droop off) then Part B (AGC switches on via `AGC_SET`, RIVE-1 idling online the whole time comes up by hand as ASHC-1 nears its ceiling and spinning reserve visibly draws down). Demand is GREY+OAKE only (350 MW peak) via a T+0 `DEMAND_OVERRIDE`, since the shared grid's other two load buses (RAVE, FENW) aren't fed by anything this shift.
- **Shift 2 "Local Support"** (was a stub since Session 71's merge) rewritten: voltage/AVR (Fenwick sags via Holt Hydro's low AVR setpoint, fixed by raising it — the Batherton/Stavely pattern from the old Shift 4) and topology/N-1 (a spare Riverside-Ashcombe circuit, L09, must be pre-closed before a scripted L01 trip or the whole Ashcombe branch blacks out).
- **Shift 3 "Full Board"** (was AGC/reserve content, now superseded) rewritten as the integration capstone — no new mechanics, full 4-unit/AGC/voltage/N-1 board active from handover, with a scripted demand jump that stresses reserve and deepens Fenwick's sag simultaneously (first shift requiring split attention), then the same L01/L09 N-1 test as Shift 2.
- **Old Shift 4's voltage/reactive content is superseded by Shift 2's**, not deleted — `shift4.json`/`shift_04.py` untouched, still reachable, but no longer part of the tutorial arc's critical path.

See Session 76 details below for the full design-decision trail, tuning process, and verification.

Session 70 implemented all six sub-parts (1a-1f) of the gameplay analysis's top-priority recommendation (`GAMEPLAY_ANALYSIS.md`, Improvement #1): decoupled frequency dynamics from the `TIME_COMPRESSION` cancellation bug, reinstated universal governor droop, retuned AGC into a working PI controller with a surfaced saturation flag, rescaled the line-overload trip timer with a live canvas countdown, softened the voltage-collapse cliff, and fixed the AGC-offset hour-boundary snap-back. See Session 70 details below. Numeric constants (`FREQ_DYNAMICS_SCALE`, `AGC_KI`/`AGC_KD`/`AGC_INTEGRAL_MAX`, `V_COLLAPSE_GAIN`/`V_COLLAPSE_RECOVERY_PU_S`) are playtest-candidate starting values verified against a new headless harness (`scripts/verify_reaction_window.py`), not final — a real in-game playtest pass is the natural next session.

Session 66 made Phase 1 planning reachable from the campaign for the first time (previously coded but orphaned — `GameState.PLANNING` had no entry point in `main.py`'s menu flow) and built Shift 5, "The Plan," the tutorial that introduces it. Added a per-unit AUTO/MANUAL dispatch mode as a core `UnitModel`/`FleetModel` feature: a unit seeded from a confirmed Phase 1 plan starts AUTO and follows its planned MW at each simulated-hour boundary via a new minimal per-hour executor (`GridSimulation._apply_hourly_schedule()`, `FleetModel.apply_hourly_schedule()`); setting a unit's target directly (the existing digit-key + Enter dispatch) drops it to MANUAL; a new `M` key / context-panel button returns a MANUAL unit to AUTO. AGC's eligibility/behaviour is completely unchanged — it still nudges `target_mw` for any HYDRO/CCGT unit regardless of dispatch mode, via a new `_set_target_internal()` UnitModel method that AGC and the per-hour executor both use instead of the mode-flipping public `set_target()`. Also added a `DEMAND_OVERRIDE` scripted-event action (`DemandModel.set_demand_override()` already existed but was unwired) for Shift 5's mid-shift demand-spike lesson. `main.py`'s `BRIEFING` state now branches to `GameState.PLANNING` instead of `GameState.PLAYING` when the target shift's config declares `USES_PLANNING = True` (new `shift_NN.py` constant, read by `loader.py`); `build_planning_model(shift_number)` was already fully generic (confirmed by reading it — no shift-10 hardcoding), so no changes were needed there. **While verifying Shift 5 end-to-end, found and the developer fixed a pre-existing voltage collapse in `shift5.json`** unrelated to this session's own work (confirmed by testing the plain flat handover dispatch with zero scripted events — FENN then YEWB still collapsed to 0.0 pu by ~t=120min): the developer added a second CLOV↔SUTT circuit (L41, parallel to L18) and moved the shift's wind unit from Marchden (MARC-1, now removed) to Sutterleigh (SUTT-1, 100 MW) directly on the strengthened tie. Re-verified after the fix: unattended with a well-balanced 24h schedule (hydro headroom held back rather than tracking forecast 1:1), the shift plays the full 240 minutes with voltage holding a stable 0.86-0.93 pu band and frequency recovering from each transient — no collapse. A schedule with no headroom (built via the existing `auto_schedule()` heuristic alone, which fills COAL/CCGT before touching hydro on this fleet and leaves ~55 MW system-wide reserve) genuinely does hit frequency/voltage trouble under the demand spike, which is the intended contrast, not a bug. 28/28 automated tests pass; full campaign-flow smoke (`build_planning_model(5)` → hand-tune/`auto_schedule()` → `PlanningScreen` F10 confirm → `on_plan_complete` → `GridSimulation` with `planning_model`) verified headless; Mode row render-checked via offscreen PNG-equivalent surface draw. See Session 66 details below.

Session 65 re-tuned Shift 4 against the now-stable solver (Session 64) and landed a working voltage/reactive tutorial. The developer had already restructured `shift4.json` — Stagshaw (STAG) is gone; the STAV support generator is now **Batherton (BATH-1)**, a 400 kV hydro station reaching Stavely through a Batherton→Nettlecross→Hollowgate chain. Confirmed empirically (headless driver + real loader path) that the original "raise the nearby generator's AVR setpoint to support the sagging bus" lesson **now works** on this topology, where it never could on the old weak-radial STAG arrangement: at BATH-1 `q_max_mvar=300`/`q_min_mvar=-100` dispatched 200 MW with its AVR setpoint starting at 0.95 pu, Stavely sags into a stable WATCH band (~0.87) and raising BATH-1's setpoint toward 1.05 pu flips it from absorbing to injecting and lifts Stavely to HEALTHY (ΔV ≈ +0.11), holding through the evening peak. Tuned STAV `peak_load_mw` 300→240 (kills a bistable AVR self-heal that let STAV recover unattended mid-shift) and FENN's capped shunt bank 25→40 MVAr/step (stops FENN collapsing to 0.0 pu at peak when the SVC is ignored — it now holds a recoverable WATCH). Rewrote `shift_04.py`: `INITIAL_SCHEDULE`/`INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS`/`SHUNT_BANK_OVERRIDES`/`HANDOVER_NOTES` now reference BATH-1 (all stale STAG-1/STAG-2 references removed, including the previously-noted stale HANDOVER_NOTES line), the docstring's narrative/grid-diagram/teaching-goal rewritten for Batherton, and the STAV Act-2 scripted events re-framed away from the (no-longer-occurring) Stagshaw Q-exhaustion beat toward "you never raised Batherton's setpoint — do it now." Verified unattended (both STAV and FENN hold recoverable WATCH the whole 240-min shift, frequency nominal, no collapse; escalating WARNING/CRITICAL alarms fire correctly) and fully-fixed (raise BATH-1 setpoint + FENN SVC → both HEALTHY, only the TUTOR confirmations fire); builds and renders through the real `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer` path; 28/28 tests still pass. FENN's SVC lesson (including its genuine Act-2 "one raise isn't enough" beat) is unchanged. See Session 65 details below. Session 64 simplified the voltage/reactive model so it "looks like it, not is like it" (developer framing), fixing the root cause behind Shift 4's un-tunable voltage collapse (`SHIFT4_VOLTAGE_INVESTIGATION.md`). Two changes: **(A)** the decoupled voltage solver's PV→PQ correction (`voltage.py`) now iterates to a bounded fixed point instead of a single linear pass — the single pass never let a PV bus settle "satisfied" (it re-chased the same voltage error every tick and pinned any active PV bus to its Q ceiling, so `q_reserve` was always ~0) and diverged into a self-inflicted blackout on any bus with two comparable electrical paths (the investigation's §5 CLOV-tie instability). The iteration accumulates Q across passes, keeps PV→PQ sticky within a solve, converges on the per-pass max Q change (`PV_CORRECTION_Q_TOL_MVAR = 0.5`), and is hard-capped (`PV_CORRECTION_MAX_ITERS = 8`) so it can never hang a tick. Verified against the investigation's own headless driver: the previously-unstable STAG→CLOV tie now holds a stable WATCH band (0.87-0.95) the whole shift instead of collapsing to 0.0 pu. **(C)** Retired the automatic transformer-tap machinery entirely — it was dead code (nothing instantiated a `TransformerTap` at runtime; `seed_default_reactive_devices` only ever seeds shunt banks + one SVC), so `transformer_taps` was always empty and its display branches never fired. Removed the class, its plumbing in `simulation.py`/`_build_q_injections`/`_build_state`/`SimulationState`, the `TAP_*` constants, the `COL_TAP` colour, `draw_tap_glyph`, all tap display rows, and the now-unused `VoltageModel.b_diag()` accessor; trimmed tap references from `SIMULATION_API.md`/`DOMAIN_GLOSSARY.md`/`GRID_SIMULATION_MECHANICS.md` and fixed `scripts/render_voltage_verify.py`. Kept unchanged: the decoupled ΔV=B'⁻¹Q solve, collapse-acceleration overlay, auto shunt banks, manual SVC, per-substation-type power factor. `shift4.json`/`shift_04.py` deliberately untouched — Shift 4 re-tuning against the now-stable solver is the immediate follow-up. 28/28 automated tests pass (was 27: removed 1 dead tap test, added 2 PV-convergence regression tests); Shift 1-3 regression byte-identical; full-Renderer PNG render smoke confirms the bus panel shows Shunt+SVC rows and no Tap row. See Session 64 details below. Session 53 fixed a display-only bug where any unit whose online state varied across the day (only possible since Session 50's auto-scheduler) showed as all-dashes across its *entire* table row if it happened to be offline at hour 0, hiding real non-zero scheduled values at later hours (confirmed on CCGT units, which the auto-scheduler correctly comes online for later in the day) — the stacked plot was already correct since it reads per-hour state independently. Also moved the plot/table block down 32px for breathing room under the title. See Session 53 details below. Session 52 corrected a misunderstanding from Session 50's auto-scheduler: the displayed `00:00` column was being hard-locked to max output as a fake "hour 0" boundary seed, when the developer's intent was for the boundary state to represent the previous day's last hour (calculation-only, never displayed), leaving `00:00` itself as a normal, fully computed hour like any other — this also incidentally fixed the "hours 0-4 always flagged out of tolerance" artifact from the old spike. Also added a Backspace/Shift+Backspace shortcut to zero-fill the selected cell/row, mirroring the existing TECH MIN/MAX shortcuts — see Session 52 details below. Session 51 fixed a display bug where solar generation was invisible on Shift 10's planning screen (it was already counted in totals but silently merged into a single "WIND" bucket) and added locked, read-only rows for every WIND/SOLAR unit so their forecasted contribution is now visible per-unit — see Session 51 details below. Session 50 fixed a crash on confirming a Phase 1 plan (F10) and added two planning-screen features: a confirm-time ±10% load-adequacy gate, and an optional auto-scheduler shortcut (Ctrl+A) that fills the full 24h schedule via a heuristic day-ahead unit commitment, respecting ramp rate, minimum up/down time, and cold-start lead time per technology — see Session 50 details below. Session 49 built the game's first Phase 1 (planning-phase) implementation: `src/gameplay/phase1.py` (previously an empty stub) now holds `PlanningModel`, a pure-logic 24-hour hourly generation schedule per dispatchable unit, checked against full-day load and wind forecasts computed directly from Shift 10's Alpha grid (`DemandModel`/`RenewablesModel.forecast_by_hour`). A new screen, `src/display/planning.py` + `planning_panels.py` (`PlanningScreen`, mirroring the Shift Builder's self-contained-screen pattern), lets the player edit MW per unit per hour in a technology-grouped, scrollable table, with TECH MIN/MAX quick-fills (single cell or whole row) and an ONLINE/OFFLINE toggle per unit, plus a stacked-bar generation-vs-load plot and WIND FCST/TOTAL GEN/LOAD FCST/DIFF/REG BAND summary rows. The player always plans a full 24h day; the real-time shift window (06:00-18:00 for Shift 10) is highlighted on the plot/table but not the full planning horizon. **Wired for Shift 10 only, as a debug path**: campaign entry (MAIN MENU → CAMPAIGN → difficulty → Enter) now jumps straight to `GameState.PLANNING` for Shift 10 instead of Shift 1's intro/briefing (the old path is commented out in `main.py`'s `DIFFICULTY_SELECT` handler, not deleted). Confirming the plan (F10) seeds the real-time session's `initial_schedule` from the schedule's 06:00 column and lands in `GameState.PLAYING`, paused. **The full 24h schedule is inert in Phase 2 this session** — stored as `GridSimulation._hourly_schedule` for a future per-hour executor, but only the 06:00 handover column currently affects real-time behaviour; hours after handover are still driven by manual dispatch/AGC exactly as before. Regulation band reuses the existing AGC-eligible type set (`simulation.units.AGC_UNIT_TYPES`, newly made public alongside the existing `_AGC_UNIT_TYPES` alias) and `TECH_MIN_FRAC_*` constants. Fuel grouping/colours (`_FUEL_ORDER`/`_FUEL_LABELS`/`_FUEL_COLOURS` in `panels.py`) were given public aliases (`FUEL_ORDER`/`FUEL_LABELS`/`FUEL_COLOURS`) so the new planning screen reuses them instead of duplicating. Other shifts (1-9) and all existing campaign/Designer/Shift-Builder paths are unaffected — `_make_sim_and_renderer()`'s new `planning_model` parameter defaults to `None`. 15/15 automated tests pass (unchanged — nothing in this session touches simulation physics tests).

### Session 49 details

- **Data model** (`src/gameplay/phase1.py`): `PlanningModel` dataclass holds `schedule`/`online` as `{unit_label: {hour: value}}` over all 24 hours, plus `load_forecast`/`wind_forecast` `{hour: mw}` computed once at construction. Pure methods: `tech_min`/`tech_max` (thermal `min_mw` vs. `rated_mw * TECH_MIN_FRAC_*`), `set_cell`/`fill_row`/`fill_cell_min`/`fill_cell_max`/`fill_row_min`/`fill_row_max`, `toggle_online` (sets all 24 hours for a unit at once), `stacked_by_tech(hour)` (online units summed by `unit_type`, plus a synthetic WIND entry from the forecast), `total_gen`/`difference`/`reg_band` per hour, `to_initial_schedule()` (the `start_hour` column, for the real-time handover), `to_hourly_dispatch()` (full 24h, zeroed where offline, for the inert sim pass-through), `in_shift_window(hour)`. Factory `build_planning_model_for_shift10()` (thin wrapper over shift-number-generic `build_planning_model()`) loads Alpha's grid via the same `load_designer_grid_named`/`DesignerGrid` path `_make_sim_and_renderer` uses, builds a throwaway `DemandModel`/`RenewablesModel` for full-day (`0.0-23.0`) forecasts, and default-seeds the schedule flat across all 24 hours from `shift_10.INITIAL_SCHEDULE`/`MAINTENANCE_UNITS` (units absent from `INITIAL_SCHEDULE`, or on maintenance, start OFFLINE for the whole day — the `[RESET]`/`R` key reapplies this).
- **Layout fix found during visual verification**: the first cut's static row layout (a fixed plot height + every unit row + 5 summary rows, no scrolling) didn't fit vertically for Alpha's 27 dispatchable units across 5 technology groups (32 table rows) — the REG BAND row and part of DIFF rendered below `NATIVE_HEIGHT` and the footer overlapped the table. Fixed by shrinking `PLANNING_PLOT_H` (340→220) and `PLANNING_ROW_H` (18→16), and making the table a **scrollable, clipped viewport** (`PLANNING_TABLE_VISIBLE_H = 480px`, `pygame.Surface.set_clip()`), keeping the selected row centred in view via the same "windowed scroll" idea the Shift Builder's SCHEDULE tab uses. Verified by rendering actual frames to PNG and visually inspecting them (this session's environment can't launch a real fullscreen window, matching the precedent from Session 46) — confirmed all 5 summary rows are visible, the footer no longer overlaps, and scrolling 25 rows down correctly keeps the selected unit in view with a "ROW n/32" indicator.
- **Verified**: (1) headless model-math checks — 24 hours, dispatchable excludes WIND, load forecast peaks ≈8003 MW near 18:00, wind forecast dips midday, `in_shift_window` correct for the 06:00-18:00 boundary (18:00 itself is outside), `reg_band` only sums online HYDRO/CCGT and drops correctly when such a unit is toggled offline, `to_initial_schedule()` matches `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` for online units. (2) Quick-fill methods (`fill_row_max`/`fill_cell_min`/`toggle_online`) checked directly against the model. (3) `PlanningScreen` instantiated and ticked headless (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`) with synthetic keyboard/mouse events driving a full cell-edit round-trip (Enter → type → Enter), a Shift+`]` row quick-fill, an online toggle, a hit-rect click-to-edit, and the F10 confirm callback; ESC correctly returns `False`. (4) `_make_sim_and_renderer(shift=10, planning_model=...)` builds a real `GridSimulation` whose fleet's initial dispatch exactly matches the plan's 06:00 column (checked per-unit, zero mismatches) and whose `_hourly_schedule` is populated; ran 50 real `sim.tick()` steps with no exception. (5) Regression: `shift=10` with no `planning_model` and `shift=5` (non-Designer-grid path) both still build unchanged. (6) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass.
- **Not done this session (deferred, matches the approved plan)**: the per-hour dispatch executor that would make hours after 06:00 actually drive Phase 2 (currently only the 06:00 seed matters); wiring Phase 1 into any shift other than 10; a merit-order auto-fill button; a real (non-headless) visual playtest of the planner in a live fullscreen window.
- Edited: `src/gameplay/phase1.py` (filled from empty stub), `src/main.py` (`GameState.PLANNING`, `DIFFICULTY_SELECT` debug redirect to Shift 10, new PLANNING state branch + `on_plan_complete` callback, `_make_sim_and_renderer` gained `planning_model` param), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (`GridSimulation.__init__` gained inert `hourly_schedule`/`_hourly_schedule`), `src/simulation/constants.py` (new `PLANNING_*` block), `src/display/palette.py` (new `COL_PLAN_*` colours), `src/display/panels.py` (public `FUEL_ORDER`/`FUEL_LABELS`/`FUEL_COLOURS` aliases), `src/simulation/units.py` (public `AGC_UNIT_TYPES` alias for the existing `_AGC_UNIT_TYPES`).

## Session Log

### Session 80 (Campaign-wide AGC difficulty curve — per-shift eligible types + response speed, Shift 10 Act 3 rewritten)

- **Context**: developer feedback on Shift 10's Act 3 ("AGC fails") — per `FUN_FACTOR_BRAINSTORM.md`'s
  Engine 2 argument that AGC quietly removes the game's core difficulty by doing the player's
  frequency-control job for them, a single scripted on/off fault on the campaign's hardest shift
  reads as a smaller idea than it should: AGC had been full-strength for the whole rest of the
  night, and the player's first-ever encounter with hand-flying frequency (since no earlier shift
  has ever turned it off) was happening during the storm's worst stretch. Developer's proposal,
  refined in conversation: make AGC's capability a campaign-wide curve, not a per-shift binary —
  fast, broad AGC on early/tutorial shifts (HYDRO + HYDRO_PUMP + CCGT), narrowing to slow,
  HYDRO-only AGC by Shift 10, so the player is doing real manual correction increasingly through
  the whole campaign rather than being handed a full safety net until one scripted crisis. Act 3
  becomes a further partial degradation of an already-thin system (specific hydro units dropping
  off AGC) rather than a full fault-and-recovery event.
- **Two developer decisions confirmed before implementation**: (1) widen eligibility to
  `HYDRO + HYDRO_PUMP` but **not** `HYDRO_ROR` — RoR's exclusion is physical (`units.py`'s own
  comment: "run-of-river has no stored head to draw on," it cannot ramp on command regardless of
  automation level), unlike `HYDRO_PUMP`'s "excluded by design" (a policy choice, reopened here).
  (2) Act 3's full `AGC_SET` on/off fault is replaced by naming ~40-50% of the eligible hydro
  fleet to exclude mid-shift, via the same eligibility mechanism used a second time — not a new
  concept.
- **Found and fixed a real bug before it could silently break the feature**: `AGC_ENABLED`/
  `DROOP_ENABLED`/`FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT`'s existing per-shift-override pattern (declared in
  `constants.py`, written onto the shared `simulation.constants` module from `main.py`'s three
  build sites, read live via `_sim_const.X` at point of use) does **not** transfer cleanly to the
  six AGC PID/rate constants (`AGC_KP/KI/KD`, `AGC_MAX_RATE_MW_S`, `AGC_DEADBAND_HZ`,
  `AGC_INTEGRAL_MAX`) or to `units.py`'s old `AGC_UNIT_TYPES` — both were **imported by value**
  at module load (`from simulation.constants import AGC_KP, ...` in `simulation.py`; a true
  module-local frozenset in `units.py`), so writing `_const.X = Y` from `main.py` would have had
  zero effect on `_apply_agc()`/`apply_agc_signal()`, which read the names bound once at import
  time. Confirmed via a dedicated Explore agent pass over the codebase before writing any code,
  not discovered by trial and error.
- **AGC eligibility made per-shift-live**: `AGC_UNIT_TYPES`/`_AGC_UNIT_TYPES` (`units.py`) removed
  entirely; the default now lives in `constants.py` as `AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES: frozenset[str] =
  frozenset({'HYDRO', 'CCGT'})` (unchanged value, just relocated per Rule 1). `FleetModel.
  apply_agc_signal()` and `agc_regulation_state()` (the dispatch logic and the Power Balance
  panel's Regulation Availability indicator, respectively) both switched from the old bare-name
  read to `_sim_const.AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES`, live every call — same pattern `_freq_bounds()`
  already used for `FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT`. `loader.py`/`main.py` (all three build sites:
  `_make_sim_and_renderer` — the real campaign path, `_make_designer_test`, `_make_shift_test`)
  gained the matching `agc_eligible_types`/`_const.AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES = cfg.get(...)` plumbing.
  **One subtlety caught during implementation**: the fallback default must be a stable, separately-
  imported constant (`_AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES_DEFAULT`), not `_const.AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES` itself —
  the latter is mutable and could still hold a *previous* shift's override if the new shift
  declares nothing, silently leaking state across shift builds (the same class of bug `AGC_SET`'s
  own docstring already warns about for `AGC_ENABLED`).
- **AGC response speed made per-shift-live**: `_apply_agc()` (`simulation.py`) rewritten to read
  all six PID/rate constants via `_sim_const.X` instead of bare imported names (the six-constant
  import block removed from the top of the file). New `AGC_SPEED_MULT: float = 1.0` constant
  scales `AGC_MAX_RATE_MW_S` and `AGC_KI` **together** at point of use — deliberately not just
  one of the two: scaling only the integral gain would still let AGC eventually snap to full
  correction, just slower to start; scaling only the rate cap risks the controller "wanting" to
  move faster than it's allowed, a bounded-oscillation risk. `AGC_KP`/`AGC_KD` stay unscaled
  (response shape, not overall speed). `_write_agc_log()`'s CSV output (a third, previously-missed
  consumer of the bare-name constants, caught only when a headless verification script crashed
  with `NameError: name 'AGC_KP' is not defined`) now logs the *effective* post-multiplier gains,
  not the raw constants — more useful for tuning, and the actual bug that proved the frozen-import
  problem was real, not theoretical.
- **Mid-shift partial exclusion**: new `AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS` scripted action (`{'type':
  'AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS', 'units': [...]}`, documented in `shift_io.py` alongside the other action
  types) sets `GridSimulation`/`FleetModel`'s new `_agc_excluded_units: frozenset[str]` instance
  field (deliberately **not** `_sim_const` — this is scoped to one shift run and must reset
  cleanly between runs, unlike the shift-wide constants above) via a new `set_agc_excluded_units()`
  method on both classes. `apply_agc_signal()`/`agc_regulation_state()` both check it as an
  additional filter alongside the type check. An empty `'units'` list restores full eligibility
  (verified working, though Shift 10 doesn't currently use the restore path — see below).
- **Phase 1 planning updated to match**: `PlanningModel` (`phase1.py`) gained an
  `agc_eligible_types` field, populated from `load_shift_config()`'s new `agc_eligible_types` key
  at `build_planning_model()` construction time (a snapshot at plan-build time, not a live
  per-tick read — Phase 1 planning happens once, before the real-time shift, and was never meant
  to reflect mid-shift `AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS` changes anyway). `reg_band()` now reads
  `self.agc_eligible_types` instead of the old module-level `AGC_UNIT_TYPES` import. Verified
  directly: `build_planning_model_for_shift10()` correctly reports `frozenset({'HYDRO'})` and a
  correspondingly smaller regulation band than before.
- **Shift 10's baseline set and headlessly tuned**: `AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES = frozenset({'HYDRO'})`,
  `AGC_SPEED_MULT = 0.35`, both declared for the shift's entire 9-hour duration (not just during
  Act 3) — both `ASHG-1`/`ASHG-2` (800 MW of CCGT) are never AGC-eligible this shift at all, so
  the player is hand-flying them continuously from handover, not just during a bounded crisis
  window. Verified via headless trace with a scripted responsive player (closes the Act 1 spare
  circuit, manages hydro unit commitment through the night, and now also nudges `ASHG-1`'s target
  by hand roughly every 4 sim-minutes reacting to frequency) that the full 9-hour shift still
  completes cleanly (`is_shift_failed() == False`, `evaluate_win_conditions()` satisfied) under
  the new baseline, both with and without the additional `AGC_SPEED_MULT = 0.35` slowdown layered
  on. A do-nothing player now fails much earlier than before — hour 22.4 (T+144.6min, an
  over-frequency `FAIL_CONDITION`, well before Act 2 even completes) rather than the old
  mechanism's ~hour 25.8 — because CCGT no longer auto-tracks the falling overnight demand at all,
  a materially harder and more honest difficulty signal than a fault confined to one act.
- **Act 3 rewritten**: the two `AGC_SET` actions (full off at T+300, full on at T+415) replaced
  with a single `AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS` action at T+300 naming `MERE-2`/`CLUN-1` (≈53% of the eligible
  hydro fleet's rated capacity — chosen against the developer's stated "40-50%" target, verified
  all four hydro units are still online and near their technical minimums at T+300 in an
  unscripted trace before picking which two to name). The exclusion is **permanent** for the rest
  of the shift (no restore action) — "nothing fixes itself on the hardest night," matching the
  new baseline's framing that this isn't a fault being repaired. Condition-gated WARNING/CRITICAL
  beats in between (frequency high/still-high/sagging) kept their triggers and rewrote their
  copy away from "AGC off"/"AGC back on" language toward "regulation thinner"/"this is on you"
  framing. Module docstring's Narrative (all four acts), Teaching goal, and `HANDOVER_NOTES`
  rewritten to match — the player is now told at handover that AGC is hydro-only and slow, not
  led to believe it's fully on until Act 3's twist. `_AGC_STILL_ON` condition (used by an Act 1
  tutor beat, actually checks `ASHG-2`'s online state, not AGC's flag) renamed
  `_ASHG2_STILL_ONLINE` — always slightly mis-named, now doubly so with CCGT never AGC-tracked.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 29 passed (pytest's default count, unchanged from
  before this session — pytest treats a print-based test's `return False` as a pass, so this
  number alone doesn't move regardless). Each test file's own direct print-based harness (this
  project's real source of truth) run separately: `test_designer_analysis.py` 6/6,
  `test_simulation.py` 9/10, `test_voltage_reactive.py` 12/13 — identical to the pre-session
  baseline, confirming no regressions (the 2 failures are the same pre-existing,
  session-79-confirmed-unrelated ones: `test_grid_loads`'s demand-profile check and
  `test_warning_to_critical_alarms_and_crisis`). Direct headless checks (not part of the
  automated suite, run ad hoc): `AGC_SPEED_MULT` demonstrably changes correction speed (built a
  synthetic fast-ramping-unit fixture after an initial attempt using the existing
  `test_voltage_reactive._build_sim()` fixture showed no measurable effect — traced to that
  fixture's unit ramp rate, not AGC's own rate cap, being the actual bottleneck, so the test
  fixture itself needed fixing before the mechanism could be verified); `AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS`
  demonstrably removes a named unit from both dispatch and the regulation-state indicator, and
  the empty-list restore path demonstrably un-excludes it. All 10 shifts (1-10) build, tick 200+
  times, and render one frame with no exception through the real
  `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer()` path. Full do-nothing and fully-responsive headless traces
  of the rewritten Shift 10 confirmed above.
- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (matches this project's established
  headless/synthetic-surface verification pattern) — whether the new Act 3 (a further narrowing
  of an already-thin AGC, rather than a full fault) reads as more coherent than the old on/off
  flip, and whether hand-flying CCGT continuously from handover (not just during one crisis
  window) feels like earned difficulty or just relentless micromanagement, are both open
  questions for a real playtest — this was already the standing open item from Session 79 and
  remains so. Early-shift `AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES`/`AGC_SPEED_MULT` values (the "fast, broad" end of
  the curve) are not declared anywhere yet — Shifts 1/4/5 are still stubs (queued from Session 79)
  and Shifts 6-9 are untouched; the mechanism supports a smooth per-shift curve whenever they're
  authored, but no numeric values exist for them yet. `online_unit_types()` (system inertia
  weighting) and `spinning_reserve_mw()` deliberately left reading a unit's actual state without
  any AGC-eligibility filter change — inertia/reserve are physical properties independent of
  whether AGC can currently reach a unit, unaffected by this session's changes.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES` new — relocated from `units.py`,
  `AGC_SPEED_MULT` new), `src/simulation/units.py` (removed `AGC_UNIT_TYPES`/`_AGC_UNIT_TYPES`,
  `apply_agc_signal()`/`agc_regulation_state()` read `_sim_const.AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES` live +
  `_agc_excluded_units` filter, new `set_agc_excluded_units()`), `src/simulation/simulation.py`
  (`_apply_agc()` reads all six PID/rate constants live + `AGC_SPEED_MULT` scaling,
  `_write_agc_log()` signature takes effective gains as params, new `AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS` action
  in `_execute_action()`, new `set_agc_excluded_units()` delegation method, `_reset_log_files()`'s
  log-header builder switched off the old frozen import), `src/gameplay/shifts/loader.py`
  (`agc_eligible_types`/`agc_speed_mult` schema entries), `src/main.py` (three build sites gained
  the matching `_const.AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES`/`_const.AGC_SPEED_MULT` overrides, new
  `_AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES_DEFAULT` stable import), `src/gameplay/phase1.py` (`PlanningModel` gained
  `agc_eligible_types` field, `reg_band()` reads it instead of the old module import),
  `src/data/shift_io.py` (`AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS` documented in the action-schema docstring),
  `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_10.py` (baseline `AGC_ELIGIBLE_TYPES`/`AGC_SPEED_MULT` declared,
  Act 3 rewritten around `AGC_EXCLUDE_UNITS`, docstring/`HANDOVER_NOTES` updated throughout,
  `_AGC_STILL_ON` renamed `_ASHG2_STILL_ONLINE`).

### Session 79 (F9: direct reactive-power control, replacing AVR — then Shift 10 "The Bad Night" Movement 2: grid + shift authored)

- **Context**: this session's original task was Movement 2 of `SHIFT10_BAD_NIGHT_PLAN.md` —
  rebuild `shift10.json` and author `shift_10.py` against the Movement 1 foundation (F1-F8)
  landed in Session 78. Mid-planning, the developer decided to pull F9 (direct-Q control,
  previously queued for *after* Shift 10 specifically so Shift 10 could be tuned against a
  stable AVR baseline — see STAGE_STATUS.md's prior "Queued immediately after Shift 10" note)
  forward instead: Shift 10 should be authored against direct-Q from the start, not AVR.
  Explicit instruction: don't re-tune Shifts 1/4/5's existing AVR content for the new
  mechanic — revert them to docstring-only stubs (matching the existing `shift_06.py`-
  `shift_09.py` pattern) and re-author them against direct-Q in a future session.
- **F9 — AVR removed, direct reactive-power dispatch wired end-to-end.** Two hooks
  (`UnitModel.set_q_target()`, `FleetModel.q_injections()`) already existed with zero
  callers; the ONLINE gate on `set_q_target()` was removed (mirroring Session 77's
  `set_voltage_setpoint()` fix — a shift can now pre-configure `INITIAL_Q_MVAR` on a unit
  that starts OFFLINE). `FleetModel.pv_bus_constraints()` was deleted outright (not
  stubbed) along with `UnitModel.set_voltage_setpoint()`/`v_setpoint_pu`,
  `FleetModel.set_unit_voltage_setpoint()`, and `GridSimulation.set_generator_voltage_setpoint()`
  — every bus is now solved PQ (`voltage.py`'s solver needed no changes; `pv_buses` was
  already optional). `simulation.py::_build_q_injections()` now sums `self._fleet.q_injections()`
  into the same accumulation as reactive devices/line-charging, and returns a plain dict
  instead of a `(dict, pv_buses)` tuple — both call sites (`_build_state()`'s tick path and
  the t=0 init snapshot) updated. A now-obsolete PV→PQ back-attribution block in
  `_build_state()` (previously re-split the solver's post-correction Q evenly across
  co-located units — meaningless once nothing is solved as PV) was deleted outright.
  `SimulationState.unit_bus_types`/`unit_v_setpoint_pu` renamed to `unit_q_target_mvar`
  (drops `bus_types` entirely — no more PV/PQ distinction to show). `INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS`
  renamed `INITIAL_Q_MVAR` in `loader.py`'s schema and `main.py`'s handover-apply loop
  (now calls `sim.set_unit_q_target()`). New `constants.py` block: `GEN_Q_SETPOINT_STEP_MVAR=5.0`/
  `GEN_Q_SETPOINT_STEP_FAST_MULT=5.0` (Ctrl+ = 25 MVAr) replace the old
  `GEN_VOLTAGE_SETPOINT_*` pu constants — per-unit clamping to `[q_min_mvar, q_max_mvar]`
  now, not a single global pu band, since every unit's Q range differs.
  - **Renderer/UI**: the whole `on_setpoint_*` family in `renderer.py` retargeted from AVR
    pu to MVAr — `on_setpoint_enter()` now parses an integer MVAr (was `float` pu) and
    calls `sim.set_unit_q_target()`; `on_setpoint_adjust()` nudges by `GEN_Q_SETPOINT_STEP_MVAR`
    clamped to the *selected unit's own* `q_min_mvar`/`q_max_mvar`. **New `on_setpoint_minus()`**
    toggles a leading `-` on the input buffer (bound to a new `K_MINUS` handler in `main.py`,
    gated on `setpoint_active` like the digit keys) — MVAr targets are routinely negative
    (absorbing), unlike the old AVR buffer which never needed a sign; the digit set also
    dropped `K_PERIOD` (MVAr entry is a whole number, no decimal). `context.py`'s
    `draw_unit_context()` AVR block (setpoint row + separate "Voltage ctrl: PV/PQ" row,
    5 rows total) became a 4-row "Q Target [Q]: nnn MVAr" block — the PV/PQ row is gone
    entirely (nothing to show), `n_rows`/`cmd_row` offsets adjusted accordingly. Parameter
    renamed `v_setpoint_pu`→`q_target_mvar`, `bus_type` parameter dropped.
  - **Shifts 1/4/5 reverted to docstring-only stubs** (9-14 line files, matching
    `shift_06.py`-`shift_09.py`'s exact placeholder pattern), each noting why (AVR content
    no longer applies) and that they're queued for direct-Q re-authoring.
  - **4 of 13 `test_voltage_reactive.py` tests rewritten** for direct-Q (not deleted — the
    other 9 including 2 that exercise `VoltageModel.solve(pv_buses=...)` directly were
    correctly left untouched, since that low-level solver primitive still supports
    `pv_buses` as an optional parameter; only `GridSimulation`/`FleetModel` no longer
    populate it). `test_generator_q_target_feeds_injections` (was
    `test_generator_setpoint_feeds_pv_constraints`) and
    `test_generator_q_target_raises_region_and_clamps` (was
    `test_generator_setpoint_raises_region_and_converts_pq`, dropped its now-meaningless
    PV→PQ-conversion assertion, kept the monotonic-rise/clamp/reserve assertions against
    the new mechanism).
  - **Found and fixed a second, pre-existing engine bug while verifying F9's responsive-player
    path**: `FleetModel.total_generation_mw()`/`p_injections()`/`q_injections()`
    (`units.py`) filtered strictly to `state == 'ONLINE'` — the instant `stop_unit()`
    transitions a unit to `SHUTDOWN`, its still-ramping-down `current_mw` (correctly
    computed by `_tick_shutdown()` for *display*) was excluded from the actual power
    balance and load-flow injection, creating an artificial one-tick generation cliff on
    every unit stop in the entire game, not just Shift 10. Confirmed via headless trace:
    stopping a 380 MW CCGT produced an instant ~300 MW deficit and a sub-minute frequency
    crash before the fix; after widening the three aggregation functions to
    `state in ('ONLINE', 'SHUTDOWN')`, the same stop ramps smoothly over ~9 real minutes
    (CCGT's 8%/min) with frequency holding within ±0.02 Hz throughout. `spinning_reserve_mw()`/
    `online_unit_types()` deliberately left excluding SHUTDOWN (reserve headroom and
    inertia-weighting judgment calls, out of scope for this fix). 28/28 (pre-existing
    baseline; see below) unaffected.
- **Movement 2 — grid rebuilt.** `src/assets/designer_grids/shift10.json`: 28 buses / 30
  lines / 11 units, ~1795 MW peak load, hand-authored via a throwaway generator script
  (buses/lines/units built programmatically then dumped to JSON — not through the
  interactive Grid Designer, matching Session 76's `tutorial.json` precedent). Fleet:
  HART-1 (nuclear, 700 MW, min 300 — "the wall"), BRCK-1/2 (coal, 300 MW each, start
  OFFLINE all shift — cold start exceeds the shift length), ASHG-1/2 (CCGT, 400 MW each),
  MERE-1/2 (hydro, 200 MW each), CLUN-1/2 (hydro, 100/65 MW), GALE-1 (wind, 200 MW — sized
  down from an initial 350 MW, see tuning note below), SAND-1 (solar, 250 MW — dormant,
  night storm). Topology: MDFD (slack) backbone to HART/BRCK/ASHG/MERE, a spare parallel
  circuit MDFD↔TARN (L07/L08, L08 starts open), TARN→HOWE→{CLUN→WYLD (single-radial dread
  bus), GALE→SAND→PORT (storm corridor, PORT single-fed)}. Two real bugs found and fixed
  during headless verification (both would otherwise have made Act 1 look like an
  immediate crisis instead of "quiet"): `L19` (MERE→ODEN) rated 175 MW was undersized for
  MERE's 400 MW of attached hydro and overloaded to 102% at handover before any player
  action — raised to 300 MW; `test_shift10_n1_cross_check()`
  (`tests/test_designer_analysis.py`) **fully rewritten**, since the old version tested
  the unrelated, now fully superseded legacy `topology.py`/`fleet.py` `Grid(10)` tier (41
  buses/62 lines/47 units) via a coincidental `load_shift_config(10)` read path, not the
  Designer grid `shift_10.py` actually points at — new version sweeps N-1 on the real
  `shift10.json`, confirms WYLD's single-radial exposure (only via its direct feed L12;
  the one-hop-further-back L11 is *not* exposed, because CLUN carries its own hydro and
  islands successfully) and the L07/L08 spare-circuit redundancy.
- **Movement 2 — shift authored.** `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_10.py` (~350 lines):
  `DIFFICULTY_LABEL='Severe'`, `START_HOUR=20.0`, `DURATION_HOURS=9.0` (20:00-05:00
  overnight — developer decision: prioritise the "one bad night" narrative fitting inside
  a single overnight window over hitting the original ~15-20 real-minute target exactly;
  it now runs ~22.5 real min at 1x / ~7.5 min at 3x). Four acts, all authored with the
  existing action/condition vocabulary (zero new engine code beyond F9):
  - **Act 1 (Dread)**: `MAINTENANCE_LINES={'L08'}` (the spare circuit bet); WYLD named as
    at-risk in `HANDOVER_NOTES` and never actually threatened (the deliberate non-firing
    risk). A second, non-storm pressure runs the whole act: every dispatchable unit's
    technical minimum plus GALE-1's non-curtailable wind exceeds the ~565-650 MW overnight
    demand trough, so a do-nothing player overshoots generation and pins frequency high
    near the bottom of the night — the fix is ordinary unit commitment (stop hydro/CCGT
    units fully offline in turn, not just down), which took substantial headless tuning to
    get right (see below).
  - **Act 2 (Tempo)**: two staggered `UNIT_DERATE` actions on GALE-1 (surge then collapse)
    plus a `DEMAND_OVERRIDE` schedule holding demand up against its natural decline as the
    cold front passes.
  - **Act 3 (Agency, the spine)**: `AGC_SET(enabled=False)` mid-storm as an in-fiction
    CRITICAL "control room" message, restored ~115 min later. Headless-traced do-nothing
    drift: ~30 real minutes from AGC-off to the 55 Hz hard clamp at 1x, confirmed as a
    fair, legible window.
  - **Act 4**: scripted `LINE_OPEN` on L15 (SAND→PORT) — PORT has no second feed, so this
    is pure triage (shed load / accept the loss), not an N-1 puzzle; L08's Act-1 state
    determines how exposed the rest of the corridor is.
  - `WIN_CONDITIONS`: frequency within [49.0, 51.0] Hz at shift end.
    `FAIL_CONDITIONS` (developer decision — both failure axes, any one triggers): sustained
    <47 Hz *or* >53 Hz for 10s (symmetric — covers both Act 1's oversupply risk and Act 3's
    AGC-off risk, which are the same failure mode in opposite directions), or PORT voltage
    <0.5 pu for 20s (Act 4's cascade).
- **Extensive headless dispatch tuning for Act 1's overnight floor problem** — this was
  the session's largest single time sink. Initial dispatch attempts (both CCGT + hydro
  near ceiling, nuclear at various floors) all left generation pinned ~150-750 MW above
  the trough with every AGC-eligible unit already at technical minimum — traced to GALE-1
  wind (~110-220 MW depending on rated capacity, non-curtailable, not AGC-eligible) being
  the component nothing could shed. Fixed via three changes together, each verified
  insufficient alone: GALE-1 rated 350→200 MW (developer decision, in place of a
  load-shed-based fix or shortening the shift — see below), HART-1 `min_mw` 400→300,
  and — the actual unlock — confirming `stop_unit()` (fully offline, not just down to
  technical minimum) is a real, distinct lever once the SHUTDOWN-state generation-cliff
  bug above was fixed. A fully responsive player stopping ASHG-2/CLUN-2/MERE-2/CLUN-1 in
  turn from T+100min through T+360min holds the entire night including the deepest trough
  (confirmed to `is_shift_failed() == False` for the full 9-hour headless run); a
  do-nothing player fails at T+312-350min (hour ~25.2-25.8, mid-Act-3) via the
  over-frequency `FAIL_CONDITION`, matching the intended difficulty signal. **Also found
  and fixed**: `DEMAND_OVERRIDE`'s schedule keys must use the shift's own continuously-
  incrementing hour axis (`GridSimulation.tick()`'s `sim_hour = start_hour + elapsed`,
  never wrapped to 0-24) — an early draft used `1.0-5.0` for 01:00-05:00 and silently held
  demand flat at the schedule's last chronologically-sorted key (`24.0`) for the entire
  back half of the shift (`_interpolate_override()` sorts keys numerically, so `1.0 < 24.0`
  put the "1am" entry *before* midnight in interpolation order) — corrected to `25.0-29.0`.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 29 passed (unchanged pass count from before
  this session; pytest's default `PytestReturnNotNoneWarning` behaviour treats every
  print-based test's `return False` as a pass, which is why the count doesn't move —
  see below). Each test file's own direct print-based harness (the actual source of
  truth for this project's test convention) run separately and diffed against a
  `git stash`ed pre-session baseline to isolate real regressions from pre-existing
  failures: `test_designer_analysis.py` 6/6, `test_voltage_reactive.py` 12/13 (1
  pre-existing failure, `test_warning_to_critical_alarms_and_crisis`, confirmed identical
  on the unmodified baseline — unrelated to this session), `test_simulation.py` 9/10 (1
  pre-existing failure, `test_grid_loads`'s demand-profile check, also confirmed
  pre-existing). **Two real regressions found and fixed during this diff-against-baseline
  process** (both in test code, not product code): `test_blackout_zones_exclude_reactive_load`
  called `sim._build_q_injections()` expecting the old `(dict, pv_buses)` tuple return;
  `test_demand_model`/`test_simulation_model` both built their fixtures from
  `load_shift_config(1)`, which returned real demand data before this session (Shift 1 had
  content) and empty data after (Shift 1 is now a stub) — decoupled both from any real
  shift file, matching the self-contained-fixture pattern every other well-isolated test
  in this file already uses. All 10 shifts (1-10) build, tick 200+ times, and render one
  frame with no exception through the real `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer()` path.

## Session Log

### Session 77 (Merge tutorial arc back to one shift, AGC-first, droop off by default, set_voltage_setpoint OFFLINE fix)

- **Context**: playtesting Session 76's 3-shift arc surfaced three issues. (1) The developer was comfortable collapsing all three shifts into one continuous shift up to ~8 real minutes — the 3-way split wasn't earning its complexity. (2) Starting Shift 1 with AGC off and switching it on mid-shift (Session 76's Part A/Part B structure) read as a confusing mode switch rather than a useful lesson beat; the developer wanted AGC on and explained from T+0, with the forcing function being an explicit stated target ("raise RIVE-1 toward X MW to cover the forecast peak") plus ongoing margin warnings, not a before/after contrast. (3) The developer reported RIVE-1's output "was being controlled by AGC" during manual adjustment — investigated via an Explore agent (full trace: `main.py` key handling → `renderer.py`'s G-key/digit-key paths → `simulation.py::set_unit_target()` → `FleetModel.set_unit_target()` → `UnitModel.set_target()`/`_set_target_internal()`) which found **no unit-type gate anywhere** in the manual-control path — `AGC_UNIT_TYPES = {'HYDRO', 'CCGT'}` already correctly excludes COAL from AGC itself. The actual cause, found independently and confirmed against the developer's description of the symptom: governor droop (`FleetModel.apply_droop_response()`, a *different* mechanism from AGC, enabled by the separate `DROOP_ENABLED` flag) applies to every synchronous unit including COAL, every tick, non-ramp-limited, and re-anchors `target_mw` each time — even a ~0.05 Hz frequency wobble swings RIVE-1 by several MW instantly, fighting a player's manual setpoint hard enough to read as "something else is driving it." Developer's call: since AGC already handles fast correction, drop droop's *default* off rather than removing the mechanism (narrowed down from an initial "remove droop entirely" ask, once the blast radius of a full removal — engine code, ~9-point Shift Builder round-trip, debug overlay — was laid out).
- **`DROOP_ENABLED` default flip, all 5 hardcoded fallback sites**: `constants.py`'s `DROOP_ENABLED: bool = True` → `False` (with an updated comment explaining the playtest finding). This alone does **not** change runtime behaviour, because every consumer reads its own hardcoded literal default rather than importing `constants.py`'s value (confirmed this is the established pattern in `loader.py` — every other per-shift config key does the same, by design, to keep shift-config defaults independent of the simulation-constants module). Found and fixed all of them: `gameplay/shifts/loader.py:118` (`getattr(mod, 'DROOP_ENABLED', True)` → `False`, plus its docstring), `main.py:409` (`cfg.get('droop_enabled', True)` → `False`, in `_make_shift_test()` for Shift-Builder-authored JSON shifts), `data/shift_io.py` at 3 sites (`ShiftDefinition.droop_enabled` dataclass default, `load_shift_named()`'s `data.get(..., True)`, `load_campaign_shift_for_editing()`'s `cfg.get(..., True)`). **Deliberately left unchanged**: `main.py:353`, `_make_designer_test()`'s unconditional `_const.DROOP_ENABLED = True` — a different tool (Grid Designer "test this grid," not a campaign shift), out of scope for this playtest-driven change. Verified via `load_shift_config(6)` (a shift stub declaring nothing) returning `droop_enabled: False`.
- **`UnitModel.set_voltage_setpoint()` bug found and fixed** (`units.py`): previously `if self._state != 'ONLINE': return False` — any `INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS` entry for a unit that starts a shift OFFLINE silently failed with no error, discovered while building Shift 1's new "Holt Hydro starts offline, pre-configured with a low AVR setpoint, player starts it later" beat (confirmed directly: `sim.set_generator_voltage_setpoint('HOLT-1', 0.95)` returned `False` and `HOLT-1._v_setpoint_pu` stayed at the campaign default 1.02 the whole time). Fixed by removing the state gate entirely — the setpoint is now always stored, and (unchanged) only has an electrical effect once the unit is ONLINE and appears in `pv_bus_constraints()`. This is a general fix, not scoped to Shift 1 — any future shift wanting to pre-configure an offline unit's setpoint for when it starts now works correctly. 28/28 tests still pass after the change.
- **Shift 1 "First Watch" rewritten as the full tutorial arc, one continuous shift**: `DURATION_HOURS` 3.5→**3.2** (192 sim-min = exactly 8 real minutes at `TIME_COMPRESSION=24`, the developer's stated real-time budget), `START_HOUR` unchanged at 6.0. `AGC_ENABLED = True` from T+0 (no more `AGC_SET` mid-shift action — that plumbing from Session 76 stays in the engine, unused by this shift, in case a future shift wants the before/after contrast). `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` reworked so only ASHC-1 is AGC-eligible and online at handover (`RIVE-1`/`RIVE-2` at their 105 MW technical minimum, `ASHC-1` at 60 MW, **`HOLT-1` absent → starts OFFLINE**) — deliberately, so ASHC-1's 250 MW ceiling alone cannot cover the shift's ~515 MW forecast peak, creating a real forcing function within the 8-minute budget (earlier baseline attempts with all 4 units online at moderate levels found ASHC-1+HOLT-1's combined 400 MW AGC-eligible ceiling comfortably covers any dispatch baseline tried, discovered via multiple headless-trace iterations that kept showing RIVE-1 never needing to move). `INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS = {'HOLT-1': 0.95}` is declared even though Holt starts offline, relying on the `set_voltage_setpoint` fix above.
  - **Two independent player fixes confirmed via headless trace**, matching the developer's "start Holt Hydro if needed — also an important lesson" framing: raising RIVE-1 toward ~165 MW by hand (do-nothing floor, freq collapses to 45 Hz by ~T+180; a player who starts raising RIVE-1 around T+80 toward 165 MW holds ~49.6 Hz through shift end), or starting Holt Hydro (5-minute cold start, `cold_start_min=5.0`) around the same time (holds ~49.99 Hz — better, since Holt is AGC-eligible and hydro-fast, vs. RIVE-1's 9 MW/min ramp).
  - **Every condition-gated `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` beat retimed against real headless traces**, repeating Session 76's own hard-learned lesson about `_process_scripted_events()`'s check-once-at-trigger_min semantics (a condition false at the exact trigger minute means the beat silently never fires, not "fires once true"): the "Ashcombe past 200 MW" `WARNING` (ASHC-1 crosses 200 MW at T+83.7 for a do-nothing player → `trigger_min=86.0`), the "spinning reserve tightening" `WARNING` (an initial `SPINNING_RESERVE_MW < 430` threshold missed by 0.04 MW at its first `trigger_min=93.0` attempt in one trace — widened to `< 445` and retimed to `86.0→87.0` for a safety margin after re-tracing the actual crossing at T+82.6), the Fenwick-sag `WARNING` (`VOLTAGE_PU < 0.92`, crosses at T+95.1 once Holt starts at T+90 → `trigger_min=96.0`), and the Fenwick-recovery `TUTOR` (`VOLTAGE_PU >= 0.98`, confirmed only true once the player actually raises Holt's setpoint, not on its own → `trigger_min=115.0`). The L09/L01 N-1 pair (unconditional `LINE_OPEN` action + the `_L09_STILL_OPEN`/`_L09_CLOSED` condition pair) reuses Session 76's exact pattern, retimed to `trigger_min=152.0`/`153.0` to land after the voltage-lesson beats within the new 192-minute window.
- **Shifts 2 and 3 reverted to stubs** (`shift_02.py`, `shift_03.py`) — docstring-only, mirroring the exact placeholder pattern Session 71 already established once for this same "merge N shifts into one, stub the rest" move, and matching today's `shift_06.py`-`shift_10.py` convention.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass throughout (checked after the droop-default change, the `set_voltage_setpoint` fix, and the final shift rewrite). All 3 shifts (1 real, 2 stubs) built and ticked through the real `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer()` path for 50 ticks each with no exception, confirming `_sim_const.DROOP_ENABLED` reads `False` at runtime for Shift 1 without the shift file declaring it. Full responsive-vs-do-nothing headless comparison for the merged shift (mirroring Session 76's per-act verification, now for the single continuous sequence): do-nothing player gets both Act-1 margin `WARNING`s, the Fenwick `WARNING` never fires (never started Holt), an escalating `WARNING`→`CRITICAL` frequency sequence, `ASHC-1 tripped — isolated from grid` at the L01 trip, and `is_shift_failed() == True` (frequency pinned at 45 Hz); a responsive player (closes L09 at T+20, starts Holt at T+80, raises Holt's setpoint at T+100) gets a clean run — every `TUTOR`/`WARNING` fires in the intended order, zero `CRITICAL` alarms, `is_shift_failed() == False`, final frequency 49.99 Hz. Separately confirmed the RIVE-1-only fix path (never starts Holt, raises RIVE-1 by hand from T+80) also avoids failure (final frequency 49.6 Hz) — both of the developer's stated fix paths are real, not just the one exercised in the main verification run.
- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (matches this project's established headless/synthetic-surface verification pattern) — this is itself a response to the previous session's playtest, so a further playtest of this merged shift is the natural next step before further tuning. Session 76's still-unresolved "is voltage/reactive actually fun" question is unchanged by this session (Fenwick's AVR lesson is structurally the same, just retimed). The droop-default flip's effect on Shifts 4-10 is unreviewed beyond confirming the fallback plumbing is consistent — those shifts are still placeholder/test content pending a rebuild.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`DROOP_ENABLED` default), `src/gameplay/shifts/loader.py` (`droop_enabled` fallback default + docstring), `src/main.py` (`_make_shift_test()`'s `droop_enabled` fallback default), `src/data/shift_io.py` (`ShiftDefinition.droop_enabled` dataclass default + 2 more fallback defaults), `src/simulation/units.py` (`set_voltage_setpoint()` — removed the `ONLINE` state gate), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_01.py` (full rewrite — merged single-shift tutorial arc), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_02.py`, `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_03.py` (both reverted to stubs).

### Session 76 (Rescope Phase 2 tutorial arc to 3 shifts — shared grid, Ferranti line charging, AGC_SET action, TIME_COMPRESSION retune)

- **Context**: the developer reported the Session 71-75 merged Shift 1 was too hard/fast for a first-time player — the demand ramp forced near-constant correction with no calm stretch, and the two-unit handoff landed on top of that pressure. Rather than re-tune the existing shift in isolation, this session rescoped the whole Phase 2 (real-time) tutorial arc through an extended design conversation covering scope (which mechanics each shift should teach), pacing (a calm/tension rhythm, target 5-7 real minutes per shift vs. the old 3.75), and several technical decisions that needed explicit developer sign-off rather than being assumed: a global `TIME_COMPRESSION` retune (affects every shift, not just the tutorials), a new game-wide simulation-physics addition (line charging), and two scripted-action engine gaps. Phase 1 (planning-screen) content was explicitly out of scope — Shift 5 is untouched.
- **`TIME_COMPRESSION`: single global constant, 48.0→24.0** (`constants.py`) — the developer's explicit choice over a per-shift override (the original plan), on the reasoning that one clock speed for the whole game is simpler and Shifts 4-10 are placeholder/test content that will be redone regardless of the pacing change. Every existing read site (`main.py`'s 2 tick call sites, `canvas.py`'s overload countdown, `simulation.py`'s `_TRIP_DELAY_REAL_S`) picks up the new value automatically since they all read the same constant — no propagation plumbing needed. `TRIP_DELAY_S`/`BLACKOUT_TRIP_S`'s doc comments updated to the new real-second figures (30s/15s at 1x, was 15s/7.5s).
- **New Ferranti-effect line-charging physics, game-wide** — the developer's answer to a separate concern (raised independently, folded into this session): what stops a player from just closing every spare/parallel circuit and leaving it closed forever, since the game had no cost for doing so? Real transmission lines inject reactive power locally at both ends when energised, more so when lightly loaded — this was entirely absent from `voltage.py`'s decoupled model (only a flat per-bus `VSHUNT_REG` numerical regulariser existed, not a per-line physical effect). Implemented as a fixed MVAr injection per bus (`simulation.py::_line_charging_mvar_per_km()`, new `LINE_CHARGING_MVAR_PER_KM_150/220/400KV` constants), fed into `_build_q_injections()` exactly like the existing shunt-bank/SVC reactive devices already are — **not** a change to `voltage.py`'s B' matrix (an earlier attempt at implementing it that way was reverted: B' diagonal terms are numerical-stability admittance contributions, e.g. `VSHUNT_REG`, not physical Q injections; `q_injections` are the model's actual solver input for real reactive sources). Only in-service lines contribute (`_get_in_service_lines()`), so a tripped/open line charges nothing. This is a deliberate expansion of simulation physics beyond CLAUDE.md Rule 6's normal "never auto-correct/extend physics" default, approved explicitly by the developer for this case, applied **game-wide with no per-shift enable flag** (also an explicit developer choice, made after confirming it would break Shift 4/5's existing voltage tuning — see Verified below).
- **Sign confirmed empirically, magnitude required several tuning passes**: closing a parallel/spare line does raise voltage at the buses it touches (confirmed via before/after headless traces), but a single flat per-km rate cannot simultaneously be "visible" on a short 2-20 km spare circuit and "physically reasonable" on a 100+ km weak-feed line needed for a voltage-sag lesson — the constants that made Shift 2's spare circuit read as a real cost also, at that magnitude, wildly overvoltaged the new grid's long weak-feed line (and catastrophically overvoltaged Shift 4's existing 126 km RIVE-CLOV line, ~1.35 pu). Landed on `LINE_CHARGING_MVAR_PER_KM_150KV=0.06`/`_220KV=0.15`/`_400KV=0.4` — small enough not to pre-saturate a supporting generator's Q range on a 100+ km feed, while still giving a ~20 km spare circuit a real non-zero contribution (~3 MVAr on Shift 3's L09). Confirmed the developer's explicit acceptance that this still overvoltages Shift 4/5's existing (untouched) tuning — flagged prominently here since those shifts' voltage behaviour is no longer valid until they are rebuilt.
- **New `AGC_SET` scripted-event action** (`simulation.py::_execute_action()`, a 6th action type alongside `LINE_OPEN`/`LINE_CLOSE`/`UNIT_TRIP`/`UNIT_DERATE`/`DEMAND_OVERRIDE`, documented in `shift_io.py`'s schema docstring) — mutates the runtime `_sim_const.AGC_ENABLED` global mid-shift, the same process-wide flag `main.py`'s Ctrl+A debug toggle already flips (`_const.AGC_ENABLED = not _const.AGC_ENABLED`), confirmed as the correct precedent by reading that call site directly rather than assuming AGC's read path was per-instance. Added specifically so Shift 1 can teach "AGC switches on partway through a single continuous shift" as a real engine event, not just a narrative beat. A parallel "bring a unit online mid-shift via a new action type" idea (for Shift 1's RIVE-1) was considered and explicitly rejected in favour of the existing precedent from the old `shift_01.py`: RIVE-1 idles online at its technical minimum from T+0 ("nothing to do there yet"), and the Part A→B transition is the player being told to raise it, not the engine starting it — no new action type needed for that half.
- **New shared grid** `src/assets/designer_grids/tutorial.json` (8 buses — RIVE/ASHC/GREY/OAKE/SUTT/RAVE/HOLT/FENW; 11 lines; 4 units — RIVE-1/RIVE-2/ASHC-1/HOLT-1), used by all 3 rewritten shifts via `GRID_SOURCE = 'tutorial'`, each activating a different subset via `INITIAL_SCHEDULE`/`SUBSTATION_TYPES`/`MAINTENANCE_LINES`/`INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS` rather than separate grid files — matches the existing pattern where `shift_02.py`/`shift_03.py`/`shift_04.py` already point at hand-authored Designer grids, extended to one grid shared across three shift files instead of one grid per file. Built by hand-authoring the JSON directly (matching the existing schema `data/designer_io.py::load_designer_grid_named()` expects — `DesignerBus(**b)`/`DesignerLine(**l)`/`DesignerUnit(**u)` dataclasses) rather than through the interactive Grid Designer UI, since that tool is pygame-driven and not scriptable by an agent — the developer's explicit choice over pausing the session for manual UI authoring.
  - The Holt/Fenwick branch (Shift 2/3's voltage lesson) required several topology iterations to tune reliably: an initial ASHC→Holt→Fenwick series placement put Ashcombe's own AVR correction (targeting 1.02 pu) in electrical series with Holt's, and the two PV buses' correction loops fought each other within `voltage.py`'s fixed-point iteration (confirmed directly: with Ashcombe's target held at 1.02, raising Holt's setpoint from 0.95→1.05 produced *no change* in Fenwick's voltage at all; dropping Ashcombe's target to 1.00 immediately unblocked it) — moved Holt off Riverside directly (parallel to Ashcombe, not in series with it), matching how `shift4.json`'s working Batherton branch actually connects (off Colvend, not through Ashcombe). A second, unrelated problem: two long lines (Riverside→Holt, Holt→Fenwick) meeting at the same junction bus made the new Ferranti charging term additive there, itself pre-saturating Holt's Q ceiling regardless of AVR setpoint — fixed by making only one of the two lines long (130 km Riverside→Holt, 15 km Holt→Fenwick) rather than splitting the distance evenly.
- **Shift 1 "First Watch"** (`shift_01.py`, full rewrite) — Part A (T+0-90 min): ASHC-1 alone, `AGC_ENABLED = False`/`DROOP_ENABLED = False`, manual dispatch only, matching the pre-Session-71 "nothing corrects frequency but you" premise. Part B (T+90 onward): a scripted `AGC_SET` action turns AGC on, RIVE-1 (idling online at 105 MW the whole shift) is brought up by hand as ASHC-1 climbs toward its ceiling and spinning reserve visibly draws down (confirmed via headless trace: reserve falls from 312→247 MW as ASHC-1 climbs 132→198 MW under a realistic tracking player). Demand reflects only Greymoor+Oakendale (350 MW combined peak, not the shared grid's full 620 MW, since RIVE-2/HOLT-1 aren't dispatched this shift and nothing serves Ravensmere/Fenwick's load) via a T+0 `DEMAND_OVERRIDE` scripted action. `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` trigger times were re-timed twice against real headless traces after the first pass's condition-gated beats silently failed to fire (see the general finding below) — the WARNING/TUTOR pair now fires at T+125/135, confirmed against a player who tracks ASHC-1 correctly but never touches RIVE-1 (ASHC-1 crosses the 150 MW threshold at T+116 in that trace).
- **Shift 2 "Local Support"** (`shift_02.py`, full rewrite from the Session-71 stub) — voltage/AVR (Fenwick sags because Holt Hydro's AVR setpoint starts pinned at 0.95 pu, the campaign floor; raising it toward 1.05 lifts Fenwick back up — the same lesson shape as the old Shift 4's Batherton/Stavely pair) and topology/N-1 (a spare circuit, L09, parallel to L01 the only Riverside→Ashcombe link, sits open at handover; a later scripted L01 trip blacks out Ashcombe and everything behind it, including Fenwick, only if L09 was never closed). Fenwick's automatic shunt bank is deliberately undersized (1 of 4 steps, 10 MVAr) — confirmed empirically (matching the old `shift_04.py`'s FENN precedent) that the campaign-default 4-step ceiling fully self-heals the AVR-setpoint sag before the player ever sees it. Confirmed via headless trace with a fresh grid instance per run: do-nothing player's Fenwick voltage floor is 0.898 pu for the whole shift; a player who raises Holt's setpoint reaches ~1.07 pu within 20 minutes — the "Fenwick recovering" TUTOR beat's condition (`>= 0.95`) sits clearly between the two.
- **Shift 3 "Full Board"** (`shift_03.py`, replaces the old AGC/reserve content, which Shift 1 now teaches) — pure integration capstone, no new mechanics: opens with all 4 units online, AGC on, voltage live, L09 open again (mirroring Shift 2's setup). A scripted `DEMAND_OVERRIDE` at T+70 stresses spinning reserve and deepens Fenwick's sag in the same window (confirmed: reserve 388.8→284.5 MW and Fenwick 0.900→0.892 pu both move within the same 20-minute span) — the first shift requiring the player to split attention across frequency/reserve and voltage instruments at once. Same L01/L09 N-1 test as Shift 2 at T+160.
- **General finding, applied across all 3 shifts**: `GridSimulation._process_scripted_events()`'s condition-gated events are evaluated **exactly once**, at `trigger_min` — if the condition is false at that instant the event is marked fired and **never retried** (`simulation.py:1312-1327`). This is different from a "wait until true" gate. Several first-pass `trigger_min` values (picked from rough demand-curve estimates, not traces) landed before the real crossing point and silently never fired; every condition-gated beat in the final 3 shifts was re-timed against an actual headless trace of the relevant player behaviour before being accepted, matching the project's existing `shift_01.py`/`shift_03.py`/`shift_04.py` precedent of tracing before picking timings — this session just needed to re-learn that precedent's importance the hard way.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass throughout (checked after every constant/engine change). Ferranti sign/magnitude verified via before/after headless traces on both the new `tutorial.json` grid and the existing `shift3.json`/`shift4.json` (confirming the severe-overvoltage blast radius on Shift 4's long lines, accepted by the developer). All 3 shifts built and ticked through the real `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer()` path (not just direct `GridSimulation` construction) headlessly for 50 ticks each with no exception. Each shift's core lesson verified via a responsive-player vs. do-nothing-player headless trace comparison: Shift 1's do-nothing player collapses to the 45 Hz floor by T+30 (Part A) and a realistic tracking player completes without failure while triggering the AGC/reserve beats correctly (Part B); Shift 2's do-nothing player gets the L09-still-open CRITICAL alarm and never gets the false "recovering" TUTOR, while a responsive player gets a clean run with no CRITICAL alarms; Shift 3's do-nothing player survives (AGC absorbs the deficit, matching the "no new mechanics" capstone framing) but racks up repeated AGC-exhausted warnings and the L09 CRITICAL alarm, while a responsive player's run is clean. **Caught and fixed a test-harness gap mid-session** (not a product bug): headless `GridSimulation` construction alone does not propagate a shift's `agc_enabled`/`droop_enabled`/`freq_tolerance_mult` onto the runtime `_sim_const` globals the way `main.py`'s real build path does (`_const.AGC_ENABLED = cfg['agc_enabled']` etc., `main.py:294-296`) — an early Shift 1 trace silently ran with AGC/droop both still on, masking Part A's "nothing corrects frequency but you" lesson entirely; every verification trace after this was caught explicitly replicates that propagation.
- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (matches this project's established headless/synthetic-surface verification pattern) — whether Shift 2's voltage/reactive mechanic (VSI tiers + AVR, the manual SVC stays in scope but secondary) is actually fun and masterable for a first-time player is **explicitly unresolved and flagged as the single biggest uncertainty in this whole rescoping** (the developer's own framing, from the design conversation preceding implementation) — shipped as scoped, to be judged by a real playtest, not resolved by further headless tuning. Shift 4/5's voltage tuning is now invalid under the new Ferranti physics and needs a full rebuild pass regardless (already known/deferred per Session 71's notes on Shift 10). `TIME_COMPRESSION`'s doubling of every non-tutorial shift's real-time length is unreviewed beyond the doc-comment fix.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`TIME_COMPRESSION` 48.0→24.0 + doc comment, `TRIP_DELAY_S`/`BLACKOUT_TRIP_S` comment updates, new `LINE_CHARGING_MVAR_PER_KM_150/220/400KV`), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (new `_line_charging_mvar_per_km()` helper + import, `_build_q_injections()` line-charging contribution, new `AGC_SET` action in `_execute_action()`), `src/data/shift_io.py` (`AGC_SET` documented in the action-schema docstring), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_01.py` (full rewrite — "First Watch"), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_02.py` (full rewrite from stub — "Local Support"), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_03.py` (full rewrite — "Full Board").
- Created: `src/assets/designer_grids/tutorial.json` (shared grid for Shifts 1-3).

### Session 75 (Remove load-variation plot, move it to a POWER BALANCE text row, zoom frequency plot to 48-53 Hz)

- **Context**: the side-by-side load-variation plot added last session didn't work out visually. The developer asked to remove it entirely, put the load-variation figure on the POWER BALANCE panel instead as a plain text row (matching the existing GEN/LOAD/BAL/SPIN RES/INERTIA/LOSSES style, placed directly under BAL), restore the FREQUENCY panel to its original single-plot full-width layout, and zoom the frequency plot/bar's Hz range from 45-55 to 48-53 (an asymmetric range around 50 Hz nominal, the developer's explicit choice).
- **FREQUENCY panel — plot removed, layout un-split, range narrowed**: `draw_frequency_panel()` (`panels.py`) lost its `half_w` two-column split and the entire "load variation" block from last session — the Hz readout, bar, trend/clock row, and vertical frequency plot are back to using the panel's full width, and the `load_rate_history` parameter was dropped from this function entirely (the data itself wasn't touched — see below). `_fill_frac()` changed from `(f - 45.0) / 10.0` to `(f - 48.0) / 5.0`; the bar's tick labels moved from 45/50/55 to 48/50.5/53; the plot's own internal gridlines moved from 49.5/50.0/50.5 to 49.0/50.0/51.0 (spread further apart to read sensibly against the now much narrower 5 Hz span, instead of nearly overlapping at the old spacing).
- **POWER BALANCE panel — new LOAD VAR (/MIN) row**: `draw_power_panel()` gained a `load_rate_history` parameter (the same deque `renderer.py` already computes — nothing changed in how the MW/min rate itself is derived, only which panel displays it) and a new row inserted into the existing `rows` list right after BAL: label `'LOAD VAR (/MIN)'`, value `f'{rate:+.1f} MW'` matching BAL's sign-aware style, coloured `COL_TEXT_SECONDARY` (matching SPIN RES's muted treatment, since this is supplementary context rather than a primary alarm figure). No other layout constant needed touching — the divider/sub-header/reg-band section beneath the row list is already positioned via `len(rows)`, so it shifted down automatically to accommodate the new row.
- **Renderer wiring swapped**: `renderer.py`'s `draw_frequency_panel(...)` call site no longer passes `load_rate_history`; `draw_power_panel(...)` now does. `freq_key`'s dirty-key tuple dropped its three load-rate-derived entries (no longer relevant to that panel); `power_key` gained one (the rate history's latest value, rounded) so POWER BALANCE's cache invalidates correctly when only the load-variation figure changes. `self._load_rate_history`, `_prev_load_mw`, `_prev_load_sim_hour`, and the sim-time-aware MW/min computation added last session are all unchanged — only which panel consumes the data moved.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged (no simulation-side logic touched). Confirmed the new axis math directly: `_fill_frac(48.0) == 0.0`, `_fill_frac(50.5) == 0.5`, `_fill_frac(53.0) == 1.0`. Render-checked both panels directly (synthetic freetype font, offscreen surface) — `draw_frequency_panel()` across zero/five-sample histories and `state=None`, `draw_power_panel()` across zero/one/multi-sample `load_rate_history` and `state=None` — all rendered without exception. Ran 50 real frames through `_make_sim_and_renderer(shift=1)`, confirming both panels draw cleanly end-to-end and `_load_rate_history` still populates correctly (unaffected by the panel move). Grepped for stray `load_rate_history` references to confirm the parameter now lives only on `draw_power_panel()`.
