- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (all verification headless/synthetic-surface, matching this project's established pattern) — real row spacing on POWER BALANCE with the new 7-row layout, and the narrower frequency bar's on-screen readability at actual scale, are easiest to judge by eye and flagged as the natural next step.
- Edited: `src/display/panels.py` (`draw_frequency_panel()`: removed two-column split + load-variation block, restored full-width layout, narrowed `_fill_frac()`/tick labels/gridlines to 48-53 Hz, dropped `load_rate_history` param; `draw_power_panel()`: new `load_rate_history` param, new LOAD VAR (/MIN) row), `src/display/renderer.py` (swapped which panel's call site receives `load_rate_history`, updated `freq_key`/`power_key` dirty-key tuples).

### Session 74 (Halve Shift 1 duration + retime events, revert frequency plot to vertical, replace load plot with side-by-side load-variation plot)

- **Context**: the developer found Shift 1 too long and asked to cut it in half while keeping the same scripted-event story beats, and asked for two FREQUENCY-panel reworks: revert last session's horizontal trend-plot rotation back to vertical, and replace the load (raw MW) plot with a load-*variation* (MW/min) plot placed side-by-side with the frequency plot instead of stacked underneath — per the developer's own mockup, both plots vertical, sharing the panel's width.
- **Shift 1 halved — with a real forcing-function check, not a naive halving**: `DURATION_HOURS` 6.0→3.0, but `START_HOUR` also moved 4.0→**6.5** (was going to be 5.5, revised after tracing it — see below). Demand in this game is driven by real sim-hour-of-day via the shared `DEMAND_PROFILE_NORMALISED` curve (`src/data/profiles.py`), not elapsed shift-time, so simply halving the duration at the old 04:00 start would have kept the shorter shift entirely within the flattest part of the morning ramp — checked directly: at the naive new end-of-shift (07:00) demand is only 232 MW, meaning ASHC-1 alone would need just ~127 MW (232 minus RIVE-1's fixed 105 MW floor) and would never cross the existing 195 MW warning threshold, silently defusing the entire two-unit forcing function the shift is built around. Traced several `START_HOUR` candidates against a real headless "realistic player" run (nudging ASHC-1 to track load minus RIVE-1's output every tick) before picking one: 5.5 crosses 195 MW at T+157 (too late in a 180-minute shift to leave reaction room), 6.5 crosses at **T+97** (comfortably mid-shift) with ASHC-1 climbing to ~242 MW by shift end (near but not pinned to its 250 MW ceiling, matching the original shift's character) — 6.5 was chosen. All 9 `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` `trigger_min` values were then re-timed against this actual T+97 crossing point (confirmed via the same trace) rather than mechanically halved from the old 6-hour shift's timings: opening TUTOR beats stay proportionally early (0, 2.5), the two ceiling-WARNING beats moved to 80/105 (was 220/245 in the old 6h shift) bracketing the real crossing, a new "how to bring Riverside up" TUTOR beat sits between them at 90 (was 245 alone), "two units now" moved to 110 (was 290), and the closing "approaching handover" check moved to 165 (was 340), all within the new 180-minute total. Module docstring's narrative numbers (demand curve values, crossing time) and two stale "pre-dawn"/"night handover" phrasings (no longer accurate once the shift starts at 06:30, not 04:00) were corrected in the docstring, `HANDOVER_NOTES`, and one scripted-event detail string.
- **Frequency plot reverted to vertical**: `draw_frequency_panel()` (`panels.py`) — restored the original top-to-bottom strip chart (X = Hz value via the bar's existing `_fill_frac()`, 45-55 Hz range; Y = time, newest sample nearest the bar) in place of last session's horizontal rotation. The now-unused `FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MIN_HZ`/`FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MAX_HZ` constants (`constants.py`) and their import in `panels.py` were removed — the vertical plot reuses the bar's own 45-55 Hz mapping instead of a separate narrower range.
- **Load plot → load-variation plot, moved beside the frequency plot**: the panel now splits its width in half (`half_w = w // 2`) with a vertical divider — left half is the original frequency readout/bar/trend-clock row/vertical plot (now confined to the left half's width instead of the full panel width), right half is new: a "LOAD VAR" label, a `±X.X MW/min` readout (`COL_FORECAST_DEMAND`, reused from last session's load plot), and its own vertical history plot (newest nearest the top, auto-scaled to visible history's min/max with padding, plus a horizontal zero-line gridline so positive/negative swings are visually distinguishable). New `Renderer._load_rate_history` deque (`renderer.py`) replaces last session's raw `_load_history` — computed once per rendered frame from consecutive `state.total_load_mw` samples divided by **sim-time elapsed** (`state.sim_hour` deltas × 60, not real-time), since the game runs at variable speed multipliers (pause/1x/3x/10x) and a real-time derivative would mean a different thing at each speed; `_prev_load_mw`/`_prev_load_sim_hour` track the previous sample for this. `freq_key`'s dirty-key tuple updated to track the rate history's length/oldest/newest sample instead of the old raw-load entries.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged (no simulation-side logic touched). Headless traces via the real `load_shift_config(1)`/`GridSimulation` path confirmed the `START_HOUR=6.5` crossing-point analysis above, then re-ran the full retimed `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` sequence against both a realistic tracking player (opening beats, the 105-min WARNING, and the 110-min "two units now" beat all fire in the right order and land sensibly relative to the new 180-minute total; `is_shift_complete()` True / `is_shift_failed()` False at shift end, frequency held ~49.9 Hz) and confirmed a do-nothing player still crashes toward the clamp early (the core "nothing corrects frequency but you" lesson survives the shorter window). Render-checked `draw_frequency_panel()` directly (synthetic freetype font, offscreen surface) across zero/one/200-sample histories for both series and a flat (zero-span) load-rate history, plus the `state=None` fallback — all rendered without exception. Ran 50 real frames through `_make_sim_and_renderer(shift=1)` confirming `sim._duration_minutes` is exactly 180, `_freq_history`/`_load_rate_history` both populate (the rate history is one shorter, as expected — its first sample needs a predecessor to diff against), and all touched modules import cleanly.
- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (all verification headless/synthetic-surface, matching this project's established pattern) — the retimed event beats are a first empirical fit against one synthetic tracking-player profile, not yet played by a human, and the two-column panel's actual pixel proportions/label placement at real scaled size are easiest to judge by eye, flagged as the natural next step alongside a real playtest of the shorter shift's pacing.
- Edited: `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_01.py` (`START_HOUR`, `DURATION_HOURS`, all `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` `trigger_min` values, docstring narrative/demand-curve numbers, `HANDOVER_NOTES` wording, one event detail string), `src/display/panels.py` (`draw_frequency_panel()`: reverted vertical frequency plot, new two-column split, new load-variation readout/plot, `load_history`→`load_rate_history` param, removed `FREQ_TREND_PLOT_*` import), `src/simulation/constants.py` (removed `FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MIN_HZ`/`FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MAX_HZ`), `src/display/renderer.py` (`_load_history`→`_load_rate_history` + `_prev_load_mw`/`_prev_load_sim_hour`, per-frame MW/min computation, updated `freq_key` dirty-key tuple, updated `draw_frequency_panel(...)` call site).

### Session 73 (Nudge step 5→1 MW, rotate frequency trend plot horizontal + narrow range, add load trend plot)

- **Context**: further review of Session 72's `G`+Up/Down active-power nudge and the FREQUENCY panel's trend plot surfaced three refinements: (1) 5 MW felt too coarse for the normal step — the fast (Ctrl) step should be the 5 MW one; (2) the frequency trend plot's orientation was confusing — it ran top-to-bottom with time on the Y-axis and Hz on the X-axis (newest sample near the top, wiggling left-right as Hz varied), the opposite of a conventional oscilloscope/strip-chart reading, and its 45-55 Hz range (inherited from sharing a mapping function with the analog bar above it, which deliberately spans the full `F_MIN`/`F_MAX` hard clamp) compressed real excursions into a visual sliver; (3) requested a new load (system demand) trend plot directly under the frequency plot for at-a-glance context. Confirmed with the developer beforehand: split the FREQUENCY panel's remaining vertical space evenly between the two stacked plots, load-only (not generation) on the new plot, auto-scaled to its own visible history rather than a fixed MW range (load ranges from ~100 MW to ~8000 MW across shifts — a fixed range would flatten small shifts or clip large ones).
- **Nudge step**: `UNIT_MW_STEP` (`constants.py`) 5.0→**1.0**; `UNIT_MW_STEP_FAST_MULT` stays 5.0, so `G`+Up/Down now moves 1 MW per press, Ctrl+Up/Down moves 5 MW (was 5/25). Single call site (`renderer.py`'s `on_target_adjust()`) already read both constants — no other code needed to change.
- **Frequency trend plot rotated + range narrowed**: `draw_frequency_panel()` (`panels.py`) previously shared one `_fill_frac()` (hardcoded 45-55 Hz span) between the horizontal analog bar and the trend plot; the bar deliberately keeps that full hard-clamp range unchanged, so a separate `_trend_frac()` was added using two new named constants, `FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MIN_HZ = 48.0`/`FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MAX_HZ = 52.0` (`constants.py`, distinct from `F_MIN`/`F_MAX`). The plot-drawing block was rewritten as a shared `_draw_trend_plot()` helper (time/sample-index → X left-to-right, oldest at the left edge and newest at the right; value → Y via a caller-supplied fraction function, inverted so a higher value sits higher on screen) used by both the frequency and load plots. Gridlines moved from vertical (at fixed Hz values) to horizontal.
- **New load trend plot**: new `self._load_history` deque in `Renderer.__init__` (`renderer.py`), sized identically to `_freq_history` (same `FREQ_HISTORY_WINDOW_S * TARGET_FPS` length) and sampled the same way — once per rendered frame from `state.total_load_mw`, alongside the existing frequency sample. Drawn in the lower half of the FREQUENCY panel (confirmed during planning: no panel in this layout stacks vertically today — every `PANEL_*_X/W` in `constants.py` implicitly spans the full 236px strip height — so this lives inside the FREQUENCY panel's own column rather than as a new top-level panel/cache-slot/blit-call). Range is computed fresh each draw from `min()`/`max()` of the visible history with ~10% padding (or a flat ±10% of the value when history is perfectly flat, avoiding a zero-span divide). Reuses the already-imported `COL_FORECAST_DEMAND` for the line colour rather than adding a new palette constant; a small "LOAD" text label distinguishes it from the frequency plot above (which needs no such label — the Hz gridlines/readout above already identify it). `draw_frequency_panel()` gained a `load_history` parameter; `renderer.py`'s dirty key (`freq_key`) extended with the load history's length + oldest/newest sample so the cache invalidates on load-only changes (e.g. load drifting while frequency briefly holds steady).
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged (no simulation-side code touched). Headless checks via the real `_make_sim_and_renderer(shift=1)` path: confirmed `UNIT_MW_STEP`/`UNIT_MW_STEP_FAST_MULT` read 1.0/5.0 and a real `G`+Up then Ctrl+Up sequence moves `ASHC-1`'s target 30→31→36 MW exactly as expected; ran 50 real render frames with varying simulated load/frequency and confirmed both `_freq_history`/`_load_history` populate correctly with no exceptions. Called `draw_frequency_panel()` directly (synthetic freetype font, offscreen surface) across edge cases: zero history, a single sample, 200 varying samples, a perfectly flat load history (zero span — confirmed the padding fallback avoids a divide-by-zero), and `state=None` (menu fallback) — all rendered without exception. Confirmed the axis math directly: `_trend_frac(48.0)==0.0`, `_trend_frac(50.0)==0.5`, `_trend_frac(52.0)==1.0` (higher Hz → higher on screen, as intended), and the `_draw_trend_plot()` x-formula places sample index 0 (oldest) at the left edge and the newest sample at the right edge.
- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (all verification headless/synthetic-surface, matching this project's established pattern) — exact pixel proportions of the now-split panel (label placement, gridline spacing, whether two half-height plots read comfortably at the strip's actual 236px scaled size) are easiest to judge by eye in a live window, flagged as the natural next step. The 1 MW/5 MW step, 48-52 Hz trend range, and load-plot padding percentage are first-pass values, not final, same caveat as every other recent tuning change.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`UNIT_MW_STEP` value, new `FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MIN_HZ`/`FREQ_TREND_PLOT_MAX_HZ`), `src/display/panels.py` (`draw_frequency_panel()`: new `_trend_frac()`, rewritten `_draw_trend_plot()` helper replacing the old vertical strip-chart block, new load-plot block, new `load_history` param, new constants import), `src/display/renderer.py` (`self._load_history` deque, per-frame sampling, extended `freq_key` dirty-key tuple, `draw_frequency_panel(...)` call site passes `load_history`).

### Session 72 (Shift 1 playtest response — fast nudge control, wider tutorial tolerance, more TUTOR beats, real blackout fail-state)

- **Context**: the developer played the Session 71 merged Shift 1 tutorial and found it too hard for a first-time player, for four reasons — (1) the only way to change a unit's output was select-unit → Enter → type digits → Enter, too slow to juggle two units under time pressure; (2) frequency tolerance is the same global band for every shift, no easier tutorial band; (3) tutorial messages could do more around the hard RIVE-1 handoff; (4) asked what frequency triggers a shift-ending blackout. Investigation for (4) found the answer was **nothing** — `is_shift_complete()` (`simulation.py`) was a pure clock check; frequency just clamps at the existing `F_MIN`/`F_MAX` (45/55 Hz) hard floor/ceiling and the shift runs to its scheduled end regardless. This also matches `GAMEPLAY_ANALYSIS.md`'s independently-flagged #1 gap ("no fail state means no tension") — scoped here to just the fail-trigger itself, not that document's much larger Improvement #2 (scoring.py/campaign.py/debrief.py rewrite, still open). The developer confirmed: a dedicated key (not bare arrows) to arm the nudge, keep the existing digit+Enter entry alongside it, and yes — add the blackout fail-state, questioning whether 45/55 Hz was too wide a trigger threshold (it is: real under-frequency load-shedding acts around 47-49 Hz) — resolved by triggering off **sustained time pinned at the existing hard clamp** rather than inventing a new absolute Hz threshold.
- **Fast active-power adjust control**: new `G` key arms "adjust mode" on the selected unit (`Renderer._adjust_active`, `on_adjust_toggle()`), then `Up`/`Down` nudge `target_mw` by a new `UNIT_MW_STEP` (5 MW, `constants.py`) via `Renderer.on_target_adjust(sim, direction, fast)`, `Ctrl+Up`/`Ctrl+Down` for `UNIT_MW_STEP_FAST_MULT` (5x → 25 MW). Mirrors the existing SVC step-adjust precedent (`on_svc_adjust()`/`SVC_Q_STEP_MVAR`, `,`/`.` keys) rather than a new pattern. Clamped to `[unit.min_mw, unit.rated_mw]`, same bounds `on_enter()` already applies; commits immediately via `sim.set_unit_target()` (no buffer/second-Enter needed). `on_tab()`/`on_escape()`/`clear_selection()` all reset `_adjust_active`, matching how every other input-mode flag is cleared on reselect/deselect. **Existing digit+Enter entry is completely unchanged** — both paths write through the same `sim.set_unit_target()`, so a player can nudge fine with `G`+arrows or type an exact number for a big jump. `context.py`'s `draw_unit_context()` shows a magenta (`COL_SVC`, reused rather than adding a new colour) Target-field border and hint-row reminder ("ADJUST ARMED...") when armed, replacing the normal `[min-rated MW]` hint line.
- **Per-shift frequency tolerance**: new `FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT: float = 1.0` (`constants.py`, default — no behaviour change for Shifts 3-10) multiplies `F_ALERT_LOW/HIGH`/`F_CRITICAL_LOW/HIGH`'s deltas from `F_NOMINAL`; `F_MIN`/`F_MAX` (the hard clamp) are deliberately never scaled. Wired through the exact plumbing Session 71 established for `DROOP_ENABLED` — `loader.py`'s `load_shift_config()` reads it via `getattr(mod, 'FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT', 1.0)`, `main.py` propagates it onto the `_const.FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT` runtime global at all three existing `AGC_ENABLED`/`DROOP_ENABLED` assignment sites, and `shift_io.py`'s full Shift Builder round-trip (`ShiftDefinition` field, JSON save/load, `shift_def_to_config()`, `CAMPAIGN_EDITABLE_FIELDS`/`_FIELD_TO_CONSTANT`/AST annotation map/values map) got the same ~9-touch-point treatment `droop_enabled` did. `simulation.py` gained `GridSimulation._freq_bounds()`, a single helper computing the effective `(alert_low, alert_high, critical_low, critical_high)` tuple from `_sim_const.FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT`, used by both `_update_frequency_alarms()` and `_update_crisis()` (previously each read the bare module-level constants directly). `shift_01.py` sets `FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT = 2.0` — roughly doubles the tutorial's working band to ~±0.4 Hz alert / ±1.0 Hz critical.
- **More TUTOR guidance**: added a T+5min beat explaining the new `G`+arrow control explicitly (Tab to select, G to arm, Up/Down/Ctrl+Up/Down to adjust, or the existing type-a-number path). Retimed the first Riverside-ceiling warning earlier (T+250→T+220, since the wider tolerance buys more reaction room) and added a new T+245 TUTOR beat walking through *how* to bring RIVE-1 up with the new control specifically ("Tab to select RIVE-1, press G, hold Up"), ahead of the existing T+285 repeat warning and T+290 two-unit reinforcement beat (both unchanged). `HANDOVER_NOTES` and the module docstring updated to mention both changes.
- **Real blackout / frequency-collapse fail state**: new `BLACKOUT_TRIP_S: float = 360.0` sim-seconds (`constants.py`, = 7.5 real seconds at 1x — a deliberately short but non-instant window, so a single bad tick doesn't insta-fail but sitting at the clamp clearly isn't survivable) alongside `TRIP_DELAY_S` in the LINE TRIP/CASCADE block. `GridSimulation` gained `_blackout_clamp_s` (running counter, reset to 0 the instant frequency is back inside `[F_MIN, F_MAX]`) and `_shift_failed`, updated each tick via new `_update_blackout_state(dt_sim_seconds)` (called right after `_update_crisis()`). `is_shift_complete()` now returns `self._shift_failed or` the original clock check; new `is_shift_failed()` accessor lets the caller distinguish a real blackout from reaching scheduled end. Applies globally (all shifts), not just Shift 1 — Shift 1's wider tolerance just makes it harder to reach by accident during the tutorial. `main.py`'s real shift-end poll (`if sim.is_shift_complete():`) now passes `failed=sim.is_shift_failed()` through to `build_debrief_lines()`, which gained a `failed` parameter — on a blackout it overrides the usual metric-based assessment with `'FAILED — SYSTEM BLACKOUT'` and inserts a red "FREQUENCY COLLAPSE" block ahead of the normal frequency-performance section. The `Ctrl+N` debug shortcut (manual end-shift, unrelated to a real failure) is unaffected — `failed` defaults to `False`.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged. Headless checks via a real `load_shift_config(1)` → `GridSimulation` build (the actual `_make_sim_and_renderer` path, through `main.py`): confirmed `freq_tolerance_mult`/`droop_enabled` load correctly; `_freq_bounds()` returns the expected widened `[49.6, 50.4]`/`[49.0, 51.0]` Hz bands (vs. default `[49.8, 50.2]`/`[49.5, 50.5]`); a do-nothing player (no target changes) still drifts to the 45 Hz floor exactly as before (tolerance widening doesn't remove the core "nothing corrects frequency but you" lesson) and now correctly triggers `is_shift_failed() == True` once pinned there past `BLACKOUT_TRIP_S`, where previously the same run would have played out to the full clock; a real `Renderer`/`GridSimulation` pair driven programmatically through `on_adjust_toggle()`/`on_target_adjust()` (simulating G, Up, Ctrl+Up, Tab key presses) confirmed the armed flag, the 5 MW and 25 MW (fast) steps both land correctly after a tick, and Tab correctly clears the armed flag. All modules re-import cleanly.
- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (all verification headless, matching this project's established pattern); the `UNIT_MW_STEP`/`FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT`/`BLACKOUT_TRIP_S` values are first-pass playtest candidates, not final, same caveat as every other recent tuning session; a real human playtest of the retuned Shift 1 (and the new blackout fail-state on any shift) is the natural next step. The broader `GAMEPLAY_ANALYSIS.md` Improvement #2 (scoring.py/campaign.py/debrief.py, real save/load, replay-for-better-grade) remains untouched — this session added only the fail-trigger, not the scoring/campaign rewrite around it.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`UNIT_MW_STEP`, `UNIT_MW_STEP_FAST_MULT`, `FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT`, `BLACKOUT_TRIP_S`), `src/display/renderer.py` (`_adjust_active` flag, `on_adjust_toggle()`, `on_target_adjust()`, resets in `on_tab()`/`on_escape()`/`clear_selection()`, `draw_unit_context()` call site), `src/display/context.py` (`adjust_active` param, Target-field border colour, hint-row text), `src/main.py` (`K_g` binding, `K_UP`/`K_DOWN` routing, `FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT` propagation at 3 sites, `build_debrief_lines()` `failed` param + blackout block, `COL_ALARM_CRIT` import, shift-end poll passes `sim.is_shift_failed()`), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (`F_MIN`/`F_MAX`/`BLACKOUT_TRIP_S` imports, `_freq_bounds()`, `_update_frequency_alarms()`/`_update_crisis()` updated to use it, `_blackout_clamp_s`/`_shift_failed` state, `_update_blackout_state()`, `is_shift_complete()`/`is_shift_failed()`), `src/gameplay/shifts/loader.py` (`freq_tolerance_mult` config key + docstring), `src/data/shift_io.py` (`freq_tolerance_mult` at all ~9 `droop_enabled` touch points), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_01.py` (`FREQ_TOLERANCE_MULT = 2.0`, new/retimed `SCRIPTED_EVENTS`, `HANDOVER_NOTES`, docstring).

### Session 71 (Fix droop breaking Shift 1/2's manual-dispatch lesson; merge Shift 1+2 into one tutorial)

- **Problem found**: Session 70's universal governor droop (called unconditionally at `simulation.py`'s tick step 5a, ahead of AGC) silently broke Shift 1 and Shift 2's entire premise. Both tutorials set `AGC_ENABLED = False` and tell the player "AGC off — its output never changes unless you change it," but droop isn't gated by `AGC_ENABLED` at all. Verified empirically: playing the old Shift 1 without ever touching ASHC-1's target, frequency sat at exactly 50.00 Hz for the whole shift while ASHC-1 silently climbed 30→44 MW on its own via droop alone — the scripted `TUTOR` beats (which key off `FREQUENCY_HZ < 49.9`) never fired, and the player was never asked to act. Shift 2 had the identical problem on both its units.
- **Fix — new `DROOP_ENABLED` per-shift flag**, mirroring `AGC_ENABLED`'s existing plumbing exactly rather than reusing `AGC_ENABLED` itself (the developer's explicit choice — droop and AGC are genuinely different control layers, and a shift may one day want one without the other). New `DROOP_ENABLED: bool = True` in `constants.py` (default **on** — opposite of `AGC_ENABLED`'s default-off — matching the standing "droop is universal plant behaviour by default" decision from Session 70; only a manual-dispatch tutorial needs to turn it off). `simulation.py`'s tick step 5a now reads `if _sim_const.DROOP_ENABLED:` before calling `apply_droop_response()`, exactly parallel to the existing AGC guard beneath it. Wired through the full stack: `gameplay/shifts/loader.py`'s `load_shift_config()` gained a `'droop_enabled'` key (`getattr(mod, 'DROOP_ENABLED', True)`); `main.py` propagates it to the runtime global at all three existing `AGC_ENABLED` assignment sites (`_make_sim_and_renderer`, the raw DesignerGrid preview path, and the Shift Builder JSON test path); `data/shift_io.py`'s full Shift Builder round-trip (`ShiftDefinition` dataclass, JSON save/load, `shift_def_to_config()`, the campaign dev-tool bridge's `CAMPAIGN_EDITABLE_FIELDS`/`_FIELD_TO_CONSTANT`/AST field-type map) got a parallel `droop_enabled`/`DROOP_ENABLED` entry at each of `agc_enabled`'s ~9 touch points, so Shift Builder can read/write `DROOP_ENABLED` in `shift_NN.py` source the same way it already does for `AGC_ENABLED`. No new keybinding — droop is shift-authored only, not runtime-togglable like AGC's Ctrl+A. Also added a "DROOP ON/OFF" fourth line to the existing `DEBUG_DISPLAY` overlay (`renderer.py`), next to "AGC ON/OFF".
- **Separately, merged Shift 1 and Shift 2 into one tutorial** (the user's decision, made while discussing the droop fix) — **supersedes a same-day recorded decision in `GAMEPLAY_ANALYSIS.md`'s Appendix** ("Shift 1 stays a pure single-lever observation tutorial"), noted there so it reads as a conscious change rather than an oversight. Scope was deliberately kept to content only, not a campaign renumbering: a side investigation found Shift 10 is currently a 9-line stub (not the built finale `STAGE_STATUS.md`'s older entries describe — gutted by commit `ae2ef49` when demand became grid-derived; already known to be pending a rebuild per `GAMEPLAY_ANALYSIS.md`), so the developer chose the smaller option — the merged tutorial becomes the new Shift 1, **Shift 2 becomes an empty stub** matching Shifts 6-10's existing placeholder pattern, and Shifts 3/4/5 keep their current numbers/files untouched. No changes to `Grid.__init__`'s shift-number bound, `menus.py`'s shift-select loop, or `main.py`'s campaign-length literals (all correctly still describe a 10-slot campaign).
- **Merged shift_01.py content**: `GRID_SOURCE` changed from `'shift1'` to `'shift2'` (the old Shift 2 grid is a strict superset — adds RIVE-1 and the OAKE load bus onto Shift 1's `RIVE→ASHC→GREY` chain — so no grid-file authoring was needed, and `shift1.json` is now simply unreferenced, not deleted). RIVE-1's `cold_start_min = 240.0` (4 real-game hours) makes a genuine mid-shift cold start infeasible for a tutorial, so RIVE-1 starts **ONLINE at its 105 MW minimum from handover** (developer's choice over building a new "release from maintenance" engine mechanic) rather than being started by the player; the second-unit lesson is taught by demand outgrowing what ASHC-1 alone can supply, not by RIVE-1's startup itself. `START_HOUR=4.0`, `DURATION_HOURS=6.0` (04:00-10:00, the old Shift 1's start through the old Shift 2's original start time), giving one continuous demand ramp (128→348 MW hour-by-hour, confirmed via the real `DEMAND_PROFILE_NORMALISED` curve) instead of two separately-shaped windows.
- **Scripted-event timing required an empirical fix mid-session**: the original design assumed ASHC-1 would need to cover the *full* load and hit its 250 MW ceiling around 07:00-08:00, so RIVE-1-introduction beats were placed at T+175/T+210. A headless trace of a realistic "track demand minus RIVE-1's 105 MW" player showed this was wrong — since RIVE-1 already covers a fixed 105 MW floor, ASHC-1 only needs to reach 243 MW even at the shift's 10:00 endpoint (348-105), so it never truly saturates within the shift, and the real "ASHC-1 output > 195 MW" crossing lands around T+250-285 (~08:14-08:43), not T+175-210. Retimed the two ceiling-warning beats to T+250/T+285 and the "two units now" reinforcement beat to T+290 to match; confirmed via headless run that a realistic player's alarms now fire exactly at those points and frequency stays controlled (49.9-50.0 Hz) throughout, while a do-nothing player (frequency never corrected) crashes to the 45 Hz floor by ~T+128 — confirming `DROOP_ENABLED=False` is genuinely restoring the "nothing corrects frequency but you" lesson.
- **`shift_02.py`** reduced to a stub matching `shift_06.py`-`shift_10.py`'s exact placeholder pattern (docstring-only, no constants — `load_shift_config()`'s `getattr(..., default)` fallbacks already handle this).
- **Collateral fix found during verification**: `tests/test_simulation.py::test_demand_model()` called `load_shift_config(2)` with a comment "shift 2 uses the GREY/OAKE load substations" — a direct dependency on the old Shift 2's grid content that broke once Shift 2 became a stub. Repointed to `load_shift_config(1)`, since Shift 1 is now the one that actually has GREY/OAKE content (via its new `shift2.json` grid source). No other test file depends on `shift_01.py`/`shift_02.py`'s scripted content (the two `test_voltage_reactive.py`/`test_designer_analysis.py` files that reference `shift_number=1` build their own synthetic `DesignerGrid` objects directly and pass explicit overrides — confirmed unaffected, still pass).
- **Verified**: `python tests/test_simulation.py` — 8/9 pass (the one failure, "Demand profile: LD01 morning load should be > 0," is pre-existing/unrelated, confirmed via `git stash` in Session 70). `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass. Headless droop-gating check: a do-nothing player on the merged Shift 1 (never touching ASHC-1/RIVE-1) now crashes to the 45 Hz floor, matching pre-Session-70 tutorial behaviour, instead of holding at nominal via silent droop. Headless playability check: a realistic player following the demand curve holds 49.9-50.0 Hz throughout, both ceiling-warning alarms and the "two units now" beat fire at their corrected trigger times, and the "approaching handover" reinforcement beat correctly does *not* fire when the player hasn't actually drifted (mirroring the old Shift 2's same condition-recheck pattern). Confirmed `load_shift_config(1)`/`load_shift_config(2)` return the expected `droop_enabled`/`agc_enabled`/schedule values via the real loader path.
- **Not done this session**: no in-game manual playtest (all verification was headless/programmatic, per this project's established pattern for sessions without a live window); the merged shift's exact scripted-event trigger times and thresholds are a first-pass fit to the demand-curve math, not yet played by a human.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`DROOP_ENABLED`), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (gate tick step 5a), `src/gameplay/shifts/loader.py` (`droop_enabled` config key + docstring), `src/main.py` (`DROOP_ENABLED` propagation at 3 sites), `src/data/shift_io.py` (`droop_enabled` at all ~9 `agc_enabled` touch points), `src/display/renderer.py` (DROOP debug overlay line), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_01.py` (full rewrite — merged tutorial), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_02.py` (reduced to a stub), `tests/test_simulation.py` (`test_demand_model()` repointed from shift 2 to shift 1).

### Session 70 (Gameplay Analysis Improvement #1 — frequency timescale, droop, AGC retune, overload countdown, voltage collapse softening, hour-boundary fix)

- **Context**: `GAMEPLAY_ANALYSIS.md` (a source-code-only review, added the previous session) identified that the game is currently unplayable rather than merely hard, because two timing constants make consequences arrive far faster than a human can react. Its top recommendation, "Improvement #1: put the player back on the clock," was implemented in full this session, in the order the analysis + a Plan-agent-authored implementation plan specified: 1a (foundational) first, then 1d/1e (independent constant retunes), then 1c (AGC), then 1b (droop, tuned against the now-working AGC), then 1f (independent).
- **Pre-existing history surfaced and resolved with the developer before writing any droop code**: git history (commits `f15ce25`/`fcbfa1f`, `STAGE_STATUS.md` Session 43/44) showed universal governor droop was already built once and deliberately reverted, because the developer concluded "the inertia of [COAL/NUCLEAR] is too big to respond in that way." The analysis document (source-only, unaware of this history) recommended re-adding droop narrowed to HYDRO/CCGT. Presented the developer with three options (narrow HYDRO/CCGT-only, no droop, or universal droop as originally built) — **the developer chose universal droop**, consciously overriding the Session-44 ruling. Architecture confirmed at the same time: droop lives in `FleetModel`/`UnitModel`, not inside `FrequencyModel` (keeps `FrequencyModel` a pure scalar swing equation and keeps `test_frequency_model()`'s existing "honest swing equation, no phantom droop" assertion valid unmodified).
- **1a — frequency/clock decoupling**: `frequency.py:123`'s `self._frequency_hz += (df_dt * dt_sim_seconds) / TIME_COMPRESSION` exactly cancelled the `TIME_COMPRESSION` (48x) baked into `dt_sim_seconds` upstream (`main.py:1023`'s `sim.tick(sim_accum * TIME_COMPRESSION * speed)`), leaving frequency the only subsystem running at real-time × speed instead of compressed sim-time. Replaced with an explicit `df_dt * dt_sim_seconds * FREQ_DYNAMICS_SCALE` (new tunable constant, deliberately scales *with* `speed` like every other subsystem — no separate speed-independence mechanism). Fixed `frequency.py`'s stale module/class docstrings, which claimed droop was computed inside `FrequencyModel` (it never was — "Governor/droop response is handled externally" was the actual code comment right next to the false docstring claim).
- **1b — universal governor droop reinstated**: `UnitModel.apply_droop_delta(delta_mw)` (immediate, non-ramp-limited MW nudge, clamped to `[min_mw, effective_max_mw]`, re-anchors `target_mw` to match so the next tick's ramp-limited path doesn't fight it) and `FleetModel.apply_droop_response(delta_f_hz)` (applies `ΔP = -(Δf/F_NOMINAL) × (1/DROOP_R) × rated_mw` to every ONLINE non-renewable unit — COAL/NUCLEAR/HYDRO/HYDRO_ROR/HYDRO_PUMP/CCGT) reintroduced in `units.py`, matching the original Session-43 shape. New tick step 5a in `simulation.py` (`self._fleet.apply_droop_response(delta_f)`, between the frequency update and AGC), restoring the module docstring's `frequency+droop+AGC` pipeline summary.
- **1c — AGC retuned into a working PI controller**: `AGC_KI` 0.01→5.0, `AGC_INTEGRAL_MAX` 5.0→60.0 (integral term was numerically capped at 0.05 MW — effectively dead, so AGC parked at a P-term-only offset instead of returning frequency to nominal), `AGC_KD` 1000.0→200.0 (cut so the now-much-stronger integral doesn't oscillate against an unchanged large derivative term). New `agc_saturated: bool` field on `SimulationState`, set in `_apply_agc()` when `apply_agc_signal()` returns no assignments despite a non-zero requested correction, edge-triggered (fires a WARNING alarm once on the transition into saturation, matching the existing alarm-transition pattern elsewhere in the file) rather than every tick. `draw_power_panel()` (`panels.py`) shows a `SAT` marker + critical-colour override on the REG NOW row when saturated.
- **1d — overload trip timer rescaled + live countdown**: `TRIP_DELAY_S` 60.0→720.0 sim-seconds (was ~1.25 real seconds before a sustained 100%+ overload tripped a line — unreadable; now 720/48=15 real seconds at 1x, mid-point of the developer's confirmed 10-20s reaction-window target). Fixed the alarm text's hardcoded `"Protection trips in 60s"` literal (now derived from `TRIP_DELAY_S`/`TIME_COMPRESSION` via a new module-level `_TRIP_DELAY_REAL_S`). New `GridCanvas.draw_overload_countdowns()` — a live, deliberately uncached top-layer render pass (the canvas's dirty-key cache quantizes loading% specifically to cap redraw rate, which would make a live per-second countdown stutter), drawing "Ns" at the midpoint of any line currently accumulating overload time, reading the already-exposed `state.overload_timers` field (needed zero new simulation-side plumbing). Wired into `renderer.py` alongside the existing `draw_load_triangles` uncached pass.
- **1e — voltage collapse cliff softened**: `V_COLLAPSE_GAIN` 2.0→0.01 (~200x reduction) and `V_COLLAPSE_RECOVERY_PU_S` 0.02→0.00035, retuned via a standalone offset-ODE simulation to hit ~15 real seconds warning-to-floor at a representative sustained 0.80pu bus (was capable of flooring a bus in a single tick at 1x) with recovery taking ~3x the decay time (was ~44x). Per-bus numeric "time to collapse" instrumentation was explicitly scoped out in favour of the existing VSI-tier halo escalation (the collapse rate is nonlinear/accelerating, so an honest countdown would need its own extrapolation math not worth building this session).
- **1f — hour-boundary AGC snap-back fixed**: `FleetModel.apply_hourly_schedule()` previously overwrote an AUTO unit's `target_mw` unconditionally at each Phase-1-plan hour boundary, discarding whatever AGC had accumulated into it over the preceding hour in one step (AGC uses the same `_set_target_internal()` path and never flips a unit out of AUTO). New `UnitModel._agc_offset_mw` running total, accumulated by a new `_apply_agc_delta(share_mw)` helper (folds the target-set + offset bookkeeping into one call, used by `apply_agc_signal()`), applied on top of the plan value at the hour boundary (`hours[hour] + model._agc_offset_mw`) instead of replacing it. Reset to 0 at the three points where a unit's target is otherwise forcibly reset in a way that shouldn't carry an implicit AGC memory forward: `trip()`, `set_target()` (a direct player command asserts a new baseline), and the STARTING→ONLINE transition.
- **Verified**: `python tests/test_simulation.py` — 8/9 pass (the one failure, "Demand profile: FAIL — LD01 morning load should be > 0," is confirmed pre-existing via `git stash` and unrelated to this session). Two `test_frequency_model()` subtests needed their `dt_sim_seconds` raised from a bare `1.0` to `1.0 * TIME_COMPRESSION` (the test was implicitly assuming the pre-1a semantics where `dt_sim_seconds=1.0` meant "1 real second" — under 1a's new `FREQ_DYNAMICS_SCALE`, a genuinely tiny tick fragment produces a genuinely tiny frequency delta, which is correct new behaviour, not a regression) — no other existing test needed modification, confirming the droop-outside-`FrequencyModel` architecture choice paid off exactly as intended. Built `scripts/verify_reaction_window.py` (a new headless tuning harness, driving a real `GridSimulation` exactly as `main.py`'s loop does) and used it to retune `FREQ_DYNAMICS_SCALE` from an initial analytical estimate (0.00075 — far too forgiving once universal droop was actually active; a full 250 MW unit trip on a ~500 MW fleet never left the alert band) to an empirically-checked 0.02. The harness confirmed `TRIP_DELAY_S`'s derivation exactly (15.1 measured real seconds vs. 15.0 predicted) and the voltage-collapse retune closely (21.6s to floor, ~65s to recover, ≈3:1). Ran a full real Shift 3 (Designer-grid, real `RIVE-2`/`RIVE-1`/`ASHC-1` fleet, real hourly schedule + scripted events) for 48 simulated hours with no exceptions; a separate real-scripted-event run confirmed the existing L01/L09 N-1 lesson (Shift 3's best moment, per the analysis doc) still fires and behaves correctly under the new physics — a player who never closes L09 still experiences the intended total blackout of Ashcombe and everything behind it, now via the retuned droop/AGC rather than the old physics. Noted a small (~0.02 Hz), bounded, non-diverging droop/AGC hunting oscillation around nominal on that same run before the scripted event — flagged for playtest attention, not fixed this session (the plan's own PID-retuning guidance anticipated this as a possible outcome of raising `AGC_KI`/lowering `AGC_KD` together and called it out as something to verify empirically rather than assume from arithmetic).
- **Not done this session (flagged, deliberately deferred)**: real in-game/manual playtest confirmation of the final constant values (all six are explicitly playtest-candidates, not locked); further PID/droop oscillation tuning beyond confirming it's bounded; `test_agc_pid_response()`/`test_droop_response()` coverage additions the plan recommended (git commit `f15ce25` had a droop test that could be resurrected as a starting point) — the existing 28-test suite (minus the pre-existing unrelated failure) passes unmodified, but AGC/droop have no dedicated automated regression coverage yet.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`FREQ_DYNAMICS_SCALE` new, `DROOP_R` comment updated, `AGC_KI`/`AGC_KD`/`AGC_INTEGRAL_MAX`, `TRIP_DELAY_S`, `V_COLLAPSE_GAIN`/`V_COLLAPSE_RECOVERY_PU_S`), `src/simulation/frequency.py` (docstrings, `update()`'s core line), `src/simulation/units.py` (`_agc_offset_mw`, `_apply_agc_delta()`, `apply_droop_delta()`, `apply_droop_response()`, `apply_agc_signal()`/`apply_hourly_schedule()` updated, reset points in `trip()`/`set_target()`/`_tick_starting()`), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (docstrings, tick step 5a, `agc_saturated` field/state/alarm, `_TRIP_DELAY_REAL_S`, alarm text fix), `src/display/canvas.py` (`draw_overload_countdowns()`), `src/display/renderer.py` (call site), `src/display/panels.py` (`agc_saturated` SAT marker), `tests/test_simulation.py` (two subtests' `dt_sim_seconds`).
- Created: `scripts/verify_reaction_window.py` (headless tuning harness, not part of the pytest suite).

### Session 69 (AUTO/MANUAL canvas visibility + Shift 5 FENN/YEWB reactive fix)

- **Context**: reviewing Shift 5 turned up two gaps. (1) The AUTO/MANUAL dispatch mode (Session 66) only showed in the unit context panel — a player watching the grid without clicking a unit had no way to tell which units were following the plan and which had been taken manual. (2) FENN and YEWB sag into WATCH (~0.86-0.88 pu) mid-shift with no way to fix it — `shift_05.py` declared no `SUBSTATION_TYPES` at all, so `seed_default_reactive_devices()` never ran and there were zero shunt banks/SVCs anywhere on the grid. Confirmed `shift5.json` itself already saves `substation_type: INDUSTRIAL` for FENN (and STAV/FARL) and `RESIDENTIAL` for THOR — the grid was evidently authored with reactive tuning in mind but `shift_05.py` never picked it up; an oversight, not intended difficulty.
- **FENN/YEWB fix**: confirmed FENN and YEWB are electrical siblings, not up/downstream of each other — both single-radial dead ends off MARC (`SUTT→{L29,L30}→MARC→L03→FENN`, `MARC→L31→YEWB`) — so each needs its own device. Also confirmed the engine's single auto-SVC placement rule (`sorted(load_bus_labels - gen_bus_labels)[0]`) would land at **APPL** for this grid, not FENN/YEWB, since all 10 of Shift 5's load buses have no co-located generation and APPL is alphabetically first — the auto-SVC can't be steered to fix this specific sag. Added `SUBSTATION_TYPES` to `shift_05.py`: FENN and YEWB both `INDUSTRIAL` (YEWB reclassified from shift5.json's saved `MIXED`, confirmed with the developer this was never deliberately tuned), STAV/FARL/THOR matching the grid file's saved types, rest `MIXED`. Verified empirically — no `SHUNT_BANK_OVERRIDES` needed, the default automatic-shunt-bank sizing alone holds both buses HEALTHY unattended for the full 240-minute shift.
- **AUTO/MANUAL canvas visibility**: `draw_unit_square()` (`display/symbols.py`) gained an optional `dispatch_mode` param — draws a small top-right corner dot (green `COL_UNIT_ONLINE` for AUTO, amber `COL_ALARM_WARN` for MANUAL, reusing the exact colours from the Session 66 context-panel Mode row) when the shift has a Phase 1 schedule covering the unit; draws nothing otherwise (Shifts 1-4 unaffected). New `UNIT_MODE_BADGE_RADIUS_PX` constant. `canvas.py`'s `_redraw_to()` builds a new `unit_modes` lookup dict mirroring the existing `unit_states`/`unit_outputs` pattern, threaded into the `draw_unit_square()` call site. Critically, `_build_canvas_key()`'s dirty-key cache tuple gained a `unit_mode_sig` entry (compact per-unit character string, only over units covered by `unit_has_schedule`) — without this the canvas would silently keep showing a stale badge after AUTO→MANUAL, since the redraw is cache-gated on this tuple. `draw_dispatch_panel()` (`display/panels.py`) got the same treatment at smaller scale — a small colour dot next to the state abbreviation column (the panel's columns are already tightly packed; a dot fit better than a new text column), extending the per-unit tuple built at the top of the function and `_FALLBACK_UNITS`'s shape to match.
- **Verified**: `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass. Headless run of the real `shift_05.py` (via `load_shift_config(5)` → `GridSimulation` with `substation_types` → `seed_default_reactive_devices()`, the actual `main.py` path) — FENN and YEWB both reach and hold HEALTHY through 240 simulated minutes. Pixel-level check of `draw_unit_square()`'s badge — exact colour match for AUTO/MANUAL/None cases at the expected corner offset. Canvas dirty-key cache check — built a `GridCanvas`, drew a frame, flipped one unit's `dispatch_mode` in the state, drew again, confirmed the rendered bytes actually changed (this is the failure mode most likely to go unnoticed — a cache that doesn't invalidate on mode change would silently show a stale badge). `draw_dispatch_panel()` render-checked with both a real state and `state=None` (fallback path).
- Edited: `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_05.py` (`SUBSTATION_TYPES`, narrative paragraph), `src/simulation/constants.py` (`UNIT_MODE_BADGE_RADIUS_PX`), `src/display/symbols.py` (`draw_unit_square()` `dispatch_mode` param + badge, new palette imports), `src/display/canvas.py` (`unit_modes` lookup, call-site wiring, `unit_mode_sig` in `_build_canvas_key()`), `src/display/panels.py` (`draw_dispatch_panel()` mode dot, `_FALLBACK_UNITS` shape).

### Session 68 (Debug logs — guaranteed fresh per shift run)

- **Context**: investigating Shift 5 via `logs/sim_state.csv` turned up stale data — the file's bus/unit columns referenced STAG-1/STAG-2/LOVE-1/LOVE-2/STAG bus, retired Shift 4 labels from before the Session 65 Batherton redesign. Root cause: both `logs/agc_log.csv` and `logs/sim_state.csv` were only opened/truncated lazily, on their first per-tick write (`_write_agc_log()`/`_write_sim_state_log()`) — if that first-write condition never occurred in a run (AGC never fires because AGC is off all shift, or `SIM_STATE_LOG` is off), the *previous* run's file was left completely untouched, with nothing marking it as not belonging to the current shift.
- **Fix**: `GridSimulation.__init__` now calls a new `_reset_log_files()` unconditionally, right after the initial `_solve_and_snapshot()` — it truncates and writes a fresh header to both files on every shift construction, regardless of whether `AGC_LOG`/`SIM_STATE_LOG` end up writing any data rows this run. Extracted `_open_agc_log(unit_labels)` and `_open_sim_state_log()` header-writing helpers out of the existing lazy-write methods (`_write_agc_log`/`_write_sim_state_log`, now call the helper if the file isn't open yet) so there's one code path for "start a fresh file" either way. The AGC log's header is now built from every AGC-eligible unit in the grid (`AGC_UNIT_TYPES` filter over `grid.get_active_units()`) rather than whichever units happened to be online at the first real AGC tick — incidentally fixes a second staleness bug where a unit that came online after the first AGC tick was silently missing its own column. `_write_agc_log` also now detects a unit outside its header's coverage (e.g. became AGC-eligible mid-shift after the header was fixed) and reopens with the full set rather than dropping it.
- Also flipped `SIM_STATE_LOG`'s default `False → True` (developer decision) — `sim_state.csv` is now populated with real per-tick data on every run by default, matching `AGC_LOG`'s existing `True` default, rather than requiring a manual flag flip to get anything beyond a header.
- **Verified**: headless run of Shift 5 (50 ticks) — both logs freshly written, 51/50 rows respectively, header and data reference the current fleet (RIVE-1/2/3, BATH-1/2, PIKE-1/2, SHEL-1/2, ASHC-1/2/3, SUTT-1) and full 24-bus topology, no STAG/LOVE remnants. Headless run of Shift 1 (AGC mostly idle) confirmed `agc_log.csv` still resets to a small, current-run row count rather than retaining a stale prior-run row count. `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (`SIM_STATE_LOG` default), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (`_reset_log_files()`, `_open_agc_log()`, `_open_sim_state_log()`, `AGC_UNIT_TYPES` import, call site in `__init__`).

### Session 67 (Planning-screen confirm gate — asymmetric reserve-margin rule)

- **Context**: the F10 confirm gate (`out_of_tolerance_hours()`, added Session 50) blocked confirmation symmetrically — any hour more than ±10% away from forecast load, whether over- or under-generating. The developer wanted this asymmetric: a minimum spinning-reserve margin is required to confirm at all, but there's no harm in scheduling extra reserve beyond a point, just a soft warning.
- **New rule**: generation < load × 1.05 (less than +5% margin) **blocks** confirmation (red status, unchanged UX from before). Generation in [load × 1.05, load × 1.20] confirms silently. Generation > load × 1.20 (more than +20% margin) **confirms**, but shows a **yellow** warning.
- **UX issue found and resolved during implementation**: `on_plan_complete()` switches `GameState` away from the Planning screen in the same call that would show a warning toast — the toast would be set and then instantly discarded before a player could see it. Fixed with a two-step confirm: the first F10 that trips the over-generation warning shows the yellow status and sets `_pending_confirm = True`, staying on the Planning screen; a second F10 press proceeds to `on_plan_complete()`. Any edit to the plan (cell edit, online toggle, TECH MIN/MAX/ZERO fill, RESET, Ctrl+A auto-schedule) clears `_pending_confirm`, so a stale "press again" can't wave through a plan the player has since changed — covered at the three plan-mutation entry points (`PlanningScreen.on_key()`'s non-CONFIRM branch, `_handle_action()` for mouse-driven toggles/cell-starts, `_commit_edit()` for committed cell edits).
- `PlanningModel.out_of_tolerance_hours()` replaced by two methods: `hours_below_min_reserve(frac=PLANNING_MIN_RESERVE_MARGIN_FRAC)` (the blocking check, `gen(h) < load(h) * (1+frac)`) and `hours_above_warn_reserve(frac=PLANNING_WARN_RESERVE_MARGIN_FRAC)` (the warning check, `gen(h) > load(h) * (1+frac)`) — both new `constants.py` fractions (0.05, 0.20) replacing the old single `PLANNING_LOAD_TOLERANCE_FRAC` (0.10).
- **Verified**: headless boundary checks at exactly 0%, +3%, +5.0%, +12%, +20.0%, +25% margin confirm the `<`/`>` (not `<=`/`>=`) boundaries land exactly where specified — +5.0% and +20.0% themselves are clean accepts, not block/warn. Headless `PlanningScreen._confirm_plan()` drive via synthetic F10 `KEYDOWN` events across all four cases (block, clean accept, warn-then-second-F10-confirms, warn-then-edit-clears-pending) — all behave as designed. `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged (no test referenced the old method/constant).

This is a separate follow-up, not bundled into this session.

### Session 66 (Shift 5 — Phase 1 planning introduced to the campaign, AUTO/MANUAL dispatch mode)

- **Context**: Phase 1 (`gameplay/phase1.py`, `display/planning.py`/`planning_panels.py`) has existed since Session 49 but was never reachable from the campaign — `GameState.PLANNING` had no entry point in `main.py`'s menu flow at all (the Session 49-era "debug redirect to Shift 10" no longer exists in the current `main.py`; `DIFFICULTY_SELECT`/`SHIFT_SELECT` both go straight `BRIEFING → PLAYING`). Shift 5 (the last tutorial shift before the non-tutorial Shifts 6-10) was always the intended point to introduce it, per the original roadmap. The developer also asked for a per-unit AUTO/MANUAL dispatch mode: a unit seeded from a plan follows it automatically; touching its MW target by hand takes manual control; a button returns it to AUTO.
- **Design decisions confirmed with the developer before implementation**: (1) Shift 5's real-time lesson is a mid-shift demand spike beyond what the Phase 1 forecast showed, not the old roadmap's interconnector-trip idea (no interconnectors exist in the current topology). (2) Plan validation stays MW-balance-only (existing `out_of_tolerance_hours()`) — no DC-load-flow-in-planning work this session. (3) AUTO/MANUAL is a core `UnitModel`/`FleetModel` feature, not Shift-5-only, but only meaningful when a shift actually has an hourly schedule to follow — Shifts 1-4 are unaffected. (4) Build the per-hour executor now (required for AUTO to mean anything past minute zero). (5) AGC keeps nudging AUTO units for regulation exactly as it does MANUAL ones — dispatch mode only determines the baseline target, not AGC eligibility.
- **`simulation/units.py`**: `UnitModel` gained `_dispatch_mode` (`'MANUAL'`/`'AUTO'`, default MANUAL) + read-only `dispatch_mode` property. `set_target()` (the existing player-dispatch entry point) now also sets MANUAL; a new private `_set_target_internal()` (no mode side effect) holds the actual clamping logic that `set_target()`, `apply_agc_signal()`, and the new `apply_hourly_schedule()` all route through — so AGC's regulation nudges never flip a unit's mode. New `set_auto_mode()` (guarded ONLINE-only, mirrors `set_target`/`set_q_target`'s guard pattern) and `FleetModel.apply_hourly_schedule(hour, hourly_schedule)` (advances every ONLINE+AUTO unit's target to its planned MW for `hour`; MANUAL and OFFLINE units untouched). `trip()` also resets a unit to MANUAL (a tripped/restarted unit shouldn't silently resume following a stale schedule unannounced). `FleetModel` gained `set_unit_auto_mode()`/`get_unit_dispatch_mode()` pass-throughs and `get_state_snapshot()` now includes `dispatch_mode` per unit.
- **`simulation/simulation.py`**: `GridSimulation.__init__` now seeds every unit named in `hourly_schedule` to AUTO right after `FleetModel` construction (units from a plan start AUTO; shifts with no `hourly_schedule` — Shifts 1-4 — are a no-op, zero behaviour change). New `_apply_hourly_schedule(sim_hour)` private method, called each `tick()` right after the time-advance step (before demand/renewables update): detects when `int(sim_hour) % 24` crosses an integer-hour boundary via `self._last_dispatch_hour`, then calls `fleet.apply_hourly_schedule()`. New public `set_unit_auto_mode()`/`get_unit_dispatch_mode()`/`has_hourly_schedule()` pass-throughs on `GridSimulation` itself. New `DEMAND_OVERRIDE` branch in `_execute_action()` (`{'type': 'DEMAND_OVERRIDE', 'schedule': {hour: mw}}` → `self._demand.set_demand_override(schedule, sim_hour)`, the latter method already existed from an earlier session but had no scripted-event action wired to it) — documented in `data/shift_io.py`'s action-schema docstring alongside `LINE_OPEN`/`UNIT_TRIP`/`UNIT_DERATE`. `SimulationState` gained `unit_dispatch_modes: dict` and `unit_has_schedule: frozenset` (which units this shift's plan covers, for the context panel to know when to show the Mode row at all).
- **`gameplay/shifts/loader.py`**: `load_shift_config()` gained `'uses_planning': getattr(mod, 'USES_PLANNING', False)`.
- **`main.py`**: `BRIEFING`'s completion handler (previously an unconditional `game_state = GameState.PLAYING`) now checks `load_shift_config(shift).get('uses_planning')` — if true, builds `_planning_model = build_planning_model(shift)` and goes to `GameState.PLANNING` instead. The existing (previously-orphaned) `PLANNING` state's `on_plan_complete` callback needed no changes — it already built the real-time sim correctly via `_make_sim_and_renderer(..., planning_model=model)`. `sim`/`grid`/`renderer` are still built once (thrown away) before `BRIEFING` for a planning shift, same as any shift — a deliberate surgical choice over deferring construction, since `BRIEFING`'s typewriter render depends on the persistent `renderer` object existing. New `M` key routed to `renderer.on_toggle_auto_mode(sim)` in both PLAYING key handlers (the main campaign one and the Designer's `DESIGNER_TEST` one), mirroring the existing `S`/`X` start/stop routing exactly (no separate focus-then-confirm step — `_cmd_active`-style focus flags in this codebase are decorative, S/X/M all fire immediately on keypress).
- **`display/context.py`**: `draw_unit_context()` gained `dispatch_mode`/`mode_cmd_active` params and a new Mode row (only drawn when dispatchable, non-renewable, and `dispatch_mode is not None` — i.e. the shift's plan actually covers this unit), via new `_draw_mode_row()` helper mirroring the existing `_draw_cmd_row()` START/STOP button treatment. `n_rows` bumped accordingly. Colours reused from the existing palette (`COL_UNIT_ONLINE` green for AUTO, `COL_ALARM_WARN` amber for MANUAL) — no new palette entries needed.
- **`display/renderer.py`**: new `_mode_cmd_active` field (reset in `clear_selection()`) and `on_toggle_auto_mode(sim)` (no-op unless the unit is ONLINE, `sim.has_hourly_schedule(label)` is true, and it's currently MANUAL — going MANUAL happens implicitly via the Target field, not this button). The `draw_unit_context()` call site now passes `dispatch_mode=state.unit_dispatch_modes.get(label) if label in state.unit_has_schedule else None`.
- **`gameplay/shifts/shift_05.py`**: replaced the placeholder stub with a full scenario — `GRID_SOURCE='shift5'`, `USES_PLANNING=True`, 07:00-11:00 played window (the steepest morning ramp: forecast climbs ~893→1370 MW; system peak 1540 MW), `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` seeding RIVE-1/2/BATH-1/2/PIKE-1/2/SHEL-1/ASHC-1/2, RIVE-3 on `MAINTENANCE_UNITS` (visible spare, but its 240-min cold start is longer than the shift — deliberately not a mid-shift lever). Narrative: the player builds their first hourly plan, confirms it (F10), every scheduled unit starts AUTO; a `TUTOR` event at t=0 explains the AUTO/MANUAL/M-key mechanic, another at t=5 explains that the wind unit (SUTT-1) is forecast-only and never player-scheduled. At t=90 a `DEMAND_OVERRIDE` action steps live demand to roughly 12% above what the Phase 1 forecast showed for that hour, holding through the rest of the shift — a plan with hydro headroom held back absorbs it via reserve; a tightly-forecast-tracking plan (e.g. the existing `auto_schedule()` heuristic's own output on this fleet, which happens to fill COAL/CCGT before hydro and leaves ~55 MW reserve) runs into a genuine frequency/reserve crisis, with escalating WARNING (t=95, reserve<250)/CRITICAL (t=110 reserve<200, t=115 freq<49.85) events and a TUTOR-on-recovery once reserve clears 250 MW again — same escalating-alarm pattern as Shifts 3/4.
- **Grid-topology bug found and fixed by the developer mid-session, not this session's own work**: while stress-testing Shift 5 unattended, found `shift5.json` (as it stood before the developer's edit) collapsed FENN then YEWB to 0.0 pu by ~t=120min even with zero scripted events and the plain flat handover dispatch — confirmed via headless driver this was pre-existing, not caused by anything built this session. The developer added a second CLOV↔SUTT circuit (**L41**, parallel to the existing L18) and relocated the shift's wind unit from Marchden (**MARC-1**, removed) to Sutterleigh (**SUTT-1**, 100 MW) directly on the now-doubly-fed bus — strengthening the tie that Marchden/Fenshaw/Yewbarrow hang off. Re-verified after the fix: unattended with a well-balanced hand-authored 24h schedule (hydro held back with real headroom rather than dispatched to track forecast 1:1 — the existing `auto_schedule()` heuristic alone was confirmed *not* suitable as this shift's "good plan" example, since its COAL/CCGT-first fill order leaves hydro at floor with near-zero reserve on this specific fleet), voltage holds a stable 0.86-0.93 pu band the full 240 minutes with no collapse, and the demand spike is absorbed cleanly (reserve never drops below ~500 MW, none of the WARNING/CRITICAL reserve/frequency conditions fire) — confirming the intended "conservative plan sails through, tight plan doesn't" contrast. `shift_05.py`'s docstring grid diagram and unit list updated to match (41 lines, SUTT-1 replacing MARC-1 throughout, including the two TUTOR events that referenced the wind unit by name).
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged. (2) Headless: `build_planning_model(5)` loads real Shift 5 grid/forecast data (12 dispatchable units, 1 renewable, `load_forecast`/`renewable_total` match hand-computed values from `profiles.py`'s demand/wind curves). (3) Headless: `GridSimulation` with a `planning_model` — units seeded from the plan start AUTO; ticking past an hour boundary advances an AUTO unit's `target_mw` to the plan's value for that hour; a MANUAL unit is untouched by the same boundary; `set_target()` on an AUTO unit flips it to MANUAL; `set_unit_auto_mode()` returns it to AUTO. (4) Headless: `DEMAND_OVERRIDE` action fires via `_execute_action()` and `DemandModel`'s live demand jumps per the override schedule. (5) Headless: `PlanningScreen` constructed for Shift 5, ticked (no crash), driven with a synthetic F10 `KEYDOWN` event — `on_plan_complete` fires with the confirmed model once the schedule passes the ±10% gate (confirmed the gate genuinely blocks an unedited flat/`auto_schedule()`-only plan at several hours, and that a few manual per-hour nudges — the realistic player workflow — clear it for all 24 hours). (6) Headless: full unattended 240-minute run of Shift 5 with the balanced schedule and with the `auto_schedule()`-only schedule, tracing frequency/voltage/reserve trajectories and `active_alarms`, confirming the intended good-plan/bad-plan contrast. (7) Render check: `draw_unit_context()` with the new Mode row across AUTO/MANUAL/no-schedule states on an offscreen surface — no crash, all combinations draw.

### Session 65 (Shift 4 re-tuned onto Batherton — the AVR-setpoint voltage lesson finally works)

- **Context**: Session 64 stabilised the voltage solver (converging PV correction). This session re-tuned Shift 4 against it. Early analysis confirmed the old Stagshaw (STAG) design still couldn't teach the AVR-setpoint lesson even with the fixed solver — but now for a *physical* reason, not a solver bug: STAG (24 MVAr) was far too small to ever hold reactive reserve while Stavely drew ~100+ MVAr through the weak Hollowgate radial, so it pinned at PQ from tick 0 and its setpoint was a bit-for-bit no-op (verified: setpoint 0.95 vs 1.05 → identical STAV voltage). Testing the developer's proposed fixes (STAG on 400 kV; STAG anchored to RIVE) showed the fundamental tension: a generator either shares STAV's weak node (→ pinned, no reserve) or is anchored to the strong backbone (→ has reserve but its setpoint no longer reaches STAV — the boost leaks into the backbone). No middle ground on that topology.
- **The developer then restructured `shift4.json` directly** (mid-session), which resolved it: Stagshaw removed entirely; a new **Batherton (BATH-1)** 400 kV hydro station now supports Stavely through a **Batherton (BATH) → Nettlecross (NETT) → Hollowgate (HOLL) → Stavely** chain — strong enough to carry BATH's reactive support down to STAV, but with BATH close enough that its setpoint genuinely reaches STAV. On this grid the AVR-setpoint lesson works: at the low starting setpoint BATH absorbs reactive power and STAV sags; raising the setpoint flips BATH to injecting and lifts STAV. (New buses BATH/NETT and generator BATH-1 were the developer's grid edits; this session tuned their values and rewrote the shift file.)
- **Tuning (all empirical, headless driver + `SIM_STATE_LOG`, against the real loader/grid — never hand-calculated)**:
  - `shift4.json`: **BATH-1 `q_max_mvar` 120→300, `q_min_mvar` -40→-100** (BATH needs real reserve headroom at the low setpoint for the setpoint raise to have somewhere to go; at 120 it pinned like STAG did). **STAV `peak_load_mw` 300→240** — at ≥260 MW the fixed AVR setpoint produces a *bistable self-heal*: BATH's automatic PV correction flips from its absorbing rail to its injecting rail as demand crosses a threshold near the peak, so STAV would recover unattended mid-shift with no player action (undercutting "act early"). At 240 MW that flip never triggers within the window, so STAV holds a stable WATCH until the player acts.
  - `shift_04.py` `SHUNT_BANK_OVERRIDES`: FENN's capped shunt **25→40 MVAr/step** — at STAV=240 the network redistribution left FENN collapsing to 0.0 pu (full blackout) at the peak if its SVC was ignored; 40 MVAr keeps FENN in a recoverable WATCH while still leaving a real gap only the manual SVC can close (the single-step cap is unchanged, so the SVC is still genuinely needed).
- **`shift_04.py` rewrite**: `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` drops STAG-1 (was 60 MW, with STAG-2 commented out) and adds `BATH-1: 200.0`; `INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS` is now `{'BATH-1': 0.95}`; `HANDOVER_NOTES` references Batherton (removing the two stale STAG lines, one of which was the long-flagged stale STAG-2 claim); docstring narrative, grid diagram, and teaching-goal section rewritten around Batherton/NETT/HOLL; the two-acts intro reframed (STAV = a single decisive setpoint action that holds through the peak; FENN = the genuine "revisit the device" second act). The three STAV Act-2 scripted events, previously written around Stagshaw's Q-exhaustion (PV→PQ) — which no longer occurs, since BATH-1 has ample reserve — were rewritten to simply catch a player who never raised BATH's setpoint. All STAV/STAG event `element`/text references updated to BATH-1; zero STAG/Stagshaw references remain in the file.
- **Verified**: (1) Real loader config (`load_shift_config(4)`) reads the intended dispatch/setpoint/shunt overrides. (2) **Unattended 240-min run**: STAV and FENN both hold recoverable WATCH (~0.87-0.89) the whole shift, frequency nominal (~50 Hz), `min_voltage_seen` ≈ 0.866, no collapse, no self-heal; the escalating WARNING→CRITICAL "still sagging / setpoint not touched / raise the SVC" alarm cascade fires correctly for both buses. (3) **Fully-fixed run** (raise BATH-1 setpoint at T+10, FENN SVC at T+20 then T+130): both buses HEALTHY by T+30 and stable through the peak; only the TUTOR "…holding…" confirmations fire, none of the WARNING/CRITICAL "still sagging" alarms. (4) Fixing BATH-1 alone also rescues FENN partway (BATH's support spills toward FENN) — confirmed, matching the shared-network framing. (5) Builds, ticks, and renders a frame through the real `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer(shift=4)` path (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`): BATH-1 ONLINE at setpoint 0.95, STAV/FENN in WATCH, FENN carries the capped shunt + SVC. (6) `python -m pytest tests/` — 28/28 pass, unchanged.
- **Not done this session**: no live (non-headless) fullscreen playtest (matches established precedent); the BATH-1 `q_max_mvar=300` is a hand-tuned value above the Grid Designer's LARGE-hydro preset (120) — intentional, the sim reads `q_max_mvar` directly, but it means BATH-1 is not a "pure" Designer-preset unit; the incidental T+0 startup frequency transient and the L03/L05 85% line-loading WARNINGs are pre-existing grid-design behaviour, not introduced or addressed here.
- Edited: `src/assets/designer_grids/shift4.json` (STAV peak 240, BATH-1 q_max/q_min), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_04.py` (dispatch/setpoints/shunt override, docstring, HANDOVER_NOTES, all STAV/STAG scripted events → BATH-1).

### Session 64 (Voltage/reactive model simplification: converging PV correction + retired dead auto-taps)

- **User request**: after three failed tuning passes and a full investigation (`SHIFT4_VOLTAGE_INVESTIGATION.md`), the developer asked whether the voltage/reactive simulation was "too much" for a game — could some mechanics be simplified so the grid still *feels* real ("look like it, not be like it") without the solver's brittle edges. Exploration confirmed the investigation's finding: of the six pieces in the voltage path, exactly one is fragile — the single-pass PV→PQ correction in `voltage.py` — and every investigation failure traces to it. Developer chose (via a scoped decision) two changes: **Lever A** (make the PV correction iterate to convergence, keeping all player-facing mechanics) and **Lever C** (retire the automatic transformer taps). Explicitly declined: a larger rewrite replacing Q-regulation with a direct voltage-boost model (kept as a fallback if A proved insufficient — it did not).
- **Lever A — converging PV correction** (`src/simulation/voltage.py`): the correction block (`solve()`) was a single linear pass — adequate only when every PV bus has one dominant electrical path. It (1) never let a satisfied PV bus settle below its Q ceiling (it re-chased the same `delta_q = b_diag × v_error` every tick, pinning any active PV bus to `q_max` so `q_reserve` was always ~0, and a generator's AVR setpoint became a no-op once pinned), and (2) diverged into an oscillating, off-scale voltage on any bus with two comparable paths (the investigation's §5 CLOV-tie self-blackout). Replaced with a bounded fixed-point iteration: solve ΔV → adjust each PV bus's Q toward target → clamp to `[q_min, q_max]` → re-solve, looped. Correctness details: Q **accumulates** across passes (threaded `q_injections_working`); PV→PQ is **sticky** within a solve (a bus in `pq_conversions` is skipped for the rest of the iteration — its Q fixed at the limit, matching the physical meaning); convergence tests the **per-pass max |ΔQ|** (break below `PV_CORRECTION_Q_TOL_MVAR`); a hard `PV_CORRECTION_MAX_ITERS` cap guarantees the solve can never hang a tick; slack/non-PV guards unchanged. `solve()` stays a pure function. New constants `PV_CORRECTION_MAX_ITERS = 8` / `PV_CORRECTION_Q_TOL_MVAR = 0.5` (`constants.py`, voltage block, Rule 1). This cashes in the fallback the original `VOLTAGE_REACTIVE_PLAN.md` decision #2 explicitly pre-authorised ("2nd pass only if violated" — the investigation is that violation).
- **Lever C — retired automatic transformer taps** (dead code, zero behaviour change): confirmed nothing instantiates a `TransformerTap` at runtime (`seed_default_reactive_devices` seeds only shunt banks + one SVC; `add_tap`/`TransformerTap` had no live callers in any shift, seed, or test), so `state.transformer_taps` was always `{}` and its display branches never fired. Removed: the `TransformerTap` dataclass + all tap plumbing in `ReactiveDevices` (`_taps`, `add_tap`, the tap loop in `step_automatics`, `get_tap_state`, `tap_delta_v`, the `tap_q_mvar` arg to `q_injections()`); the tap Q-conversion in `simulation.py::_build_q_injections` and `_build_state`; the `transformer_taps` field from `SimulationState`; the now-unused `S_BASE` import in `simulation.py`; the `TAP_*` constant block (`constants.py`); the tap display in `context.py` (row + row-count), `canvas.py` (dirty-sig entry, local, draw loop, import), `symbols.py` (`draw_tap_glyph` + `COL_TAP` import), `palette.py` (`COL_TAP`); and the now-unused `VoltageModel.b_diag()` accessor (its only consumer was the tap conversion). Trimmed tap references from `SIMULATION_API.md`, `DOMAIN_GLOSSARY.md` (removed the tap-changer glossary entry; rewrote the "Does NOT Simulate" line to state taps are not modelled at all), and `GRID_SIMULATION_MECHANICS.md` (device table, auto-regulator section, campaign-progression sketch). Fixed `scripts/render_voltage_verify.py` (removed its `TransformerTap` use).
- **Not changed (conscious keeps)**: the decoupled `ΔV = B'⁻¹Q` solve, `VSHUNT_REG`, the stateful collapse-acceleration overlay (`_apply_collapse_acceleration` — bounded, self-healing, the cheap "region goes dark if mismanaged" drama), automatic shunt banks, the manual SVC, per-substation-type power factor. `shift4.json`/`shift_04.py` deliberately untouched — Shift 4 re-tuning against the now-stable solver is the immediate follow-up, done empirically with the investigation's headless driver, not this session.
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m pytest tests/` — **28/28 pass** (was 27: removed the now-dead `test_auto_tap_holds_near_nominal`, added two PV-convergence regression tests). New tests exercise `VoltageModel.solve()` directly on a synthetic §5 two-path topology: `test_pv_bus_satisfied_keeps_reserve` asserts a non-exhausted PV bus settles at a Q strictly inside its limits (real reserve) **and** that Q is a stable fixed point (re-solving with the settled Q as a plain injection reproduces the voltage — the true "converged" guarantee the single pass could never give); `test_pv_correction_bounded_on_two_path_bus` asserts finite, physically-bounded voltages across a setpoint sweep on the arrangement that used to diverge. (2) Ran the tests directly (`python tests/test_voltage_reactive.py`, the real True/False gate — the module's tests `return` rather than `assert`, so pytest counts them passed regardless) — 13/13 in that module. (3) **Go/no-go against the investigation's own headless driver + real `shift4.json`**: the previously-unstable STAG→CLOV tie (`NEW_LINE` override, the §5 configuration that self-blacked-out STAV to 0.0 pu by minute 30-80 regardless of player action) now holds a **stable WATCH band (0.87-0.95) the entire 240-min shift** — the single-pass divergence is gone. (4) **Shift 1-3 regression** via the established `regression_shifts123.py` harness — frequency nominal (50.008/50.011/49.990 Hz), no reactive devices seeded, voltages sensibly non-flat — unaffected as predicted (these shifts have no PV-correcting stress and no taps). (5) `python -m py_compile` on all edited files — clean. (6) **Render smoke through the full `Renderer` path** (`scripts/render_voltage_verify.py`, `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy` → PNG): a WEAK bus with a shunt bank + SVC renders its context panel with Voltage/V/VSI/Q/Load/**Shunt (auto)**/**SVC** rows and **no Tap row**, plus the WARNING halo, shunt glyph, and SVC diamond on canvas — visually confirmed from the saved PNG; also confirmed `SimulationState` no longer carries a `transformer_taps` attribute.
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (PV_CORRECTION constants, removed TAP_* block + header), `src/simulation/voltage.py` (iterated PV correction, removed `b_diag`), `src/simulation/reactive_devices.py` (removed `TransformerTap` + tap plumbing), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (removed tap plumbing from `_build_q_injections`/`_build_state`/`SimulationState`/tick comments + unused `S_BASE` import), `src/display/context.py`/`canvas.py`/`symbols.py`/`palette.py` (removed tap display), `tests/test_voltage_reactive.py` (removed dead tap test, added 2 convergence tests), `scripts/render_voltage_verify.py` (removed tap use), `SIMULATION_API.md`/`DOMAIN_GLOSSARY.md`/`GRID_SIMULATION_MECHANICS.md` (trimmed tap references).

### Session 63 (Grid Designer: authored substation type + hydro plant size picker)

- **User request**: while iterating on Shift 4's grid (still in progress, not committed as a finished shift this session), the developer identified two Grid Designer gaps and asked for both: (1) substation type (MIXED/INDUSTRIAL/RESIDENTIAL) cannot be authored on a bus at all — it only ever existed as a runtime concept (a shift file's `SUBSTATION_TYPES` dict, or `DESIGNER_TEST`'s random round-robin assignment); (2) the `HYDRO` unit type is a single fixed 250MW size, with no way to place a smaller or larger plant.
- **Substation type — new persisted field**: `DesignerBus` (`src/data/designer_io.py`) gained `substation_type: str = 'MIXED'` — additive, same pattern as Session 62's `length_km`, so every existing saved grid loads unchanged (defaults to MIXED). Per developer decision, scoped to LOAD buses only in the UI (matches the only place the field is actually consumed — reactive load/power-factor/auto-shunt-bank eligibility in `seed_default_reactive_devices()`). New click-to-cycle control in the bus properties panel (`src/display/designer_panels.py`, directly below the existing PK LOAD field, only shown for LOAD buses) cycling MIXED→INDUSTRIAL→RESIDENTIAL→MIXED, wired via a new `prop_substation_type_toggle` action in `src/display/designer.py` — mirrors the existing SLACK BUS toggle's exact interaction pattern per developer's explicit preference.

Now it reads `{b.label: b.substation_type for b in buses if ...}` directly. `default_substation_types()` (`src/data/profiles.py`) was **deleted**, not deprecated — confirmed via repo-wide grep it had exactly one caller, which this change replaced, and its own docstring already described itself as a stand-in for authored data that no longer applies now that authoring exists.

Implemented as a second dialog step chained onto the existing single-dialog mechanism (`_dialog_active`/`_dialog_prompt`/`_dialog_buffer`/`_dialog_callback`, already used for the unit-count and peak-load prompts) — `_ask_hydro_size()`/`_finish_hydro_size()` in `src/display/designer.py`, the latter setting a new sticky `self._palette_hydro_size` state (defaults to `'LARGE'`) and chaining straight into the existing `_ask_unit_count()`. `_finish_place_units()` overrides the four scaling fields from `HYDRO_SIZE_DEFAULTS[self._palette_hydro_size]` only when `unit_type == 'HYDRO'` — `HYDRO_ROR`/`HYDRO_PUMP` bus-click handlers skip straight to the count dialog unchanged (confirmed via a dedicated regression test, not just code inspection).
