### Session 62 (Line length in km as the physical basis for reactance_pu)

- **User request**: reviewing Session 61's Shift 4 grid, the developer noticed three new lines (L10/L11/L12) carried a `reactance_pu` of 2.6-2.8 — ~100x higher than every other line in every other grid file (max ~0.19 elsewhere) — with no corresponding change to their on-canvas length, and asked whether line length should instead be proportional to Manhattan distance on screen, with a px-to-km scale factor, and whether that length should be Designer-editable in km rather than as a raw per-unit number.
- **Confirmed the existing mechanism first**: the Grid Designer already derives an initial `reactance_pu` guess from raw **Euclidean** canvas distance at line-placement time only (`_place_line()`/`_auto_route_lines()`'s `make_line()`, both in `src/display/designer.py`, via the `DESIGNER_X_SCALE` constant) — but `reactance_pu` becomes freely hand-editable afterward with no link back to geometry, and the actual rendered line path (`src/display/canvas.py`'s port-based, obstacle-avoiding orthogonal routing) is a separate, ephemeral per-frame display cache never stored in data.
- **New `length_km` field, additive, `reactance_pu` derived from it**: `DesignerLine` (`src/data/designer_io.py`) and the production `Line` (`src/data/topology.py`) both gained `length_km: float | None = None`. `reactance_pu` is *recomputed from* `length_km` whenever it changes (placement or edit) — the simulation layer (`loadflow.py`/`voltage.py`, the only two consumers of `reactance_pu`) is completely untouched and keeps reading `reactance_pu` exactly as before.
- **Manhattan distance, per-voltage-tier reactance-per-km**: new constants in `constants.py` — `KM_PER_PX = 0.35` (Manhattan px → km, new-line placement/edit only) and `REACTANCE_PU_PER_KM_150KV/220KV/400KV` (0.015556 / 0.007231 / 0.002187), physically derived from a real ~0.35 Ω/km overhead-line reactance divided by each tier's `Z_BASE = V_BASE²/S_BASE` (`S_BASE=1000 MVA` shared across all tiers, so `Z_BASE` — and therefore reactance_pu/km — varies ~7x per tier even before considering any ohms/km difference). A new `reactance_pu_per_km(voltage_kv)` helper lives in `designer.py` (the sole consumer) rather than `constants.py`, which stays pure-data per Rule 1. Confirmed via a sweep of every existing grid file that none of them show any real per-tier signal today (all cluster ~0.0002-0.00026 reactance_pu/px regardless of tier) — this is a genuine physics improvement, not a compatibility break.
- **`_place_line()`/`make_line()`** now compute `manhattan_px = |dx| + |dy|` → `length_km = max(0.1, manhattan_px * KM_PER_PX)` → `reactance_pu = length_km * reactance_pu_per_km(vkv)`, storing both fields on the constructed line.
- **Designer editing**: replaced the "X PU" editable field with an editable "LENGTH KM" field (`edit_length_km` action + `_commit_edit()` branch in `designer.py`, mirroring the exact pattern the old `edit_reactance_pu` used) that recomputes and stores `reactance_pu` immediately on commit; `designer_panels.py`'s line-property panel now shows LENGTH KM as the editable primary field with a read-only `X PU:` line beneath it showing the derived value, so the causality (km drives pu, not the reverse) is visually explicit. `DESIGNER_X_SCALE` is left in `constants.py`, marked superseded — no remaining call sites, not deleted this session to keep the diff scoped.
- **Migration, back-derived only (never touching `reactance_pu`)**: every line in every existing grid JSON file (`grid1`, `Alpha`, `shift1-4`, `shift10` — 379 lines total) and every `Line(...)` call in the shared production `topology.py` (62 lines) gained `length_km = round(reactance_pu / reactance_pu_per_km(tier), 1)`, computed from and preserving the existing, unchanged `reactance_pu` exactly. Confirmed Shift 4's three outlier lines back-derive to 167.1/180.0/167.1 km respectively — consistent with their already-written "deliberately long, weak, unparalleled rural spur" narrative, no changes needed there.
- **Migration tooling pitfall found and corrected mid-session**: the first `topology.py` migration pass (a small `ast`-based script that parses each `Line(...)` call and inserts `length_km=X.X` before its closing paren) opened the file in plain text mode without `newline=''`, and Python's universal-newline translation silently rewrote the file's original LF line endings to CRLF on write — producing a whole-file diff (661 insertions/656 deletions) that looked like every line had changed, though the actual content was unaffected. Caught by inspecting the diff stat before treating the migration as done; fixed by re-running with explicit `newline=''` on both the read and write, which reduced the diff to the intended 62 single-line insertions. Also caught and fixed a related self-inflicted issue: reverting the file via `git checkout` to fix the line-ending problem also reverted the `Line` dataclass's own `length_km` field addition (made earlier in the same session, before the migration script ran) — re-added directly, no data lost since the migration script's `length_km=` insertions into the 62 `Line(...)` calls were on-disk-only until the dataclass itself defined the field to accept them.
- **`topology_lines_to_designer()`** (`designer_io.py`, converts production `Line`s to `DesignerLine`s for opening the shared campaign topology in the Designer) and **`designer_grid.py`'s `_to_line()`** (`DesignerLine` → production `Line`, the `DesignerGrid` test-session adapter) both updated to pass `length_km` through — the only two conversion seams between the two schemas.
- **Verified**: (1) Snapshotted every line's `reactance_pu` across all 7 grid JSON files (379 lines) before migration; re-loaded after and confirmed bit-for-bit identical, with every line now carrying a non-`None` `length_km`. (2) Dry-ran the `topology.py` migration against a scratch copy first, verified it compiled and every field except the new `length_km` matched the real module exactly, before running it for real. (3) `python -m py_compile` on all edited files. (4) `python -m pytest tests/` — 27/27 pass, unchanged. (5) Re-ran the existing Shift 1-3 regression check and Shift 4's full verified trajectory/intervention test from Session 61 — both byte-identical to their pre-session output (frequency range, WATCH/CRITICAL timings, alarm sequence), confirming zero physics drift from this change. (6) Headless Designer smoke test (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`): placed a new line between two buses at a known Manhattan pixel distance and confirmed `length_km`/`reactance_pu` matched the new formula exactly by hand-computed comparison; exercised the `_start_edit`/`_commit_edit` text-buffer flow directly, confirmed editing `length_km` immediately recomputes `reactance_pu` consistently; rendered full Designer frames (`gd.draw()`) with a line selected, mid-edit, and with a legacy `length_km=None` line, all without exception.
- **Not done this session**: `DESIGNER_X_SCALE` was not deleted (superseded but harmless, flagged for a future cleanup pass); the Shift Builder's JSON schema (`shift_io.py`) was not touched — it round-trips whatever `DesignerLine` already carries, so `length_km` passes through it automatically with no separate change needed, but this was not explicitly exercised this session; no live (non-headless) fullscreen Designer playtest was performed, matching this environment's established headless-verification precedent.
- Edited: `src/data/designer_io.py` (`DesignerLine.length_km`, `topology_lines_to_designer()`), `src/data/topology.py` (`Line.length_km`, 62 `Line(...)` calls gained `length_km=`), `src/simulation/designer_grid.py` (`_to_line` passthrough), `src/simulation/constants.py` (`KM_PER_PX`, 3 `REACTANCE_PU_PER_KM_*` constants, `DESIGNER_X_SCALE` comment), `src/display/designer.py` (`reactance_pu_per_km()` helper, `_place_line`/`make_line` formula, `edit_length_km` action + commit branch), `src/display/designer_panels.py` (LENGTH KM editable row + read-only X PU row), `src/assets/designer_grids/*.json` (grid1/Alpha/shift1-4/shift10 — `length_km` added to every line, migration only).

### Session 61 (Shift 4 — "Local Support" voltage/reactive tutorial; campaign-shift wiring for the Session 60 mechanic)

- **User request**: build a new campaign tutorial shift (Shift 4) expanding the Shift 1-3 narrative arc to teach voltage/reactive power regulation — the mechanic Session 60 made fully live in the simulation/display layers but explicitly scoped out of any campaign shift (exercisable only via `DESIGNER_TEST`). Developer chose: expand Shift 3's grid rather than build fresh; teach **both** manual levers (generator AVR setpoint and manual SVC), not just one; wire both keybindings into real campaign play now; extend duration to 4 hours; place the shift the day after Shift 3 (Wed 09 Nov 1994, vs. Shift 3's Tue 08 Nov).
- **Two real, pre-existing gaps found and fixed as prerequisites** (the shift was unplayable without them): (1) `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer()` (the real campaign bootstrap) never called `seed_default_reactive_devices()` or passed `substation_types` — every campaign shift ran with zero reactive devices and all-MIXED power factor regardless of grid design; only the `DESIGNER_TEST` debug path did this. (2) The two manual voltage levers' keybindings (`,`/`.` for SVC, `v`+digits+Enter for AVR setpoint) existed only in the `DESIGNER_TEST` keydown block of `main.py`, never in `GameState.PLAYING` — the renderer methods (`on_svc_adjust`, `on_setpoint_toggle/digit/backspace/enter`) worked correctly but had no route from real gameplay.
- **New opt-in shift-file constants** (all empty/no-op unless a shift declares them, so Shifts 1-3/5-10 are unaffected): `SUBSTATION_TYPES` (per-bus power factor + device seeding, read by `loader.py`, applied in `_make_sim_and_renderer` via the existing `seed_default_reactive_devices`), `SHUNT_BANK_OVERRIDES` (resize/pre-engage an individual bus's automatic shunt bank — new `GridSimulation.resize_shunt_bank(bus, max_steps, mvar_per_step, initial_step)` method), `INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS` (per-unit AVR setpoint at handover, overriding the campaign default `GEN_VOLTAGE_SETPOINT_DEFAULT_PU`). `main.py`'s `GameState.PLAYING` keydown block gained the SVC/AVR key routes, mirroring `DESIGNER_TEST`'s existing pattern exactly (same guard conditions) — purely additive, no change to any existing PLAYING behaviour since `_setpoint_active` is always `False` unless a player explicitly presses `V` first.
- **New `VOLTAGE_PU` scripted-event condition metric** (`simulation.py::_eval_condition`, `target`=bus label, reads the collapse-adjusted `SimulationState.bus_voltages`) — no prior metric exposed voltage to the declarative condition schema; documented in `shift_io.py`'s docstring alongside the existing metric list.
- **Debrief gained one informational line** (`build_debrief_lines`): `Minimum bus voltage: X.XXX pu` under NETWORK SECURITY, reading the existing (previously unsurfaced) `state.min_voltage_seen` — purely additive, does not affect the EXCELLENT/SATISFACTORY/MARGINAL/UNSATISFACTORY assessment logic, applies to all shifts harmlessly (defaults to 1.0 when nothing sags).
- **Grid** (`src/assets/designer_grids/shift4.json`): Shift 3's 6-bus/9-line/3-unit grid (RIVE slack, ASHC hydro-gen, GREY/OAKE/RAVE load, SUTT transmission hop, RIVE-1/RIVE-2/ASHC-1) plus three new buses/lines and one new unit: **FENN** (Fenshaw, LOAD, off SUTT via new high-reactance single-circuit L10 — no local generation, teaches the manual SVC) and **STAV** (Stavely, LOAD, off ASHC via new high-reactance L11) with a new dedicated small generator **STAG-1** (Stagshaw CCGT, 80 MW, on its own new bus STAG, connected to STAV via L12 — teaches the AVR-setpoint lever). RIVE-2's Shift 3 cooling-fault derate is resolved per developer's mid-session correction ("it's a new day") — both Riverside units return to full 300 MW availability.
- **Tuning found the physics far less forgiving than assumed, and required three full re-designs before landing** (all verified empirically against the real `GridSimulation`, never by hand calculation, per this project's established precedent): (1) FENN's automatic shunt bank at default sizing (4 steps × 50 MVAr) fully self-heals any reachable sag to exactly 1.000 pu within ~5 sim-minutes regardless of how severe — undersized to 1 step via `SHUNT_BANK_OVERRIDES` (and pre-engaged at that step via the new `initial_step` param, since starting at step 0 produced a brief but real near-blackout transient before the bank's first switch — not a viable "handover state" for a shift whose briefing implies stability, not a sudden fault). (2) Reusing an existing Shift 3 load bus (GREY) for the AVR lesson failed outright: those buses are deliberately built with strong parallel low-reactance feeds (the redundancy Shift 3 already taught), so a generator's AVR setpoint has no visible reach — this is why STAV/STAG were added as new, dedicated topology rather than reusing GREY/ASHC-1. (3) A first STAV/STAG design also failed because ASHC-1 (initially the intended support generator) was already fully Q-saturated (`q_reserve_mvar=0`) just serving GREY/OAKE's own demand before STAV entered the picture at all, and a same-bus/short-strong-line STAG design saturated instantly for the same reason in reverse — the working design needed a weak line *and* real Q-vs-load headroom margin between the low (0.95) and high (1.05) setpoint, found only by sweeping reactance/peak-load/Q-capacity together against the real solver. Also surfaced and fixed a **frequency-collapse bug in the tuning itself** (not a game bug): an earlier attempt sized FENN/STAV's peak load without checking it against the fleet's actual dispatch capacity, driving total demand to within ~15% of the true generation ceiling and permanently pinning frequency at 45 Hz — corrected by re-deriving `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` to properly track the new grid's real 18:00 demand peak (270+290+230+60 = 850 MW dispatch against ~870 MW peak demand, comfortably under the fleet's 930 MW ceiling).
- **Final tuned values**: FENN `L10` reactance 2.6 pu / peak 160 MW / shunt capped at 1 step (pre-engaged); STAV `L11` reactance 2.8 pu / peak 210 MW; STAG-1 `L12` reactance 2.6 pu, `q_max/q_min` 55/-18 MVAr, AVR setpoint starts at 0.95 pu (`INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS`). Both acts run concurrently rather than sequentially (STAV/STAG narrative events at T+10/45/80, FENN narrative events at T+15/30/50) since both buses are in genuine WATCH tier only during the first ~60-80 minutes of the 240-minute shift before the evening demand ramp eases them back to HEALTHY on its own if left unattended — narrative/docstring reframed from "two acts in sequence" to "two problems presenting together" to match.
- **Verified**: (1) Headless, using the real `_make_sim_and_renderer`-equivalent construction path (`load_shift_config` → `DesignerGrid` → `GridSimulation` with the new opt-in hooks applied exactly as `main.py` applies them): frequency stays in [49.86, 50.01] Hz throughout an unattended 240-minute run (no collapse); STAV and FENN both start in genuine WATCH tier and both correctly cross to CRITICAL (T+80 / T+50 respectively) if left completely unattended; with correct, timely player intervention (`set_generator_voltage_setpoint('STAG-1', 1.05)` at T+10, `set_svc_setpoint('FENN', 150.0)` at T+20) both buses recover to HEALTHY and **zero** "still sagging" CRITICAL alarms fire, while the `_STAV_RECOVERED`/`_FENN_RECOVERED` TUTOR confirmations do. (2) Regression: Shifts 1-3 rebuilt via the same construction path — `substation_types` resolves to `None` (opt-in default), zero reactive devices seeded, `bus_shunt_step` all-zero, frequency nominal — confirming byte-identical behaviour to pre-session. (3) `python -m pytest tests/` — 27/27 pass (unchanged; pre-existing `PytestReturnNotNoneWarning` warnings in `test_voltage_reactive.py` are prior-session style debt, not touched here). (4) Headless renderer-driven smoke test (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`): Tab-selected STAG-1 and FENN through the real `Renderer`/`DesignerGrid` path built exactly as `_make_sim_and_renderer` builds it, drove the full `V`→digits→Enter AVR-setpoint sequence and the `,`/`.` SVC-adjust sequence, confirmed both commands reach `GridSimulation` and take effect on the next tick (the one-tick-stale `get_state()` behaviour observed mid-session is pre-existing, documented `SimulationState` snapshot semantics, not a bug — confirmed by checking the identical lag exists for the long-standing `set_unit_target` MW-dispatch command). (5) `python -m py_compile` on all edited/created files — clean.
- **Not done this session**: no `F4` dev-shortcut key was added alongside the existing `F1`/`F3`/`F5` shift-switch shortcuts in `GameState.PLAYING` — that gap already existed for shifts other than 1/3/5 and is unrelated to this session's scope; Shift 4 is reachable through the normal shift-select progression (unlocks after Shift 3 is completed) without it. `SUBSTATION_TYPES`/`SHUNT_BANK_OVERRIDES`/`INITIAL_VOLTAGE_SETPOINTS` are not yet exposed in the Shift Builder's JSON schema (`shift_io.py`) — this session's three new constants are `shift_NN.py`-only, matching how `MAINTENANCE_LINES` was also campaign-only before any Shift Builder work touched it. A live (non-headless) fullscreen playtest was not performed — matches this environment's established precedent (Sessions 46/49/60) of headless + offscreen-PNG verification only.
- Edited: `src/main.py` (`_make_sim_and_renderer` substation_types/shunt_bank_overrides/initial_voltage_setpoints wiring, `GameState.PLAYING` keydown block SVC/AVR key routes, `build_debrief_lines` min-voltage line), `src/gameplay/shifts/loader.py` (`substation_types`/`shunt_bank_overrides`/`initial_voltage_setpoints` keys + docstring), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (`_eval_condition` new `VOLTAGE_PU` metric, new `resize_shunt_bank()` method), `src/data/shift_io.py` (docstring — new condition metric), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_04.py` (filled from empty stub).

### Session 60 (Voltage & reactive power made live — reactive load, AVR setpoints, collapse offset, auto shunts/taps, manual SVC — VOLTAGE_REACTIVE_PLAN.md)

- **User request**: implement `VOLTAGE_REACTIVE_PLAN.md` (a plan the developer had already written and approved) — make voltage/reactive power a live gameplay mechanic. Exploration confirmed the plan's own finding: the decoupled voltage solver (`src/simulation/voltage.py`, `ΔV = B'⁻¹ × Q`) was already fully built and called every tick, but fed all-zero reactive inputs (no load Q, generator setpoints hardcoded to `1.0`, no compensation devices) — so every bus solved to exactly 1.0 pu regardless of topology and the voltage alarms/crisis/VSI machinery could never fire. This was a forcing-function + gameplay/display build, not a physics build. Implemented phase-by-phase (A→E) with a full `pytest` run after each phase, per the plan's own build order.
- **Phase A — reactive forcing + substation types**: `src/data/profiles.py`'s `SubstationDemandSpec` gained a `substation_type` field (`INDUSTRIAL`/`RESIDENTIAL`/`MIXED`, default `MIXED`), plus a `default_substation_types()` helper (deterministic round-robin by sorted label) for runtime-seeding grids with no authored types. `src/simulation/demand.py::DemandModel` gained `q_load_injections()` mirroring `p_load_injections()`'s shape (caches `tan(acos(PF))` per bus from `SUBSTATION_TYPE_PF`, returns negative MVAr) — filtering by blackout zones happens caller-side in `simulation.py`, matching how `p_load_injections()` is already filtered, not inside `demand.py` itself. `src/simulation/units.py::UnitModel` gained `_v_setpoint_pu` (default `1.02`) and `set_voltage_setpoint()`; `pv_bus_constraints()`'s hardcoded `.append(1.0)` (line 624) now appends each unit's real setpoint. `simulation.py::_build_q_injections()` now merges `q_load_injections()` (blackout-filtered exactly like `_build_p_injections`) instead of returning an all-zero dict; threaded through the main tick, the post-trip re-solve, and `run_forecast_mode()`.
- **Phase B — VSI tiers + collapse acceleration**: the voltage solver stays a pure, stateless matrix solve, so the documented nonlinear collapse acceleration (GRID_SIMULATION_MECHANICS.md §5.6) is implemented as a **stateful post-solve overlay owned by `GridSimulation`** (`self._v_collapse_offset: {bus: offset_pu}`), applied once per tick after topology is final via `_apply_collapse_acceleration()`: buses below `V_WARNING_LOW` (0.85) accumulate a negative offset per `severity² × V_COLLAPSE_GAIN`; buses at/above it decay the offset back toward 0 at `V_COLLAPSE_RECOVERY_PU_S`. The result (`v_eff`) — never the raw solve — now feeds `_update_voltage_alarms`, `_update_crisis`, `min_voltage_seen` tracking, and both `bus_voltages`/`bus_vsi` in the snapshot (previously `bus_vsi` was a verbatim, independently-computed copy of `bus_voltages` — now both are written from the same `v_eff` so they can't silently diverge). Offset resets to 0 for any bus entering a blackout zone. New `SimulationState.bus_vsi_tier` (`HEALTHY`/`WATCH`/`WARNING`/`CRITICAL`) via a `_vsi_tier()` helper so display never recomputes tiers from raw voltage.
- **Phase C — reactive devices**: new `src/simulation/reactive_devices.py` (no pygame) — `ShuntBank`/`TransformerTap` (both **automatic**, stepped by `ReactiveDevices.step_automatics()`) and `SVC` (**manual**, player-set), all modelled as Q injections (never B' edits, so switching a device never re-factorises the voltage matrix). Automatics act on the *previous* tick's raw solved voltage (one-tick lag, called before that tick's Q injections are built — no algebraic loop with the solver), gated by a deadband + a minimum dwell time between switches to prevent hunting. Transformer taps approximate their voltage-ratio correction as `ΔQ = B'_diag × ΔV × S_BASE` via a new `VoltageModel.b_diag()` accessor — a documented simplification, not true ratio modelling. `GridSimulation` gained `set_generator_voltage_setpoint()` and `set_svc_setpoint()` (the two manual player levers) plus `seed_default_reactive_devices()` (runtime seeding, not yet authored into the Designer JSON schema). Also fixed a real pre-existing gap surfaced while wiring this: `unit_q_injections_mvar`/`unit_bus_types` were previously driven only by manual `set_unit_q_target()`, never by the voltage solver's own PV-correction pass — so a generator holding voltage via AVR always showed `q=0.0`/`PV` even while saturated. `_build_state()` now writes the solver's actual `q_injections_used` back onto co-located units (split evenly, mirroring how `pv_bus_constraints()` already sums their limits) before building the per-unit snapshot, so `unit_q_injections_mvar`, `unit_bus_types` (PV→PQ), and the new `unit_q_reserve_mvar` are all now real.
- **Phase D — display**: `palette.py` fixed a latent bug (`COL_VSI_WARNING`/`COL_VSI_CRITICAL` were identical bright red) — CRITICAL is now bright magenta, distinct from WARNING; added `COL_SHUNT_CAP`/`COL_SHUNT_REACTOR`/`COL_SVC`/`COL_TAP`. `symbols.py` gained `draw_vsi_halo()` (ring around a substation, WATCH/WARNING steady, CRITICAL blinking at `VSI_HALO_BLINK_HZ`) and three device glyphs (shunt bars, tap arrow, SVC diamond), wired into `canvas.py`'s per-bus draw loop with distinct corner offsets so up to 3 co-located devices stay visually separable (`DEVICE_GLYPH_OFFSET_PX`) — first real use of `COL_VSI_*`. `context.py::draw_bus_context` grew VSI-tier/Q rows plus read-only auto-shunt/auto-tap rows and (only for a bus hosting one) an interactive SVC row; `draw_unit_context` grew an editable AVR setpoint field, a PV/PQ indicator, and a Q/Q-reserve row, all gated behind `is_dispatchable and not is_renewable`. `renderer.py` gained `_get_selected_bus()` (mirrors `_get_selected_line`), a separate `_setpoint_buffer`/`_setpoint_active` pair (so AVR editing can never collide with the existing MW-target buffer), `on_svc_adjust()`, and `on_setpoint_digit/backspace/enter/toggle()`. **Manual controls are keyboard-only this pass** — exploration found GRIDCOM has no mouse hit-rect testing for *any* command today (line trip/close, unit start/stop are all keyboard-only; `on_click` only hit-tests for selection), so this isn't a regression, just consistency with the existing precedent; flagged as a pre-existing, project-wide gap against CLAUDE.md's mouse+keyboard-parity rule, not fixed here.
- **Phase E — DESIGNER_TEST wiring + a pre-existing bug fix**: `_make_designer_test()` (`main.py`) now calls `sim.seed_default_reactive_devices(substation_types)` after construction. New DESIGNER_TEST keys: `,`/`.` (SVC adjust ±`SVC_Q_STEP_MVAR` on the selected bus), `v` (enter AVR setpoint edit mode on the selected unit) then digits/`.` + Enter, all routed through the existing Backspace/Enter handlers by checking `_rend._setpoint_active` first. **Found and fixed a real pre-existing bug while smoke-testing this path** (unrelated to voltage/reactive, but blocking): `_make_designer_test`/`_make_campaign_shift_test` both construct `GridSimulation(shift_number=0, ...)`, but no `gameplay/shifts/shift_00.py` module existed, so `load_shift_config(0)` raised `ModuleNotFoundError` and DESIGNER_TEST could not be entered at all, for any grid, independent of this session's work — confirmed via direct reproduction before fixing. Fixed with a new `shift_00.py` sentinel stub (same empty-stub pattern as `shift_04.py`-`shift_10.py`) so the existing `getattr(..., default)` fallback in `load_shift_config()` resolves it; all real config for these sessions was already passed explicitly to `GridSimulation`'s constructor, so the stub's fallback values are never actually read.
- **Docs updated** (developer decision: `constants.py` is authoritative, docs updated to match): `GRID_SIMULATION_MECHANICS.md` §5 rewritten — VSI tiers corrected to the real 0.90/0.85/0.70 thresholds (previously documented 0.95/0.90/0.85/0.80), new §5.3b (per-substation-type power factor), §5.6 rewritten around the actual stateful-overlay implementation, §5.7 rewritten around automatic regulators vs. the two manual levers and reframed around local/regional voltage management. `DOMAIN_GLOSSARY.md` — VSI/collapse-acceleration entries corrected to match, new entries for power factor/substation type, AVR setpoint, Q-reserve, shunt bank, transformer tap changer, SVC/STATCOM; the "Does NOT Simulate" list's tap-changer line corrected (approximated via Q injection now, not entirely unsimulated). `SIMULATION_API.md` — documented `bus_vsi_tier` and the 6 new device-state fields, `unit_v_setpoint_pu`/`unit_q_reserve_mvar`, the two new `set_*` methods, and noted `bus_voltages`/`bus_vsi` now carry `v_effective`.
- **Verified**: phase-by-phase, each followed by a full `python -m pytest tests/` run (15/15 baseline throughout, no regressions). New `tests/test_voltage_reactive.py` (12 tests, all passing): reactive load moves non-slack voltage off 1.000 while slack stays exactly 1.0; industrial (low-PF) buses sag more than residential under comparable MW; `q_load_injections()` sign/magnitude math directly; generator setpoint feeds `pv_bus_constraints()` and clamps to range; blackout zones zero reactive load exactly like active load; collapse offset strictly decreases voltage tick-over-tick while sustained below 0.85; WARNING then CRITICAL alarms fire with `crisis_active`/`crisis_type='CRITICAL'` and tiers transition WARNING→CRITICAL in order; offset decays and voltage recovers once load is relieved; an automatic shunt bank steps up and holds within a bounded switch count (no hunting) over a 600-tick run; an automatic tap does the same; a manual SVC moves voltage monotonically with its setpoint and clamps at ±150 MVAr, rejecting a bus with no SVC; a generator setpoint sweep raises a neighbouring bus's voltage while its own Q rises monotonically to `q_max_mvar` and the bus converts PV→PQ with `q_reserve` reaching 0. Phase D additionally verified visually: `scripts/render_voltage_verify.py` renders two offscreen (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`) frames to PNG — a bus context panel showing a yellow WATCH halo, tap/SVC/shunt glyphs, and all new rows; a unit context panel showing the AVR field, PV/PQ indicator, and Q/Q-reserve row — both inspected pixel-by-pixel to confirm correct rendering and colours. Final end-to-end smoke test (`_make_designer_test`-style sim, typed load buses, seeded devices, driven ~1 sim-hour): substation types applied correctly, industrial bus sags more than residential, auto shunt bank steps in over the hour and holds, both manual levers accepted and further raised the sagging bus's voltage — matches the plan's Verify E spec. Confirmed the real `_make_designer_test()` path itself (not just the synthetic test fixtures) builds, ticks, and renders without exception against a real Designer grid (`shift3.json`) after the `shift_00.py` fix.
- **Not done this session (explicitly out of scope per the plan)**: mouse hit-rect controls for the two manual levers (or for any existing command) — flagged as a pre-existing, project-wide gap, not introduced here. Authoring substation types/devices into the Grid Designer JSON schema (still runtime-seeded only). True transformer voltage-ratio modelling (still a Q-injection approximation). A campaign-shift framing/tutorial for voltage management (this pass is mechanic + display + tools only, exercisable via DESIGNER_TEST, per the plan's explicit scope).
- Edited: `src/simulation/constants.py` (new REACTIVE/device/VSI-halo constant blocks), `src/data/profiles.py` (`SubstationDemandSpec.substation_type`, `default_substation_types()`, `get_substation_demand_specs()` gained a types param), `src/simulation/demand.py` (`q_load_injections()`), `src/simulation/units.py` (`v_setpoint_pu`/`set_voltage_setpoint()`, `pv_bus_constraints()`, `get_state_snapshot()` gained `v_setpoint_pu`/`q_reserve_mvar`, `set_unit_voltage_setpoint()`), `src/simulation/voltage.py` (`b_diag()` accessor), `src/simulation/simulation.py` (`_build_q_injections` real Q, collapse overlay, `bus_vsi_tier`, device wiring, `set_generator_voltage_setpoint`/`set_svc_setpoint`/`seed_default_reactive_devices`, per-unit Q backfill from the solver, ~10 new `SimulationState` fields), `src/display/palette.py`, `src/display/symbols.py`, `src/display/canvas.py`, `src/display/context.py`, `src/display/renderer.py`, `src/main.py` (`_make_designer_test` seeding + DESIGNER_TEST keys), `GRID_SIMULATION_MECHANICS.md`, `DOMAIN_GLOSSARY.md`, `SIMULATION_API.md`.
- Created: `src/simulation/reactive_devices.py`, `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_00.py`, `tests/test_voltage_reactive.py`, `scripts/render_voltage_verify.py`.

### Session 59 (WARNING gets its own alarm loop — warning_ping.wav — separate from CRITICAL's alarm.wav)

- **Bug reported**: the developer expected every non-CRITICAL alarm-panel message to produce a ping, but heard nothing. Investigation found two issues in the Session 55 sound subsystem: `ping.wav` was never supplied as an asset (developer will supply it, not this session's concern), and `src/display/sound.py`'s `update()` used an `if`/`elif` where any WARNING alarm took the "clear silence" branch and could never also reach the ping branch, so WARNING never produced any one-shot sound regardless of asset availability.
- **First pass (mid-session, since superseded)**: added a `warning_ping.wav` *one-shot* for new WARNING alarms, split the if/elif into independent conditions. This was corrected within the same session — see below.
- **Final design, per developer clarification**: WARNING is not a one-shot at all — it should **loop** `warning_ping.wav` continuously while any WARNING alarm is unacknowledged, exactly like CRITICAL already loops `alarm.wav`. `alarm.wav` becomes CRITICAL-exclusive; `warning_ping.wav` is WARNING-exclusive; INFO/TUTOR remain a one-shot `ping.wav`, never looping. Since CRITICAL and WARNING can both be unacknowledged simultaneously with two different sounds, `SoundManager` now reserves **two** mixer channels instead of one (`pygame.mixer.set_reserved(2)`, channel 0 = `_critical_channel` for `alarm.wav`, channel 1 = `_warning_channel` for `warning_ping.wav`) and computes `critical_active`/`warning_active` as two independent booleans (each `any(priority == X and not acknowledged)`), each driving its own channel independently. SPACE (`silence()`) stops both channels and sets one shared `_silenced` flag (developer confirmed no per-severity silence — a single key silences both loops together, matching the original single-loop behavior); a new CRITICAL or WARNING alarm clears the shared flag and re-arms both.
- **Constants** (`src/simulation/constants.py`, `# SOUND` section): `SOUND_PATH_WARNING_PING = 'assets/sounds/warning_ping.wav'` (comment corrected from "one-shot" to "loops while a WARNING alarm is unacknowledged"), `SOUND_VOLUME_WARNING_PING = 0.5`; `SOUND_PATH_ALARM`'s comment narrowed from "WARNING/CRITICAL" to "CRITICAL" only.
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m py_compile` on both edited files — clean. (2) Headless smoke test (`SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy`) with fake channel/sound stand-ins, covering: CRITICAL alone loops `alarm.wav` only; WARNING alone loops `warning_ping.wav` only; both simultaneously loop independently on their own channels; `silence()` stops both and holds silent across repeated ticks with the same unacknowledged alarms; a new alarm after silence re-arms both; acknowledging everything stops both channels and resets `_silenced`; INFO/TUTOR still produce a `ping.wav` one-shot with no effect on either loop channel. All 7 scenarios passed. (3) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass, unchanged.
- **Not done this session**: neither `ping.wav` nor `warning_ping.wav` were supplied as actual audio files — the developer will add both `src/assets/sounds/ping.wav` and `src/assets/sounds/warning_ping.wav` themselves; until then, both remain silent no-ops via the existing `_load_sound()` soft-fail pattern (same as `alarm.wav` already relies on for its own presence check). Per-severity independent silence was considered and explicitly declined by the developer in favor of one shared silence flag. Channel-count/starvation under heavy simultaneous alarm traffic remains a latent, unaddressed concern (now watching 2 reserved channels instead of 1) — not reported as an issue.
- Edited: `src/display/sound.py` (two reserved channels/`_critical_channel`/`_warning_channel` replacing the single `_alarm_channel`, independent `critical_active`/`warning_active` checks, `silence()` stops both, docstrings), `src/simulation/constants.py` (`SOUND_PATH_WARNING_PING`/`SOUND_VOLUME_WARNING_PING` comments corrected to describe a loop, `SOUND_PATH_ALARM` comment narrowed to CRITICAL-only).

### Session 58 (Shift 3: L09 backbone-redundancy lesson — first use of MAINTENANCE_LINES/LINE_OPEN)

- **User request**: Shift 3's 4-hour duration felt thin after the developer noticed only one real teaching beat (the RIVE-2 derate/AGC-reserve arc, T+60 through T+150). Rather than shortening the shift, the developer asked for a second, independent lesson on grid redundancy, and chose to add a new normally-open spare line the player must notice and close themselves — not a scripted trip the sim recovers from automatically.
- **Grid change** (`src/assets/designer_grids/shift3.json`, made directly by the developer, not this session): added `L09`, a new circuit in parallel with `L01` (RIVE→ASHC), identical reactance/rating (0.0143 pu, 400 MW, 220kV), no port overrides. This is the single backbone link every downstream load (GREY, OAKE, RAVE via SUTT) ultimately depends on — chosen over paralleling L06 (branch-local stakes, SUTT/RAVE only) or a new SUTT→ASHC mesh tie (a materially different loop-flow concept, deferred to a future shift) after the developer reviewed diagrammed options for all three.
- **`shift_03.py` changes**: added `MAINTENANCE_LINES = {'L09'}` (this is the first campaign shift to set this constant — the `maintenance_lines`/`LINE_OPEN`/`LINE_CLOSE` mechanism has existed in `shift_io.py`/`simulation.py` since it was built for the Shift Builder JSON schema, but no shift file had exercised it until now). Five new `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` added, layered on top of the existing AGC/reserve arc without changing any existing trigger times: a T+15 TUTOR introducing L09 as an open spare the player can close at will; a T+180 WARNING that trips L01 (`action: {'type': 'LINE_OPEN', 'line': 'L01'}`), simulating an unplanned backbone fault after the AGC/reserve arc has already resolved (~T+150); and two mutually-exclusive T+181 follow-ups gated on a new `LINE_LOADING` condition against `L09` itself (`<= 0.0` = still open, `> 0.0` = closed and carrying flow) — a CRITICAL "grid is dark" message if the player never closed it, or a calm TUTOR confirming the handoff worked if they did. No existing condition metric exposes line in-service/bus-energized state directly, so `LINE_LOADING` on the spare line itself (an out-of-service line is excluded from the load-flow's `_active_lines` entirely, so it reads exactly `0.0` while open) was reused as the gating signal instead of adding a new metric type.
- **Verified the actual grid consequence headlessly** before finalizing event wording, rather than assuming: with L09 left open, tripping L01 splits the grid into two islands (`{RIVE, SUTT, RAVE}` and `{ASHC, GREY, OAKE}`); the ASHC-side island's only generator, ASHC-1, itself trips within the same tick (`_trip_frequency_runaway_islands`'s island-frequency-collapse logic) — so GREY/OAKE genuinely lose all supply, not just a topology change with load quietly still served (confirmed `bus_loads` reports raw demand, not served power, so it stays nonzero even during a real blackout — read `SimulationState.bus_loads`'s own field comment to avoid misreading this). With L09 closed beforehand, L01's trip causes no islanding at all (single connected island), ASHC-1 stays online at its pre-trip output, and frequency is undisturbed.
- **Docstring updated**: narrative section describes the L09 sub-plot alongside the existing AGC/reserve plot; `Grid:` summary line count corrected 8→9 lines; explicit note that this is the mechanism's first real use.
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m py_compile src/gameplay/shifts/shift_03.py` — clean. (2) Headless `GridSimulation` build via the real `load_shift_config`/`DesignerGrid` path — confirmed `L09` starts `TRIPPED` (open) and `L01` starts `IN_SERVICE` at tick 0. (3) Full event-timeline drive-through under two scenarios: player never closes L09 (L01 trip → island split → ASHC-1 trips → CRITICAL event fires, TUTOR recovery event correctly does not) and player closes L09 at T+20 (L01 trip → no island split, ASHC-1 unaffected, TUTOR recovery event fires, CRITICAL correctly does not) — confirmed via direct per-tick tracing of `line_status`/`frequency_hz`/unit `current_mw`, not just the final alarm log. (4) Confirmed the one-shot `fired` flag in `_process_scripted_events` means the two T+181 mutually-exclusive events each get exactly one evaluation and never retry, and that both land on the same tick as the T+180 trip's own `_execute_action` (sequential within one `_process_scripted_events()` call, so state is already updated by the time the later-indexed conditions are checked) without needing a wider time gap than 1 minute. (5) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass, unchanged.
- **Not done this session**: `DURATION_HOURS` left at 4.0 per developer's explicit choice ("add events, keep duration") rather than shortening the shift. The SUTT→ASHC mesh-tie option discussed and diagrammed alongside L09's final placement was not built — flagged as a possible future shift, not this one, since it teaches a materially different (loop-flow/meshed-network) concept than "spare backup for a single span." The pre-existing `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` oversupply note (flagged in Session 54/57) remains unrebalanced, unrelated to this session's work.
- Edited: `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_03.py` (`MAINTENANCE_LINES`, 5 new `SCRIPTED_EVENTS`, 2 new condition dicts, docstring). `src/assets/designer_grids/shift3.json` was edited directly by the developer (L09 added) prior to this session, not by this session.

### Session 57 (Demand derived from grid load-bus data; PEAK_DEMAND_MW/SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW/DEMAND_SHAPE_NORMALISED removed)

- **User request**: `PEAK_DEMAND_MW` (a hand-typed per-shift constant, added Session 56) was supposed to equal the sum of a shift's load-bus peaks but wasn't enforced to match — it had already drifted for Shift 3 (`PEAK_DEMAND_MW = 1400.0` vs. the shift's own docstring/grid total of 500 MW). The developer asked for demand to be derived from the grid's own load-bus data instead of hand-authored per shift.
- **Full unification, not just the peak scalar**: the developer's direction was to also stop hand-authoring per-shift demand *shape* — `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW` (Shifts 1-2's per-bus hourly tables) and `DEMAND_SHAPE_NORMALISED` (Shift 3's custom ramp curve) are both removed. All three shifts now derive demand identically: `gameplay/shifts/loader.py::load_shift_config()` loads the shift's Grid Designer grid (`GRID_SOURCE`), sums every active LOAD bus's `peak_load_mw` for `peak_demand_mw`, and builds `substation_load_mw` by scaling each LOAD bus's `peak_load_mw` by the single shared `DEMAND_PROFILE_NORMALISED` curve (`data/profiles.py`) — the same pattern `main.py::_make_designer_test()` already used for ad-hoc Designer-grid test sessions. `main.py::_make_sim_and_renderer()` simplified accordingly (its old `grid_source`/`demand_shape_normalised` branching collapsed to a single `cfg['substation_load_mw'] or None` line, since the loader now always returns it fully populated for `GRID_SOURCE` shifts). `demand_shape_normalised` dropped entirely from `load_shift_config()`'s returned dict (no remaining reader).
- **Pre-existing bug surfaced and fixed**: `shift_01.py`'s `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW` keyed its load table to `'OAKE'` — a bus that does not exist in `shift1.json` (that grid was redesigned down to 3 buses: RIVE/ASHC/GREY, with `GREY` as the only LOAD bus, `peak_load_mw=100.0`). The docstring still described the old 4-bus RIVE→ASHC→GREY→OAKE layout. Under the old code this silently produced demand at a phantom, unconnected `OAKE` bus while the grid's real `GREY` load bus received zero injection — Shift 1 has likely been running with no real demand since whenever `shift1.json` was last redesigned. Fixed by construction (the new derivation reads bus labels straight from the grid file); docstring and one scripted-event line corrected to say Greymoor/GREY instead of Oakendale/OAKE.
- **Shift 2 rebalanced, not just re-labelled**: switching Shift 2 (GREY+OAKE, 400 MW combined peak) from its old custom-tuned 10:00-14:00 ramp (273→311→340→371→400 MW) to the generic `DEMAND_PROFILE_NORMALISED` curve changes it from a rising ramp to a narrow band (348→356→352→344→340 MW, since the generic curve's true daily peak of 1.0 falls at 18:00, outside this shift's 10:00-14:00 window). Per developer decision, `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` was raised (`RIVE-1` 120→150, `ASHC-1` 160→200, 350 MW combined) to re-match the new ~348 MW handover demand instead of leaving the old 280 MW dispatch against the new curve's larger deficit; `HANDOVER_NOTES`/`SCRIPTED_EVENTS` text and the two `UNIT_OUTPUT_MW_SUM` thresholds (290→330, 320→330) rewritten to match the new flat-band framing.
- **Shift 3** narrative/docstring updated to the new 14:00-18:00 curve (425→435→455→480→500 MW, `PEAK_DEMAND_MW` corrected from the stale 1400.0 to the true 500.0) — actually a better fit for the "demand rising" framing than the old custom shape, which was nearly flat/slightly falling across the played window. `INITIAL_SCHEDULE`'s pre-existing oversupply note (previously "~204 MW over the 341.2 MW 10:00 checkpoint") updated to "~120 MW over the 425 MW 14:00 checkpoint" — still flagged as an unrebalanced, separate gameplay-balance item, not fixed this session. Scripted events were already keyed to unit-output/frequency thresholds rather than absolute demand values, so none needed logic changes.
- **`run_forecast_mode()`** (`simulation.py`) and `phase1.py::build_planning_model()`'s fallback role for `peak_demand_mw` (used only when `substation_specs` is empty or a `demand_override` schedule has no explicit peak) were traced but left untouched — confirmed out of scope and, for `run_forecast_mode`, not currently reachable (Phase 1 remains Shift-10-only and Shift 10 is an empty stub).
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m py_compile` on all edited files. (2) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass, unchanged (`test_demand_model` asserts only relative behaviour — morning>night, bus-sum, load-shed, losses — no hardcoded absolute MW pinned to the old tables, so it passed against the new derivation with no edits needed). (3) Headless: `load_shift_config(1..3)` gives `peak_demand_mw` 100.0/400.0/500.0 (Shift 3 corrected) with correctly-keyed `substation_load_mw` (Shift 1: `GREY` only, not `OAKE`); `load_shift_config(4..10)` (empty stubs) still return `0.0`/`{}` with no exception. (4) Built `GridSimulation` for shifts 1-3 via the real `DesignerGrid`/`load_designer_grid_named` path and ticked 50 steps each with no exception — shift 1 now shows real non-zero load (32.8 MW at hour ~4.1) where it previously would have been zero. (5) Grepped `PEAK_DEMAND_MW`/`SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW`/`DEMAND_SHAPE_NORMALISED` across `src/` — zero hits in `shift_01/02/03.py`; remaining `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW` references are all in `shift_io.py`/`designer_io.py`, describing the separate, untouched Shift-Builder JSON-authoring path (JSON-authored shifts keep their own literal `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW`, which was never part of this change).
- Edited: `src/gameplay/shifts/loader.py` (`load_shift_config()` demand derivation, docstrings), `src/gameplay/shifts/shift_01.py`/`shift_02.py`/`shift_03.py` (constants removed, docstrings/narrative/thresholds updated), `src/main.py` (`_make_sim_and_renderer()` simplified), `src/data/profiles.py` (docstring), `src/debug_scenario.py` (comment).
- **Not done this session (out of scope)**: `run_forecast_mode()`'s empty-`substation_specs` gap, and the equivalent gap in `phase1.py::build_planning_model()` for `GRID_SOURCE` shifts without `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW` — both pre-existing, both currently unreachable (Phase 1 is Shift-10-only, Shift 10 is a stub). Shift 3's pre-existing `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` oversupply was re-quantified against the new curve but not rebalanced, matching its existing "separate gameplay-balance pass" flag from Session 54/56.

### Session 56 (Consolidated SHIFT_SPECS into shift_NN.py files; emptied shifts 4-10)

- **User request**: `SHIFT_SPECS` (`src/data/profiles.py`) was the one piece of per-shift data that didn't live in its own `shift_NN.py` file — every other field (`SHIFT_DATE`, `INITIAL_SCHEDULE`, `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW`, etc.) already did, read centrally via `gameplay/shifts/loader.py::load_shift_config()`. The developer asked for this dispersion fixed so all shift-specific data lives in its own file.
- **`ShiftSpec`/`SHIFT_SPECS` dissolved.** `start_hour`, `duration_hours`, `peak_demand_mw` became new `START_HOUR`/`DURATION_HOURS`/`PEAK_DEMAND_MW` module constants in `shift_01.py`/`shift_02.py`/`shift_03.py` (placed after `DIFFICULTY_LABEL`, before `HANDOVER_NOTES`/`GRID_SOURCE`, matching the existing constant style), read by a 3-key extension to `load_shift_config()`'s returned dict (`getattr(mod, 'X', default)`, same pattern as every other field). `grid_size` and `has_phase1` were dropped entirely (not relocated) — repo-wide grep confirmed neither field had any real reader anywhere; `grid_size` duplicates the grid design's own bus count and `has_phase1` was fully dead.
- **Shifts 4-10 emptied to placeholder stubs, not deleted.** The developer confirmed shifts 4-10 (stale data left over from the old shared `topology.py`/`fleet.py` campaign grid, predating the Shift 1-3 move to individual Grid Designer grids) will be rebuilt later from the Shift 3 pattern. Deleting the files outright would crash the still-10-shift-long campaign scaffolding (`Grid.__init__`'s `1 <= shift_number <= 10` check, `"SHIFT N OF 10"` UI text, the 10-slot shift-select screen, campaign-end trigger) via `ImportError` on `importlib.import_module`, so `shift_04.py` through `shift_10.py` were reduced to a bare module docstring only — `load_shift_config()`'s existing `getattr(..., default)` fallback keeps them safely loadable (empty schedule, `AGC_ENABLED=False`, `PEAK_DEMAND_MW=0.0`) if hit via the F-key dev shortcuts or shift-select, without being playable. This also means Shift 10's Phase 1 planning debug path (`build_planning_model_for_shift10()`) now resolves an empty `GRID_SOURCE` and falls through to the shared-topology `Grid(10)` — acceptable since STAGE_STATUS.md already documented that path as debug-only/not reachable from normal campaign entry.
- **Callers updated**: `simulation.py` (`GridSimulation.__init__` and `run_forecast_mode()` now call `load_shift_config(shift_number)` instead of indexing `SHIFT_SPECS`), `simulation/demand.py` (`DemandModel.__init__` now takes `peak_demand_mw: float` directly instead of a whole `ShiftSpec` object — it was the only field ever read off `spec`), `gameplay/phase1.py::build_planning_model()`, `main.py` (`build_briefing_lines`/`build_debrief_lines` now take `shift_number: int` and call `load_shift_config()` themselves; all 7 `_spec = SHIFT_SPECS.get(shift)` call sites collapsed to a direct `build_briefing_lines(shift)`/`build_debrief_lines(shift, ...)` call — no existence guard needed, `shift` is always 1-10 there and shifts 4-10 remain importable stubs). `debug_scenario.py`'s `make_debug_sim()` previously monkey-patched the module-level `SHIFT_SPECS` dict in a try/finally to inject overrides — rewritten to use `GridSimulation.__init__`'s existing `start_hour`/`duration_hours` override params directly, with `peak_demand_mw` applied post-construction onto `sim._demand._peak_demand_mw` (consistent with the file's existing style of reaching into `sim._demand` for `set_demand_override`).
- **`tests/test_simulation.py`** updated: `test_demand_model` previously read `SHIFT_SPECS[7]`/shift 7's `LD01`-family buses — switched to shift 2 (`GREY`/`OAKE` buses) since shift 7 is now an empty stub; `test_simulation_model`'s `SHIFT_SPECS[1]` lookup replaced with `load_shift_config(1)['duration_hours']`.

(2) `grep -rn "SHIFT_SPECS|ShiftSpec" src/ tests/` — zero remaining hits (two stale doc-comment references in `profiles.py`/`shift_io.py` also fixed). (3) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass. ### Session 55 (Alarm and info/tutor sound effects)

- **User request**: play a short ping whenever an INFO/TUTOR alarm is raised, loop `alarm.wav` while any WARNING/CRITICAL alarm is unacknowledged, and let SPACE silence the loop until a genuinely new WARNING/CRITICAL alarm is raised.
- **No audio infrastructure existed** before this session — no `pygame.mixer` usage anywhere in the codebase. Only `src/assets/sounds/alarm.wav` exists on disk; the developer confirmed building only for that file plus a new `ping.wav` (not yet supplied) rather than CLAUDE.md's aspirational 5-file severity-split plan — `ping.wav`'s absence is handled as a soft no-op (existence-check pattern matching `FONT_PATH_MONO_REGULAR` in `renderer.py`), not a crash.
- **New `src/display/sound.py`** (`SoundManager`): loads both sounds via `resource_path()`; tracks the highest `Alarm.alarm_id` seen to detect newly-raised alarms each frame with no separate event system (alarm_id is monotonically increasing from `simulation.py`'s `_raise_alarm`). `update(active_alarms)` — called once per frame — plays the ping one-shot for new INFO/TUTOR alarms, clears any active silence on a new WARNING/CRITICAL alarm, and drives a **reserved mixer channel** (`pygame.mixer.set_reserved(1)` + `Channel(0)`) for the alarm loop so `Sound.play()`'s auto-allocation (used by the ping) can never steal it. Loop-active condition is a simple existence check — `any(unacknowledged WARNING/CRITICAL)` — no severity ranking; matches the developer's explicit choice over "most recent alarm decides."
- **SPACE decoupled from Pause**: SPACE was previously bound together with `P` in one branch (toggle pause). The developer confirmed SPACE should silence-only and not affect pause at all, so the combined `(K_p, K_SPACE)` branch was split into two independent branches in both places it existed (`main.py`'s `GameState.PLAYING` and `GameState.DESIGNER_TEST` keydown blocks) — `P` still toggles pause unchanged; `K_SPACE` now calls `renderer.on_silence_alarm()` (thin wrapper over `SoundManager.silence()`).
- **Hook point**: `SoundManager` is owned by `Renderer` (constructed in `__init__`) and driven from the top of `Renderer.tick()` (`if state is not None: self._sound.update(state.active_alarms)`) — this one hook covers both PLAYING and DESIGNER_TEST since both call `renderer.tick(dt, state=state, ...)` every frame, avoiding duplicating the *detection* half in `main.py` (only the SPACE key branch itself needed duplicating, matching the existing Pause/Ack duplication pattern in that file).
- **`pygame.mixer.init()`** added in `main.py`'s `main()`, alongside the existing `pygame.init()`/`pygame.freetype.init()`.
- **New constants** (`src/simulation/constants.py`, `# SOUND` section next to `# ALARM DISPLAY`): `SOUND_PATH_ALARM`, `SOUND_PATH_PING`, `SOUND_VOLUME_ALARM` (0.6), `SOUND_VOLUME_PING` (0.5).
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m py_compile` on all 4 edited/created files — clean. (2) Headless smoke test (`SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy`) driving `SoundManager` directly through a full state sequence: confirmed `alarm.wav` loads and `ping.wav` correctly reports missing (no crash); a new TUTOR alarm doesn't start the loop; a new WARNING alarm starts it; `silence()` stops it; a subsequent new CRITICAL alarm breaks through silence and resumes the loop; acknowledging every outstanding alarm stops the loop and clears silence. (3) Confirmed no remaining `(K_p, K_SPACE)` collisions anywhere in `main.py` — exactly one `K_p` and one `K_SPACE` branch per game-state block, both present in PLAYING and DESIGNER_TEST. (4) Confirmed `_rend.tick(dt, state=state, ...)` (DESIGNER_TEST) also passes `state` through, so the sound hook fires there too, not just PLAYING.
- **Not done this session**: `ping.wav` itself was not supplied — the developer needs to add `src/assets/sounds/ping.wav` before the info/tutor ping will audibly play (code path is fully wired and fails soft in the meantime). No live audio-device playtest was performed (headless dummy driver only) since this session's environment can't launch real audio output — matches the precedent noted in prior sessions for fullscreen/display testing.
- Edited: `src/main.py` (`pygame.mixer.init()`; split `(K_p, K_SPACE)` branch into two, both game-state blocks), `src/display/renderer.py` (`SoundManager` import/construction, `tick()` hook, `on_silence_alarm()`), `src/simulation/constants.py` (new `# SOUND` section).
- Created: `src/display/sound.py` (`SoundManager`).

### Session 54 (Shift 2 rewired onto its new Grid Designer grid; new TUTOR alarm priority)

- **User request**: rewire Shift 2 onto a newly-designed Grid Designer grid (`src/assets/designer_grids/shift2.json`), the same way Shift 1 was rewired in commit `df5c583` (never logged here — see the retroactive note below). `shift_02.py` still described and numerically depended on the old shared `topology.py`/`fleet.py` slice (`LD01`/`LD02` buses, `RVSD-1/2/3` + `DUND-1/2` units), none of which exist in the new grid (`RIVE`/`ASHC`/`GREY`/`OAKE` buses, `RIVE-1` COAL + `ASHC-1` HYDRO units), so Shift 2 was a dangling, unplayable mismatch before this session.
- **Concept change**: the old Shift 2 was an "AGC regulation band" tutorial (`DUND-1` hydro as the sole AGC unit, `RVSD-1` coal manually ramped to relieve it). The developer confirmed AGC must be off for Shift 2 (matching Shift 1, which already has `AGC_ENABLED = False`), ruling out that framing entirely. The new concept is **manual two-unit dispatch**: `RIVE-1` (COAL, 300MW, 105MW min, 3%/min ramp) and `ASHC-1` (HYDRO, 250MW, 25MW min, fast ramp) are both online at handover with AGC off, and the player must manually raise both units as demand rises 273→400MW across the 10:00-14:00 shift, since nothing does it automatically. Checked the ramp-rate math directly: RIVE-1's 3%/min rate can cover its full 105-300MW range in ~22 sim-minutes, well inside the shift's 240 sim-minute window — ramp rate is not a binding constraint here (unlike Shift 3's much tighter nuclear droop scenario), so the lesson is the simpler two-unit-balancing step up from Shift 1's single-unit tutorial, ahead of Shift 3+'s larger AGC-assisted fleets. The old "RVSD-2 relay maintenance" COALCOM beat was dropped — the new grid only has 2 units total, no spare to hold on maintenance.
- **`shift_02.py` rewritten** (`GRID_SOURCE = 'shift2'`, same pattern `shift_01.py`/`shift_10.py` established): new docstring/`HANDOVER_NOTES` describing the two-unit manual dispatch narrative; `MAINTENANCE_UNITS = set()`; `AGC_ENABLED = False`; `INITIAL_SCHEDULE = {'RIVE-1': 120.0, 'ASHC-1': 160.0}` (280MW combined, ~7MW headroom over the 273MW hour-10 load). `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW` rebuilt as a full 24h table (`GREY`/`OAKE`, not `LD01`/`LD02` — `get_profile_value()` interpolates across the whole day even though the shift only plays 10:00-14:00, so a partial table crashes with `KeyError: 24.0`, caught during verification) shaped like the old shift's full-day curve, rescaled 400/315 to the new grid's 400MW combined peak, with the 10:00-14:00 window forced to exact anchor values (273→311→340→371→400MW) matching the new scripted events' thresholds.
- **`SCRIPTED_EVENTS` rewritten for both Shift 1 and Shift 2** (Shift 1's was previously empty; the developer asked to add matching tutorial events while doing Shift 2's). Both use the new `'TUTOR'` priority (see below). Shift 2 gets 4 events (T+0/30/60/120) using the declarative `UNIT_OUTPUT_MW_SUM` condition (`shift_10.py`'s `_CCGT_BELOW_1000MW` precedent — confirmed the `'targets'` key is plural and sums across arbitrary unit labels) checking combined `RIVE-1`+`ASHC-1` output against the load curve, replacing the old callable conditions (`_dund1_near_saturation`/`_reg_band_narrow`) which called `fleet.agc_regulation_state()` — meaningless with AGC off. Shift 1 gets 3 simpler events (T+0/60/120): the T+0 event is unconditional, the T+60 event keys off `FREQUENCY_HZ` directly (rather than a raw MW comparison) since this is the player's first-ever exposure to a frequency deviation from under-generation, and T+120 falls back to a direct `UNIT_OUTPUT_MW` check (single unit, no `_SUM` needed).
- **New `'TUTOR'` alarm priority** (green, for tutorial-style scripted-event messages, distinct from real grid-fault alarms): confirmed priority was a plain string with no central enum — two of the five previously-used values (`'MAINTENANCE'`, `'ALARM'`, from `shift_03.py`) already weren't wired to a colour and silently fell back to dim grey, so this was a small additive change. Added `COL_ALARM_TUTOR = (0, 170, 0)` (`palette.py`, reuses the existing "Green" value, no new RGB introduced — strict 16-colour constraint intact); wired into `_ALARM_DOT_COL` (`panels.py`); added to `_PRIORITY_CYCLE` (`shift_builder.py`, so it's selectable in the event editor); extended `_expire_alarms()`'s auto-expiry check (`simulation.py`) from `priority == 'INFO'` to `priority in ('INFO', 'TUTOR')` so acknowledged tutorial alarms also clear after 60 sim-minutes. No changes needed to acknowledgment, crisis/border-flash, alarm sorting, or blink suppression — all either priority-agnostic or already scoped to `('CRITICAL', 'WARNING')` only.
- **`SHIFT_SPECS[2]`** (`src/data/profiles.py`): `peak_demand_mw` 315.0 → 400.0, matching `shift2.json`'s authored per-bus `peak_load_mw` ceilings (developer confirmed targeting the grid file's own ceiling rather than preserving the old total). `grid_size` (4 buses) unchanged.
- **Retroactive note**: commit `df5c583` (Shift 1's own rewiring onto `shift1.json`, plus a Grid Designer label-anchor bug fix, a `DESIGNER_TEST` keyboard fix, and a `designer_io.py` naming overhaul) was never logged in this file when it landed — flagged here since this session builds directly on that work.
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m py_compile` on all edited files — clean. (2) Headless smoke test (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`): `_make_sim_and_renderer(display_surf, shift, 'standard')` for both shift 1 and shift 2 builds a working `DesignerGrid`, initial dispatch matches `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` exactly (`ASHC-1: 30.0` for shift 1; `RIVE-1: 120.0`/`ASHC-1: 160.0` for shift 2), both tick 200+ steps with no exception. (3) Confirmed `AGC_ENABLED = False` takes effect for both shifts via `simulation.constants.AGC_ENABLED` after `_make_sim_and_renderer()`. (4) Drove both shifts through their full event timelines: all 3 (shift 1) / 4 (shift 2) `TUTOR` events fire at their exact trigger times when dispatch is left untouched; re-ran shift 2 with both units manually ramped to 200MW each at T+20 and confirmed the T+60/T+120 conditional "generation lagging" warnings correctly do **not** fire once combined output clears their thresholds. (5) Confirmed `_ALARM_DOT_COL['TUTOR']` resolves to the real green `COL_ALARM_TUTOR`, not the dim-grey fallback. (6) Confirmed an acknowledged `TUTOR` alarm auto-expires after 60 sim-minutes, matching `INFO`. (7) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass, unchanged.
