- **Not done this session (out of scope, per developer confirmation)**: `shift2.json` itself was not modified — it's the developer's own in-progress Grid Designer work, still being hand-edited; if it changes further, `shift_02.py`'s numeric tables will need re-syncing again (same manual-sync caveat Shift 1 already has). `src/display/palette.py`'s separate uncommitted coal-colour tweak (`COL_UNIT_COAL`) present at session start was left untouched, unrelated to this work.

### Session 53 (Phase 1 Planning Screen — per-hour table dash/value bug, layout shift)

- **Bug reported**: player ran the auto-scheduler and saw CCGT generation on the stacked plot, but the table showed every CCGT cell as a blank dash for every hour, hiding real non-zero scheduled MW.
- **Root cause found** (`src/display/planning_panels.py`, `_draw_table()`'s per-unit row loop): a single `online = model.is_online(unit.label, model.hours[0])` — hour `00:00` only — was reused to decide the dash-vs-value display and colour for *every* hour cell in the row, not just the `[ON]/[OFF]` label. This bug has existed since Session 49 but was invisible until now: before Session 50's auto-scheduler, a unit's online state was always flat across all 24 hours (the default seed or a manual whole-row `toggle_online()`), so hour 0's state always matched every other hour. The auto-scheduler is the first thing that produces genuinely time-varying per-hour online states within one unit's row (e.g. CCGT off at `00:00`, on from `06:00` onward), exposing the dormant bug. Confirmed via direct render: `ASHG-1` is offline at hour 0 and online with 400 MW from hour 6 onward in the model, but the table rendered `-` across its entire row regardless. The stacked plot was never affected — it already reads `stacked_by_tech(h)`, which correctly checks `is_online` per hour.
- **Fix**: each hour cell now computes `hour_online = model.is_online(unit.label, h)` inside the per-hour loop and uses that (not the row-level hour-0 snapshot) for both the dash-vs-value text and its colour. The `[ON]/[OFF]` label itself still reflects hour 0's state, unchanged — consistent with `toggle_online()`'s own whole-row semantics.
- **Layout**: `PLANNING_TOP_MARGIN` (`src/simulation/constants.py`) increased 24→56 (+32px) per the developer's request for more breathing room between the title and the plot. Both the plot and the table derive their vertical position from this one constant (`plot_top = PLANNING_TOP_MARGIN`, `table_top = plot_top + PLANNING_PLOT_H + 16`), so both shift down together with a single value change; confirmed ~222px of unused space existed below the summary rows before the footer, so no clipping risk.
- **Verified**: (1) Rebuilt Shift 10's model, ran `auto_schedule()`, rendered a frame, confirmed `ASHG-1`'s row shows `-` for hours 00-05 and real values (`400`, etc.) for hours 06-23, matching the model exactly — same confirmed for BARR-1/2/3 and SLFD-1/2/3. (2) Confirmed always-online units (e.g. `HART-1`, nuclear baseload) and always-offline units (e.g. `RVSD-3`) still render correctly with no regression. (3) Visual check confirmed the plot/table now sit noticeably lower under the title. (4) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass (display-only fix, no model/simulation logic touched).
- Edited: `src/display/planning_panels.py` (`_draw_table()` per-cell online check), `src/simulation/constants.py` (`PLANNING_TOP_MARGIN` 24→56).

### Session 52 (Phase 1 Planning Screen — auto-scheduler hour-0 boundary fix, zero-fill shortcut)

- **Misunderstanding corrected**: Session 50's auto-scheduler treated "H0" as the table's displayed `00:00` column, hard-locking every non-maintenance dispatchable unit to `PLANNING_HOUR0_FRAC_<TYPE> * rated_mw` (default: full output) as a fixed, non-schedulable seed, then running the real commitment algorithm only for `01:00`-`23:00`. The developer clarified this was a misreading: in market/scheduling convention the displayed `00:00` column is the *first* hour of the day, not "hour 0" — what they'd actually asked for was that the *previous day's final hour* (H24 of D-1, which doesn't exist in the data) be assumed at max output, purely as a **calculation-only boundary/lookback state** feeding `00:00`'s own commitment and ramp decision. `00:00` itself was never meant to be locked.
- **Fix** (`src/gameplay/phase1.py`): `auto_schedule()` restructured from a "hard-set hour 0, then loop hours 1-23" split into a single unified loop over all 24 hours. Before the loop, three per-unit lookback dicts (`prev_online0`, `prev_mw0`, and a far-in-the-past `last_change_hour` seed) represent the synthetic D-1 boundary state — read only when computing `00:00`'s own decision (`h == h0`), never written to `self.schedule`/`self.online`, and never displayed. Every hour including `00:00` is now a fully computed output of the same ramp/min-up/min-down logic.
- **Bug caught during verification and fixed in the same pass**: the first version of the fix left `last_change_hour` unset at `00:00` whenever `00:00`'s own decision happened to match the D-1 lookback's assumed state (the common case) — this made `01:00` see "hours_in_state" as enormous (inherited from the far-past seed), incorrectly treating the unit as having already satisfied its full min-up/min-down window by `01:00`, defeating minimum-up-time within the first real hour. Concretely: `RVSD-2` (COAL, min_up=6h) went online at `00:00` then flipped straight back offline at `01:00` — a 1-hour "on" run against a 6-hour minimum. Fixed by always resetting `last_change_hour[label] = h0` when processing `00:00`, regardless of whether its decision changed from the lookback — the lookback only exists to free up `00:00`'s *own* choice, it must never propagate as "been in this state indefinitely" into `01:00` onward. Re-verified zero violations after the fix.
- **Constants renamed** (`src/simulation/constants.py`, `src/gameplay/phase1.py`): `PLANNING_HOUR0_FRAC_<TYPE>` → `PLANNING_PREV_DAY_FRAC_<TYPE>` (values unchanged, still default `1.0`/max output per technology) and `_HOUR0_FRAC` → `_PREV_DAY_H24_FRAC`, to name what they actually represent post-fix.
- **Zero-fill shortcut**: new `PLANNING_KEY_ZERO = pygame.K_BACKSPACE` (`constants.py`). `PlanningModel` gained `fill_cell_zero(label, hour)`/`fill_row_zero(label)` (thin wrappers over the existing `set_cell`/`fill_row` with `0.0`, matching `fill_cell_min`/`fill_cell_max`'s exact pattern — MW only, online state untouched). `PlanningScreen.on_key()` (`src/display/planning.py`) routes Backspace to the single-cell version and Shift+Backspace to the whole-row version, alongside the existing TECH MIN/MAX branches. Footer hint (`planning_panels.py`) updated with `[BACKSPACE] zero  [Shift+BACKSPACE] zero row`.
- **Verified**: (1) Re-ran the ramp-limit and min-up/min-down violation checks from Session 50 against the restructured `auto_schedule()` — zero violations across all 24 hours, now including `00:00` itself as a checked/algorithm-derived hour (previously exempt since it was hard-set). (2) Confirmed `00:00`'s values are no longer uniformly `rated_mw` for every dispatchable unit — e.g. Shift 10's real model now shows RVSD-1 at 300 MW, RVSD-2 at 160 MW at `00:00` (previously both would have been forced to 300). (3) `out_of_tolerance_hours()` on Shift 10's real model is now **empty for all 24 hours** (previously hours 0-4 were always flagged due to the old artificial over-generation spike) — total generation now tracks load within ±25-250 MW across the entire day instead of spiking ~4000 MW over load at `00:00`. (4) Zero-fill: `fill_cell_zero`/`fill_row_zero` checked directly against the model (MW zeroed, online state untouched); headless `PlanningScreen` keyboard test confirmed Backspace zeroes the selected cell, Shift+Backspace zeroes the whole row, and Backspace during an active cell-edit still performs the pre-existing text-buffer-backspace behavior (routed through `_handle_edit_key`, unaffected by the new top-level binding). (5) Rendered an actual frame to PNG — visually confirmed the stacked plot now tracks the load line smoothly from `00:00` with no start-of-day spike, and the footer shows the new shortcut. (6) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass.
- Edited: `src/gameplay/phase1.py` (`auto_schedule()` restructured to a single 24h loop with D-1 lookback seed and the `last_change_hour` reset fix; renamed `_HOUR0_FRAC`→`_PREV_DAY_H24_FRAC`; new `fill_cell_zero()`/`fill_row_zero()`), `src/simulation/constants.py` (renamed `PLANNING_HOUR0_FRAC_*`→`PLANNING_PREV_DAY_FRAC_*`; new `PLANNING_KEY_ZERO`), `src/display/planning.py` (new Backspace key branch), `src/display/planning_panels.py` (footer hint text).

### Session 51 (Phase 1 Planning Screen — visible per-unit WIND/SOLAR rows, solar display fix)

- **Bug reported**: player saw no solar generation anywhere on Shift 10's planning screen. Investigation found the underlying forecast data was correct all along — `RenewablesModel.forecast_by_hour()` already returns a proper per-unit forecast for every WIND and SOLAR unit (Shift 10's `shift10.json` grid has 3 SOLAR units: `DUNH-1`, `BARD-1`, `WNBR-1`), and solar MW was already being counted into `total_gen()`/the DIFF row. The actual bug was purely display/labeling: `build_planning_model()` immediately collapsed every renewable unit's forecast into one flat `wind_forecast: {hour: mw}` dict, discarding the WIND/SOLAR distinction, and `PlanningModel.stacked_by_tech()` lumped the combined total under a single `'WIND'` key — so solar's contribution was real but invisible as its own line and mislabeled.
- **Fix + new feature** (`src/gameplay/phase1.py`): `PlanningModel.wind_forecast` (flat dict) replaced with `renewable_specs: list[GenerationUnit]` (the WIND/SOLAR units, parallel to `unit_specs`) and `renewable_forecast: dict[str, dict[float, float]]` (per-unit, `{label: {hour: mw}}` — exactly what `RenewablesModel.forecast_by_hour()` already returns, no transformation needed). `stacked_by_tech()` now sums each renewable unit's forecast into its own correct `unit.unit_type` bucket instead of a hardcoded `'WIND'` key — this is the actual bug fix. New `renewable_total(hour, unit_type=None)` sums across `renewable_specs`, optionally filtered to `'WIND'` or `'SOLAR'`, used by the summary rows and by `auto_schedule()`'s `net_load` calculation (same total as before, just correctly sourced).
- **Table now shows every WIND/SOLAR unit as its own locked, informational row** (`src/display/planning_panels.py`), per the developer's explicit request rather than just fixing the aggregate bug silently: two new read-only group sections ("WIND (forecast, locked)" / "SOL (forecast, locked)") are drawn at the top of the table, above the editable NUCLEAR/COAL/CCGT/HYDRO/HYDRO_ROR/HYDRO_PUMP groups, each listing its units with one dimmed (`COL_TEXT_DIM`), right-aligned MW value per hour column read from `renewable_forecast` — no `[ON]/[OFF]` toggle, no cell editing. Critically, these rows' labels are never appended to `screen._row_index` (so Up/Down cursor navigation skips them entirely, landing only on dispatchable rows exactly as before) and no hit-rects are registered for them (clicking does nothing). The summary rows split the old single `'WIND FCST'` line into separate `WIND FCST`/`SOLAR FCST` rows, both now correctly showing forecast dips to 0 at night (solar) vs. the wind profile's own shape.
- **Verified**: (1) `renewable_specs` for Shift 10 correctly lists 2 WIND units (`KELM-1`, `KELD-1`) and 3 SOLAR units (`DUNH-1`, `BARD-1`, `WNBR-1`); `renewable_total(0.0, 'SOLAR')` is `0.0` and `renewable_total(12.0, 'SOLAR')` is `1128.0`, confirming solar now forecasts correctly through the model. (2) `stacked_by_tech(12.0)` now has separate non-zero `'WIND'` and `'SOLAR'` keys (previously only `'WIND'` existed and included solar's MW mislabeled). (3) Combined renewable total at every hour matches exactly what the old flat-sum approach would have produced — confirms `total_gen()`/DIFF/REG BAND math is unchanged, this was a pure display/attribution fix, not a totals change. (4) Re-ran Session 50's `auto_schedule()` ramp-limit and min-up/min-down verification — identical zero-violation result, confirming the `net_load` calculation change is a no-op numerically. (5) Headless `PlanningScreen` smoke test (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`): `tick()` renders with no exception; confirmed none of the 5 renewable unit labels appear in `screen._row_index`; confirmed no hit-rects reference any renewable unit label; confirmed cursor selection starts on the first dispatchable row (`HART-1`), never a renewable row. (6) Rendered an actual frame to PNG and visually inspected it — WIND/SOL group headers and per-unit forecast rows render correctly (solar column values ramp 0→400→0 across the day), the stacked plot's legend and bars now show a distinct solar-coloured segment separate from wind, and the summary rows correctly show WIND FCST/SOLAR FCST as two separate lines. (7) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass.
- Edited: `src/gameplay/phase1.py` (`PlanningModel.renewable_specs`/`renewable_forecast` replacing `wind_forecast`, `stacked_by_tech()` fix, new `renewable_total()`, `auto_schedule()`'s `net_load` calc, `build_planning_model()`'s forecast-building block), `src/display/planning_panels.py` (`_draw_table()` new read-only WIND/SOLAR group sections, `_draw_summary_rows()` split WIND FCST/SOLAR FCST rows).
- **Not done this session**: no changes needed to `src/display/planning.py` (event handling already skips non-`_row_index` rows correctly by construction) or `constants.py` (no new constants required, reused existing `COL_TEXT_DIM`); wiring this pattern into any shift other than 10 (Phase 1 remains Shift-10-only).

### Session 50 (Phase 1 Planning Screen — F10 crash fix, load-adequacy gate, auto-scheduler)

- **Bug reported**: confirming a Phase 1 plan with F10 crashed every time (`AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'tick'` at `main.py:762`). Root cause: pressing F10 routes through `PlanningScreen._confirm_plan()` → `on_plan_complete(model)` → `main.py`'s `_on_plan_complete` closure, which sets `_planning_screen = None` and `game_state = GameState.PLAYING` **inside the same frame's event-processing loop**. After that loop finished, execution fell through unconditionally to `_planning_screen.tick(dt, display_surf)` with no re-check that the screen still existed. Fixed with a one-line guard (`if _planning_screen is not None: _planning_screen.tick(...)`), matching the existing convention elsewhere in `main.py` that state transitions take effect next frame and the current frame's post-transition draw is skipped.
- **±10% load-adequacy gate** (`src/gameplay/phase1.py`, `src/display/planning.py`): new `PlanningModel.out_of_tolerance_hours(frac=PLANNING_LOAD_TOLERANCE_FRAC)` (new constant, default `0.10`) returns any hour where scheduled generation deviates from the load forecast by more than the tolerance. `PlanningScreen._confirm_plan()` now checks this before invoking `on_plan_complete` — if any hour is out of tolerance, F10 is refused and a status message names the first two offending hours (+ a count of any more) instead of confirming. Reuses the existing status-message mechanism (`_set_status`); no new UI chrome. The existing DIFF summary row's 2%/5% colour-coding is unchanged and still serves as the always-visible detail view underneath this pass/fail gate.
- **Auto-scheduler** (`src/gameplay/phase1.py`, Ctrl+A): optional player shortcut — never runs automatically, and the player can freely hand-edit any cell before or after running it. `PlanningModel.auto_schedule()` is a numpy-free heuristic day-ahead unit commitment (no MILP/LP solver dependency, consistent with `DOMAIN_GLOSSARY.md`'s explicit "no merit order / no unit commitment optimisation" disclaimer for the core simulation — this stays a planning-layer-only tool). Hour 0 is a fixed seed per developer decision: every non-maintenance dispatchable unit is forced ONLINE at `PLANNING_HOUR0_FRAC_<TYPE> * rated_mw` (new per-technology constants, all default `1.0`/max power, tunable independently later). Hours 1-23 are filled in a single forward pass, walking technology groups in a fixed merit order within each hour — **HYDRO_ROR → NUCLEAR → COAL → CCGT → HYDRO → HYDRO_PUMP** (wind/solar are forecast-driven and excluded, per the existing `_RENEWABLE_TYPES` split) — tracking a running "already covered" MW total per hour so each group only fills what the previous ones didn't. NUCLEAR/COAL/CCGT respect new per-technology `min_up_time_h`/`min_down_time_h` (never toggled within their commitment window); HYDRO/HYDRO_ROR/HYDRO_PUMP have `0.0`/`0.0` (free to toggle every hour, matching their real near-instant ramp/cold-start). All output is additionally ramp-limited hour to hour using each unit's existing `ramp_pct_per_min`.
- **New data**: `GenerationUnit` (`src/data/fleet.py`) and `DesignerUnit` (`src/data/designer_io.py`) both gained `min_up_time_h`/`min_down_time_h` (hours) — confirmed via full-codebase search that neither the real-time simulation (`simulation/units.py`) nor any prior session ever implemented this despite `GRID_SIMULATION_MECHANICS.md` §6.3 documenting it as intended design; it remains unenforced by `UnitModel.start()`/`stop()` (no real-time cooldown) and is deliberately a planning-layer-only constraint, keeping Rule 6 intact. All 47 `UNITS` entries in `fleet.py` updated with per-technology values (NUCLEAR 24h/24h, COAL 6h/8h, CCGT 2h/2h, all HYDRO variants/WIND/SOLAR 0h/0h); `UNIT_DEFAULTS` (`designer_io.py`) extended with matching per-type values for the Grid Designer's unit-placement tool. `DesignerUnit` itself defaults both fields to `1.0`/`1.0` as a neutral fallback so older saved grids (e.g. `Alpha.json`, and the currently-uncommitted-in-progress `shift10.json`) that predate this field still load without modification — confirmed via direct load test. All new constants added to `simulation/constants.py` per Rule 1 (`MIN_UP_HOURS_<TYPE>`/`MIN_DOWN_HOURS_<TYPE>`/`PLANNING_HOUR0_FRAC_<TYPE>`), mirroring the existing `TECH_MIN_FRAC_<TYPE>` block style.
- **`PlanningModel` gained `maintenance_units: frozenset[str]`** (populated from the shift config in `build_planning_model()`), needed by `auto_schedule()` to force maintenance units OFFLINE at hour 0 regardless of the technology's hour-0 fraction — previously this info was only passed transiently into `_default_init_schedule()` and not retained on the model.
- **Known limitation (by design, matches the approved plan)**: this is a heuristic, not a true optimizer — on the currently-uncommitted, visibly stale `shift10.json` fixture (mismatched fuel-type labels vs. real station names, a pre-existing separate issue untouched this session — see below), the auto-scheduler correctly fills every hour except hour 0 (intentionally over-generated by the fixed hour-0 seed) and a handful of ramp-down hours right after, plus a genuine ~600 MW shortfall at the evening peak (17:00-20:00) coming from that fixture's tight real capacity margin, not an algorithm defect — confirmed by checking total rated capacity against forecast peak. Verified with a direct script that ramp limits and min-up/min-down windows both hold with zero violations across all 47/30/21-unit fleets tested.
- **Pre-existing, unrelated issue noticed but not touched**: `src/assets/designer_grids/shift10.json` (the file `shift_10.py`'s `GRID_SOURCE = 'shift10'` actually loads, separate from the cleaned-up `Alpha.json` from Session 47) is currently sitting as a large uncommitted working-tree diff with scrambled fuel-type/station-name pairings (e.g. `THNF`/`WRNG`/`BARR` tagged `HYDRO`/`CCGT` instead of their real coal/CCGT/pumped-storage roles, several stations tagged `WIND`/`SOLAR`) — recognized as the player's own in-progress Grid Designer work already flagged by git status at session start, left untouched per the safety protocol around uncommitted changes. The auto-scheduler was verified against it as-is (see above) since it's what Shift 10 currently loads, but its output only reflects that fixture's current (unfinished) fuel assignments, not real Shift 10 station roles.
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m pytest tests/` — 15/15 pass, unchanged. (2) Direct repro of the original crash path (headless, `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`): confirmed the unguarded code path would call `.tick()` on `None` and that the fix's guard correctly skips it instead, with `on_plan_complete` still firing exactly once. (3) Confirm-gate: an out-of-tolerance schedule correctly blocks F10 (status message set, `on_plan_complete` not called); matching generation to load forecast for every hour then correctly allows confirmation. (4) Auto-scheduler run against Shift 10's real (`shift10.json`-backed) model: zero ramp-limit violations and zero min-up/min-down violations across all dispatchable units, checked programmatically hour-by-hour. (5) Backward compatibility: loading `Alpha.json` (predates the new fields) via `load_designer_grid_named` and through the full `DesignerGrid` construction path both succeed, with `min_up_time_h`/`min_down_time_h` correctly falling back to `1.0`/`1.0`. (6) Shifts 1/5/9 (plain `Grid`, non-Designer path) still build correctly with the new `GenerationUnit` fields populated from `fleet.py`'s real per-technology values. (7) All edited files pass `python -m py_compile`.
- Edited: `src/main.py` (crash-guard fix), `src/simulation/constants.py` (`PLANNING_LOAD_TOLERANCE_FRAC`, `MIN_UP_HOURS_*`/`MIN_DOWN_HOURS_*`, `PLANNING_HOUR0_FRAC_*`, `PLANNING_KEY_AUTO`), `src/gameplay/phase1.py` (`out_of_tolerance_hours()`, `auto_schedule()`, `PlanningModel.maintenance_units` field), `src/display/planning.py` (confirm-gate check, Ctrl+A keybinding), `src/display/planning_panels.py` (footer hint), `src/data/fleet.py` (`GenerationUnit` new fields, all 47 units), `src/data/designer_io.py` (`DesignerUnit` new fields, `UNIT_DEFAULTS` per-type additions), `src/simulation/designer_grid.py` (`_to_unit()` passthrough), `src/display/designer.py` (unit-placement tool passes `UNIT_DEFAULTS`' new fields through to newly placed `DesignerUnit`s).
- **Not done this session (deferred)**: wiring the auto-scheduler or load-adequacy gate into any shift other than 10 (Phase 1 remains Shift-10-only, per existing scope); cleaning up `shift10.json`'s scrambled fuel-type data (flagged above as the player's own in-progress uncommitted work, not this session's concern); a real non-headless visual playtest of the new footer hint / auto-scheduled plot.

### Session 48 (Shift Builder — campaign-shift dev tool: open/edit shift_01.py..shift_10.py in place)

- **User request**: the second half of Session 47's original ask — let the Shift Builder open and fine-tune any existing campaign shift, not just author new Continuous-mode JSON shifts, framed explicitly as a developer-facing tool (not a player feature).
- **Data-model decision**: did not extend `ShiftDefinition` into a full campaign-shift representation with a dataclass-to-source dump — that would risk clobbering `shift_NN.py`'s hand-written module docstrings and `HANDOVER_NOTES` prose on save. Instead, campaign shifts are read into the same in-memory `ShiftDefinition` shape the builder already edits (`data.shift_io.load_campaign_shift_for_editing(shift_number)`, wrapping `gameplay.shifts.loader.load_shift_config()` plus a direct `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` read), and written back via `data.shift_io.save_campaign_shift_fields(shift_number, shift_def, edited_fields)` — a targeted splice that parses the file with `ast`, locates the exact line span of each edited constant (`INITIAL_SCHEDULE`, `MAINTENANCE_UNITS`, `MAINTENANCE_LINES`, `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW`, `AGC_ENABLED`, `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` — the `CAMPAIGN_EDITABLE_FIELDS` set), and replaces only that span. `ShiftBuilder` now tracks a per-session `_edited_fields` set (alongside its existing `_dirty` bool) so an untouched tab's original formatting/comments are never touched on save.
- **Line-ending bug caught during testing**: the first version of the splice writer used `Path.read_text()`/`write_text()`, which perform Python's universal-newline translation — on Windows this silently rewrote every LF in the file to CRLF on write, turning a one-constant edit into a whole-file diff. Fixed by reading/writing through `open(..., newline='')` (preserving the file's exact original line endings) and detecting the file's actual EOL convention from its own content before splicing in new text. Verified the fix produces a true minimal diff (confirmed via `git diff` after a live round-trip through the real `ShiftBuilder` UI: toggling `AGC_ENABLED` for shift 1 changed exactly one line).
- **Known, accepted formatting tradeoff**: only the edited constant's own inline comments (e.g. a per-unit `# Hartwell Nuclear 1 — rated baseload` comment next to a dict entry) are lost when that specific constant is re-spliced — the pretty-printer re-flows the value with one dict/list entry per line rather than preserving the original compact hand-formatted grid layout. Confirmed via testing on `shift_10.py`'s `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW`/`SCRIPTED_EVENTS` (both hand-formatted with per-value or per-event comments) that the module docstring, all other constants, and the file's overall structure are otherwise untouched — this matches the plan's accepted tradeoff exactly.
- **UI additions** (`src/display/shift_builder.py`, `src/display/shift_builder_panels.py`): new `'campaign_browser'` mode (Ctrl+Shift+O — distinct from the existing Ctrl+O JSON-load browser), listing `data.shift_io.list_campaign_shift_numbers()` (scans `gameplay/shifts/shift_*.py`, currently `[1..10]`). Selecting a shift calls `load_campaign_shift_for_editing`, sets a new `self._campaign_shift_number` flag that controls save-target routing and read-only field rendering. Ctrl+S now routes through `_save_current()`, which calls `save_campaign_shift_fields()` when a campaign shift is loaded or falls back to the original JSON `_open_save_dialog()` otherwise — the header text always shows which target Ctrl+S will hit ("EDITING: CAMPAIGN SHIFT 10 (shift_10.py)" vs a JSON filename). The GRID tab and META tab's narrative fields (shift date, difficulty label, handover notes) render read-only (dimmed, with an inline hint to edit `shift_NN.py`/the Grid Designer directly) when editing a campaign shift; Ctrl+G is also blocked with a status message in this mode, since campaign topology isn't Designer-grid-backed except Shift 10's read-only `GRID_SOURCE` reference. `AGC_ENABLED` remains editable in both modes (it's a trivially safe single bool to splice). SCHEDULE and DEMAND tabs are fully editable in both modes.
- **Test-launch routing** (`src/main.py`): new `_make_campaign_shift_test(display_surf, shift_number)`, a thin wrapper around the same `_make_sim_and_renderer()` Session 47 modified — so Ctrl+T on a campaign shift exercises the exact real bootstrap path (including Shift 10's Alpha `grid_source` branch), rather than the generic `DesignerGrid`-preview path `_make_shift_test` uses for authored JSON shifts. Wired into the `SHIFT_BUILDER` game-state block via a new `ShiftBuilder.on_campaign_test_request` callback (parallel to the existing `on_test_request`), reusing the same `DESIGNER_TEST` state/pause/speed/dispatch-input plumbing already shared by the Designer and JSON-shift test sessions.
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m pytest tests/test_simulation.py` — 9/9 pass (nothing in this session touches simulation code). (2) All 10 campaign shifts load via `load_campaign_shift_for_editing` and round-trip through a save with zero edited fields with no file changes (`git status` clean after). (3) A live, full-UI-path edit (open shift 10 via the campaign browser, toggle AGC via keypress, Ctrl+S) produces exactly a one-line diff. (4) Attempting to edit a field with no matching top-level constant in the target file (e.g. `MAINTENANCE_LINES` on shift 1, which has never declared that constant) raises a clear `ValueError` before writing anything, rather than corrupting the file. (5) `_make_campaign_shift_test` builds a working `DesignerGrid`+57-bus grid for shift 10 and a plain `Grid`+3-bus grid for shift 1, both ticking with no exception. (6) Existing JSON-shift flow (Ctrl+O load, Ctrl+G grid browser, Ctrl+S save-dialog) confirmed unaffected by loading/testing `shift1_fixture.json` through the modified `ShiftBuilder` and checking `_campaign_shift_number` stays `None` throughout.
- Edited: `src/data/shift_io.py` (`load_campaign_shift_for_editing`, `save_campaign_shift_fields`, `list_campaign_shift_numbers`, `CAMPAIGN_EDITABLE_FIELDS`, AST-splice helpers), `src/display/shift_builder.py` (campaign browser mode, save/test routing, per-field dirty tracking, read-only guards), `src/display/shift_builder_panels.py` (campaign browser rendering, read-only field styling, updated header/footer), `src/main.py` (`_make_campaign_shift_test`, `on_campaign_test_request` wiring).
- **Not done this session (deferred)**: editing narrative fields (`SHIFT_DATE`, `DIFFICULTY_LABEL`, `HANDOVER_NOTES`) via the same splice mechanism — these remain read-only/hand-edit-only, per the plan's explicit recommendation to treat this as lower priority given `HANDOVER_NOTES`' deliberately-wrapped multi-line string formatting. No automated test coverage was added for `shift_builder.py`/`shift_io.py` (matches the project's existing convention noted in Session 45 — manual/headless verification only).

### Session 47 (Shift 10 grid swap — Alpha cleanup, scenario regeneration, campaign grid-source wiring)

- **User request**: use the saved Designer grid "Alpha" as Shift 10's topology, and evolve the Shift Builder into a general dev tool that can open and fine-tune any campaign shift (not just author new Continuous-mode JSON shifts). This session covers the Alpha/Shift-10 half; the Shift Builder dev-tool evolution is scoped as a following session.
- **Problem found before starting**: `Alpha.json` (57 buses/115 lines/30 units) reused several real GRIDCOM station codes (RVSD, THNF, ASHG, WRNG, BARR, KELM, DUNH, BARD, WNCN, WNBR, DUND, KELD, AR01, AR03) but with fuel types contradicting CLAUDE.md canon (e.g. THNF tagged `HYDRO` when Thornfield is Coal; DUNH tagged `NUCLEAR` when Dunmore upper is pumped Hydro), plus 8 placeholder bus labels (`B050, B051, B054, B055, B056, B057, B058, B059`) instead of proper 4-letter place names. Confirmed with the user this was scratch/test data from experimenting in the Grid Designer, not intentional; user asked for a full cleanup rather than abandoning the idea.
- **Alpha.json cleanup** (one-off script, not a permanent repo file): renamed the 8 placeholder buses to proper place names (`TARN, CLNT, HAGN, BREN, MYRE, OTLY, STOW, ELMR`), fixing every `DesignerLine.from_bus`/`to_bus` and the one affected `DesignerUnit.bus_label` (`BARD-1/2/3`) in lockstep. Fixed `unit_type` + matching physical params (rated_mw/min_mw/ramp/inertia/cold_start/q_max/q_min/can_pump) on THNF (→COAL), WRNG (→CCGT), BARR/KELM/DUNH (→HYDRO_PUMP), BARD/DUND/KELD (→HYDRO), WNBR (→WIND), and AR03 (→HYDRO_ROR), sourcing canon values directly from `src/data/fleet.py`'s real unit entries rather than the generic `UNIT_DEFAULTS` table. Left unit counts as Alpha already had them (e.g. BARR has 1 unit, not the real fleet's 2) since Alpha is deliberately a smaller/different grid, renumbering labels contiguously per station. RVSD/ASHG/WNCN/AR01 were already correct and untouched.
- **Shift 10 scenario data regenerated** against Alpha's cleaned bus/unit set (`src/gameplay/shifts/shift_10.py`): `SUBSTATION_LOAD_MW` built by profiling each Alpha LOAD bus's `peak_load_mw` across `data.profiles.DEMAND_PROFILE_NORMALISED`, scaled by a uniform factor so the aggregate hits the campaign's signature 8,000 MW peak at hour 18:00 (lands at 8,003 MW). `INITIAL_SCHEDULE` derived per-unit from a `unit_type`-keyed dispatch-fraction rule (coal ~47% for ramp headroom, CCGT ~22.5% held in reserve, pumped/lower hydro ~10-20% as fast regulation reserve, run-of-river cascade ~65% at available flow; wind omitted, forecast-driven). `MAINTENANCE_UNITS` set to `RVSD-3` (largest coal station's last unit, mirroring the original THNF-3 planned-outage beat). Module docstring, `HANDOVER_NOTES`, and `SCRIPTED_EVENTS` rewritten to match Alpha's actual fleet (no nuclear, no solar on this grid — 14 stations: RVSD/THNF coal, ASHG/WRNG CCGT, BARR/KELM/DUNH pumped storage, BARD/DUND/KELD lower hydro, AR01/AR03 cascade, WNCN/WNBR wind). **`SCORING_HOOKS` (the L02/L03 MDBY-STHW N-1 bonus/penalty) dropped**: confirmed via grep that nothing in `loader.py`/`scoring.py`/`campaign.py`/`debrief.py` ever reads `SCORING_HOOKS` today, it referenced line labels specific to the old topology, and Alpha has no MDBY-STHW *double* circuit to test against (only a single L05 line) — the user agreed to drop it rather than guess at an equivalent; can be reintroduced once scoring logic actually consumes it. The T+330/T+390 scripted-event pair describing that same N-1 test was dropped for the same reason (no clean line analogue in Alpha); the wind-lull and evening-peak scripted events were kept and re-worded (WNCN + WNBR instead of WNCN + solar).
- **Campaign wiring** (`src/gameplay/shifts/loader.py`, `src/main.py`): `shift_10.py` gained `GRID_SOURCE: str = 'Alpha'`; `load_shift_config()` now reads `getattr(mod, 'GRID_SOURCE', None)` into the returned dict as `grid_source` (default `None` — every other shift unaffected). `main._make_sim_and_renderer()` now branches on `cfg.get('grid_source')`: when set, it loads the named grid via `data.designer_io.load_designer_grid_named()` and builds a `simulation.designer_grid.DesignerGrid` (the same Grid-compatible adapter `_make_designer_test`/`_make_shift_test` already use) instead of `Grid(shift)`, and calls `renderer.set_designer_grid(grid)` instead of `renderer.set_grid(grid)` so `GridCanvas` actually swaps its baked-in topology via `load_designer_topology(...)` — without this the simulation would run on Alpha but the canvas would still visually show the old real topology, since `GridCanvas.__init__` always builds from `topology.py`/`fleet.py` internally regardless of what's later passed to `set_grid()`. `shift_number=10` is passed to `GridSimulation` unchanged in both branches, so `SHIFT_SPECS[10]`, `_load_scripted_events(10)`, briefing/debrief text, and the `SHIFT 10 OF 10` HUD title all continue to resolve exactly as before — none of that code keys off the `grid` object itself.
- **Known cosmetic gap, documented, not fixed**: `canvas.py`'s `_HYDRAULIC_CONNECTORS` (dashed penstock overlay between pumped-storage upper/lower bus pairs, e.g. `('MDBY', 'DUND')`) is a hardcoded list of real-topology bus-label pairs built once in `GridCanvas.__init__` and is **not** recomputed by `load_designer_topology()`. Alpha's actual upper/lower bus pairs (DUNH on `WEST`, DUND on `ASHF`; KELM on `FAIR`, KELD on `DUNM`; BARR on `COAL`, BARD on `MYRE`) don't match any hardcoded pair, so Shift 10 will not show the dashed hydraulic-connector lines for its pumped-storage stations. Purely visual — the simulation's actual hydro coupling is unaffected. Fixing this properly would require `DesignerGrid` exposing its own hydraulic-pair data, which the Designer's data model doesn't have today; out of scope for this session.
- **Verified**: (1) `python -m pytest tests/test_simulation.py` — 9/9 pass, unaffected (nothing in this session touches `Grid`, `topology.py`, `fleet.py`, or `GridSimulation`'s constructor signature). (2) Headless smoke test (`SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy`): `_make_sim_and_renderer(display_surf, 10, 'standard')` builds a `DesignerGrid` with Alpha's 57 buses/30 units, `renderer._canvas._buses` matches (57, not the old 41), sim ticks 200 steps with no exception. (3) Shifts 1/3/5/9 confirmed still build plain `Grid` instances at their original bus counts (3/10/23/36) — completely unaffected by the new branch. (4) `build_briefing_lines`/`build_debrief_lines` for shift 10 render correctly, showing "SHIFT: 10 OF 10" and the rewritten handover notes. (5) Reloaded the cleaned Alpha grid directly and asserted: no bus label matches `^B0\d\d$`, no dangling `from_bus`/`to_bus`/`bus_label` references, all units per station share one consistent `unit_type`; also ran it through `DCLoadFlow` at an arbitrary generic dispatch — solves cleanly, max line loading 96.3%, no islanding.
